## A

### [absolutely](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/absolutely) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈæbsəluːtli/` · **US:** `/ˈæbsəluːtli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/abs/absol/absolutely__gb_6.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/abs/absol/absolutely__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You're absolutely right.
  - He made it absolutely clear.
  - I'm not absolutely certain I posted it.
  - Are you absolutely sure?
  - Training is absolutely essential in this business.
  - Don't delay for longer than absolutely necessary.
  - The place was absolutely packed.
  - It was absolutely pouring with rain.

### [academic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/academic_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌækəˈdemɪk/` · **US:** `/ˌækəˈdemɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/aca/acade/academic__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/aca/acade/academic__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - high/low academic standards
  - She had a brilliant academic career.
  - one of this country's most prestigious academic institutions
  - improving the academic achievement of all students
  - The university is renowned throughout the world for its academic excellence.
  - academic research/researchers
  - We are deeply committed to safeguarding academic freedom.

### [access](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/access_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈækses/` · **US:** `/ˈækses/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/acc/acces/access__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/acc/acces/access__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - High-speed internet access has become a necessity.
  - access to something/somebody You need a password to get access to the computer system.
  - access to information/services/resources
  - access to healthcare/education
  - They are seeking increased access to markets overseas.
  - to provide/allow access to something/somebody
  - to restrict/block access to something/somebody
  - Journalists were denied access to the President.
  - Many divorced fathers only have access to their children at weekends (= they are allowed by law to see them only at weekends).

### [access](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/access_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈækses/` · **US:** `/ˈækses/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/acc/acces/access__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/acc/acces/access__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Most people use their phones to access the internet.
  - to access a file/database
  - It has never been easier to access information.
  - All employees can access real-time data.
  - You should regularly change the password you use to access your online accounts.
  - access something via/through something Their website can be accessed via the link below.

### [accommodation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/accommodation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˌkɒməˈdeɪʃn/` · **US:** `/əˌkɑːməˈdeɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/acc/accom/accommodation__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/acc/accom/accommodation__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - rented/temporary accommodation
  - Hotel accommodation is included in the price of your holiday.
  - We may have to provide alternative accommodation for you.
  - First-class accommodation is available on all flights.
  - It can be difficult to find suitable accommodation for those in need.
  - The building plans include much needed new office accommodation.
  - furnished accommodation

### [account](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/account_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈkaʊnt/` · **US:** `/əˈkaʊnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/acc/accou/account__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/acc/accou/account__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I don't have a bank account.
  - I paid the money into my savings account.
  - a joint account (= one in the name of more than one person)
  - With internet banking you can manage your account online.
  - account with somebody/something Do you have an account with us?
  - account at something He opened an account at a bank in Germany.
  - Interest will be paid monthly into the account holder's current account.
  - What's your account number please?

### [achievement](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/achievement) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈtʃiːvmənt/` · **US:** `/əˈtʃiːvmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ach/achie/achievement__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ach/achie/achievement__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the greatest scientific achievement of the decade
  - sporting/artistic/academic achievements
  - It was a remarkable achievement for such a young player.
  - an outstanding achievement
  - This work is the crowning achievement (= the greatest achievement) of her career.
  - They were proud of their children's achievements.

### [act](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/act_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ækt/` · **US:** `/ækt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/act/act__/act__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/act/act__/act__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You have committed a serious criminal act.
  - act of something an act of kindness
  - This was one of the most appalling acts of terror of recent times.
  - acts of terrorism/violence/aggression
  - They performed acts of great heroism.
  - The murder was the act of a psychopath.

### [ad](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ad_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/æd/` · **US:** `/æd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ad_/ad__g/ad__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ad_/ad__u/ad__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The TV ads were first run last year.
  - They've produced an ad for a new chocolate bar.
  - We put an ad on a classifieds website.
  - A $10 million TV ad campaign
  - an ad agency

### [addition](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/addition) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈdɪʃn/` · **US:** `/əˈdɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/add/addit/addition__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/add/addit/addition__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - All of these technologies are fairly recent additions.
  - addition to something the latest addition to our range of cars
  - This is a welcome addition to the literature of western art history.

### [admire](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/admire) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ədˈmaɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/ədˈmaɪər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/adm/admir/admire__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/adm/admir/admire__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - admire somebody/something I really admire your enthusiasm.
  - Her work was much admired by critics.
  - You have to admire the way he handled the situation.
  - Actually, I greatly admire and respect him.
  - admire somebody/something for something The school is widely admired for its excellent teaching.
  - admire somebody for doing something I don't agree with her, but I admire her for sticking to her principles.

### [admit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/admit) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ədˈmɪt/` · **US:** `/ədˈmɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/adm/admit/admit__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/adm/admit/admit__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It was a stupid thing to do, I admit.
  - + speech ‘I'm very nervous,’ she admitted reluctantly.
  - admit to something Don't be afraid to admit to your mistakes.
  - admit to doing something She admits to being strict with her children.
  - admit something He admitted all his mistakes.
  - She stubbornly refuses to admit the truth.
  - Why don't you just admit defeat (= recognize that you cannot do something) and let someone else try?
  - I hate to admit it, but I think he is right.
  - Admit it! You were terrified!
  - I'm almost ashamed to admit it, but I watch this show.
  - admit (that)… They freely admit (that) they still have a lot to learn.
  - You must admit that it all sounds very strange.
  - She readily admits mistakes were made.
  - admit to somebody that… I couldn't admit to my parents that I was finding the course difficult.
  - be admitted that… It was generally admitted that the government had acted too quickly.
  - be admitted to be/have something The appointment is now generally admitted to have been a mistake.

### [advanced](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/advanced) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ədˈvɑːnst/` · **US:** `/ədˈvænst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/adv/advan/advanced__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/adv/advan/advanced__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Scientists are working on highly advanced technology to replace fossil fuels.
  - It is a technologically advanced society.
  - Even in advanced industrial societies, poverty persists.
  - Economically advanced developing countries must make a contribution.
  - Sweden has a reputation for advanced and stylish design.

### [advise](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/advise) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ədˈvaɪz/` · **US:** `/ədˈvaɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/adv/advis/advise__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/adv/advis/advise__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - advise against doing something I would strongly advise against going out on your own.
  - advise somebody against doing something They advised me against visiting the troubled south of the country.
  - advise somebody against something The Foreign Office is advising Britons against all non-essential travel to the region.
  - advise against something They advised against the use of military force.
  - advise somebody Her mother was away and couldn't advise her.
  - advise something I'd advise extreme caution.
  - + speech ‘Get there early,’ she advised (them).
  - advise somebody to do something Police are advising people to stay at home.
  - Doctors advised the patient to have surgery.
  - I'd advise you not to tell him.
  - Solicitors advised their clients to contact their local councillors.
  - advise that… They advise that a passport be carried with you at all times.
  - (British English also) They advise that a passport should be carried with you at all times.
  - it is advised that… It is strongly advised that you take out insurance.
  - advise doing something I'd advise buying your tickets well in advance if you want to travel in August.

### [afford](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/afford) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈfɔːd/` · **US:** `/əˈfɔːrd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/aff/affor/afford__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/aff/affor/afford__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - afford something Can we afford a new car?
  - None of them could afford £50 for a ticket.
  - I’d give up work if I could afford it.
  - afford to do something We can't afford to go abroad this summer.
  - She never took a taxi, even though she could afford to.
  - She can well afford to pay for herself.
  - They couldn't afford to buy a house there.
  - We can't afford to have children.
  - afford something to do something He couldn't afford the money to go on the trip.

### [age](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/age_3) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/eɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/eɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/age/age__/age__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/age/age__/age__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - As he aged, his memory got worse.
  - The population is aging (= more people are living longer).
  - He looks a lot older than 45—he hasn't aged well at all.

### [aged](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/aged) *(adjective)*
- [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/age/aged_/aged__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/age/aged_/aged__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They have two children aged six and nine.
  - volunteers aged between 25 and 40
  - Children have the opportunity to meet similarly aged children.

### [agent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/agent) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeɪdʒənt/` · **US:** `/ˈeɪdʒənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/age/agent/agent__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/age/agent/agent__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Our agent in New York deals with all US sales.
  - If you're going to rent out your house while you're abroad, you'll need someone to act as your agent here.
  - an insurance agent
  - Typical agent's fees are around 6 per cent of the sale price.

### [agreement](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/agreement) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈɡriːmənt/` · **US:** `/əˈɡriːmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/agr/agree/agreement__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/agr/agree/agreement__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an international peace agreement
  - The agreement (= the document recording the agreement) was signed during a meeting at the UN.
  - agreement with somebody They have entered into a free trade agreement with Australia.
  - agreement between A and B An agreement was finally reached between management and employees.
  - agreement to do something They had an agreement never to talk about work at home.
  - They had made a verbal agreement to sell.
  - under an agreement The software is provided under a license agreement and may not be copied without permission.
  - The specific terms of the agreement have not been disclosed.
  - The documents are covered by a confidentiality agreement.

### [ahead](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ahead) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈhed/` · **US:** `/əˈhed/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ahe/ahead/ahead__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ahe/ahead/ahead__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'll run ahead and warn them.
  - The road ahead was blocked.
  - We've got a lot of hard work ahead.
  - This will create problems in the months ahead.
  - He was looking straight ahead (= straight forward, in front of him).

### [aim](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/aim_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/eɪm/` · **US:** `/eɪm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/aim/aim__/aim__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/aim/aim__/aim__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the stated aims of the study
  - Our main aim is to provide affordable childcare.
  - His sole aim in life is to enjoy himself.
  - Teamwork is required in order to achieve these aims.
  - to pursue/further/fulfil your aims
  - She set out the company's aims and objectives in her speech.
  - with the aim of doing something The organization was formed with the aim of helping local people.

### [aim](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/aim_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/eɪm/` · **US:** `/eɪm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/aim/aim__/aim__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/aim/aim__/aim__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He has always aimed high (= tried to achieve a lot).
  - aim for something We should aim for a bigger share of the market.
  - The team is aiming for the top this season.
  - Don't try to aim for perfection.
  - aim at something The government is aiming at a 50% reduction in unemployment.
  - aim to do something They are aiming to reduce unemployment by 50%.
  - The project aims to provide employment for people with learning difficulties.
  - We aim to be there around six.
  - aim at doing something The training programme aims at raising employees' awareness about human rights.

### [alarm](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/alarm_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈlɑːm/` · **US:** `/əˈlɑːrm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ala/alarm/alarm__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ala/alarm/alarm__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She decided to sound the alarm (= warn people that the situation was dangerous).
  - I hammered on all the doors to raise the alarm.
  - By the time the alarm was raised the intruders had escaped.

### [album](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/album) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈælbəm/` · **US:** `/ˈælbəm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/alb/album/album__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/alb/album/album__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a photo album
  - an online album (= a website where you can store and view photographs)
  - I keep the photographs in an album.

### [alcohol](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/alcohol) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈælkəhɒl/` · **US:** `/ˈælkəhɔːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/alc/alcoh/alcohol__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/alc/alcoh/alcohol__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He never drinks alcohol.
  - alcohol abuse/use/dependence
  - the dangers of excessive alcohol consumption
  - Alcohol should only be consumed in moderation.
  - Licences to sell alcohol are granted by local councils.
  - He was unable to overcome his addiction to alcohol.

### [alcoholic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/alcoholic_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌælkəˈhɒlɪk/` · **US:** `/ˌælkəˈhɑːlɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/alc/alcoh/alcoholic__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/alc/alcoh/alcoholic__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - alcoholic drinks/beverages
  - The fruit juice tasted slightly alcoholic.

### [alternative](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/alternative_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɔːlˈtɜːnətɪv/` · **US:** `/ɔːlˈtɜːrnətɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/alt/alter/alternative__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/alt/alter/alternative__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The road was closed so we had to find an alternative route.
  - Can you offer an alternative explanation?
  - an alternative method of working
  - Do you have an alternative solution?

### [amazed](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/amazed) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈmeɪzd/` · **US:** `/əˈmeɪzd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ama/amaze/amazed__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ama/amaze/amazed__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an amazed look
  - an amazed silence
  - amazed at somebody/something We were all amazed at the number of people who came.
  - I was amazed at her knowledge of French literature.
  - We just moved house and are amazed at the amount of junk we have.
  - amazed by somebody/something We are constantly amazed by the quality and range of the books.
  - We were amazed by his generosity.
  - amazed (that)… I was banging so loudly I’m amazed (that) they didn’t hear me.
  - amazed how… She was amazed how little he had changed.
  - amazed at how… I was amazed at how well we did.
  - amazed to see, find, learn, discover We were amazed to find that no one was hurt.

### [ambition](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ambition_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/æmˈbɪʃn/` · **US:** `/æmˈbɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/amb/ambit/ambition__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/amb/ambit/ambition__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She had fulfilled her lifelong ambition.
  - His burning ambition was to study medicine.
  - political/artistic/career ambitions
  - At last he had realized his life's ambition.
  - He had only one ambition in life.
  - the country's nuclear ambitions (= plans to develop nuclear weapons)
  - ambition of being/doing something She never achieved her ambition of becoming a famous writer.

### [ambitious](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ambitious) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/æmˈbɪʃəs/` · **US:** `/æmˈbɪʃəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/amb/ambit/ambitious__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/amb/ambit/ambitious__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a fiercely ambitious young manager
  - They were very ambitious for their children (= they wanted them to be successful).

### [analyse](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/analyse) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈænəlaɪz/` · **US:** `/ˈænəlaɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ana/analy/analyse__gb_6.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ana/analy/analyse__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - analyse something The job involves collecting and analysing data.
  - Researchers have analysed the results in detail using specialist software.
  - Learn to step back and critically analyse situations.
  - He tried to analyse his feelings.
  - The first step is to define and analyse the problem.
  - analyse something for something The water samples will be analysed for the presence of polluting chemicals.
  - analyse what, how, etc… We need to analyse what went wrong.

### [analysis](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/analysis) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈnæləsɪs/` · **US:** `/əˈnæləsɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ana/analy/analysis__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ana/analy/analysis__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - statistical/data analysis
  - a detailed analysis of the data
  - to perform/conduct an analysis
  - Further analysis revealed significant regional variations in the results.
  - We made the decision based on our analysis of the situation.
  - to be included in/excluded from the analysis

### [announce](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/announce) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈnaʊns/` · **US:** `/əˈnaʊns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ann/annou/announce__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ann/annou/announce__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - announce something He officially announced his intention to resign at today's press conference.
  - They haven't formally announced their engagement yet.
  - (figurative) A ring at the doorbell announced Jack's arrival.
  - announce that… We are pleased to announce that all five candidates were successful.
  - it is announced that… It was announced that new speed restrictions would be introduced.
  - announce something to somebody The company announced its decision to the public in March.
  - The government yesterday announced to the media plans to create a million new jobs.
  - announce somebody/something as something She was announced as the winner at last night's ceremony.

### [announcement](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/announcement) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈnaʊnsmənt/` · **US:** `/əˈnaʊnsmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ann/annou/announcement__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ann/annou/announcement__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to make an announcement
  - a formal/an official/a public announcement
  - The campaign will run public service announcements on radio stations around the country.
  - We welcome the recent announcement by the Government.
  - Announcements of births, marriages and deaths appear in some newspapers.
  - Today's announcement of a peace agreement came after weeks of discussion.
  - An announcement about her future is expected soon.
  - We saw an announcement in the national newspaper.

### [annoy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/annoy) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈnɔɪ/` · **US:** `/əˈnɔɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ann/annoy/annoy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ann/annoy/annoy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - annoy somebody His constant joking was beginning to annoy her.
  - I’m sure she does it just to annoy me.
  - I only stay out late to annoy my parents.
  - it annoys somebody when… It really annoys me when people forget to say thank you.
  - it annoys somebody to do something It annoys me to see him getting ahead of me.

### [annoyed](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/annoyed) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈnɔɪd/` · **US:** `/əˈnɔɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ann/annoy/annoyed__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ann/annoy/annoyed__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - annoyed with somebody at/about something He was beginning to get very annoyed with me about my carelessness.
  - annoyed at/about something I was a little annoyed about the whole thing.
  - annoyed with somebody I was a bit annoyed with him.
  - I was annoyed with myself for giving in so easily.
  - I bet she was annoyed at having to write it out again.
  - annoyed that… I was annoyed that they hadn't turned up.
  - annoyed to find, see, etc. He was annoyed to find himself going red.

### [annoying](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/annoying) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈnɔɪɪŋ/` · **US:** `/əˈnɔɪɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ann/annoy/annoying__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ann/annoy/annoying__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This interruption is very annoying.
  - Her most annoying habit was eating with her mouth open.
  - It's incredibly annoying, but that's life.

### [apart](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/apart) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈpɑːt/` · **US:** `/əˈpɑːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/apa/apart/apart__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/apa/apart/apart__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The two houses stood 500 metres apart.
  - Their birthdays are only three days apart.
  - (figurative) The two sides in the talks are still a long way apart (= are far from reaching an agreement).

### [apologize](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/apologize) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈpɒlədʒaɪz/` · **US:** `/əˈpɑːlədʒaɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/apo/apolo/apologize__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/apo/apolo/apologize__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Why should I apologize?
  - apologize to somebody Go and apologize to her.
  - He wanted to publicly apologize to his victims.
  - I want to apologize publicly on his behalf to those who have suffered.
  - He apologized profusely to his family and staff.
  - apologize for something I sincerely apologize for the enormous delay in delivery.
  - We apologize for the late departure of this flight.
  - I think Mr Peters should withdraw and apologize for his remark.
  - I apologize unreservedly for any offending remarks I made.
  - apologize to somebody for something We apologize to our customers for the inconvenience caused.

### [application](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/application) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌæplɪˈkeɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌæplɪˈkeɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/app/appli/application__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/app/appli/application__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a planning/patent/visa application
  - We have received applications from more than 3 000 students.
  - You need to complete the online application form.
  - application for something an application for asylum/a licence
  - (formal) an application for leave to appeal against the conviction
  - application to do something After graduating she made an application to join the BBC.
  - application to somebody (for something) His application to the court for bail has been refused.
  - application to somebody to do something They submitted an application to the council to build two houses.
  - on application to somebody Further information is available on application to the principal.

### [appointment](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/appointment) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈpɔɪntmənt/` · **US:** `/əˈpɔɪntmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/app/appoi/appointment__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/app/appoi/appointment__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I've got a dental appointment at 3 o'clock.
  - to book/make/keep an appointment
  - Do you have an appointment?
  - She has a doctor's appointment in twenty minutes.
  - Viewing is by appointment only (= only at a time that has been arranged in advance).
  - an appointment book
  - appointment with somebody an appointment with my agent/doctor/specialist/consultant
  - appointment for something an appointment for a blood test
  - appointment for somebody to do something She made an appointment for her son to see the doctor.

### [appreciate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/appreciate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈpriːʃieɪt/` · **US:** `/əˈpriːʃieɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/app/appre/appreciate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/app/appre/appreciate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Over the years he came to appreciate the beauty and tranquillity of the river.
  - His talents are not fully appreciated in that company.
  - Her family doesn't appreciate her.
  - You can't really appreciate foreign literature in translation.
  - The sound quality was poor so we couldn't fully appreciate the music.

### [approximately](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/approximately) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈprɒksɪmətli/` · **US:** `/əˈprɑːksɪmətli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/app/appro/approximately__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/app/appro/approximately__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The journey took approximately seven hours.
  - The two buildings were approximately equal in size.
  - Approximately £4  000 worth of product was stolen in the raid.

### [arrest](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/arrest_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈrest/` · **US:** `/əˈrest/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/arr/arres/arrest__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/arr/arres/arrest__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The police made several arrests.
  - under arrest She was under arrest on suspicion of murder.
  - Opposition leaders were put under house arrest (= not allowed to leave their houses).
  - He was placed under arrest by the New York City police.

### [arrest](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/arrest_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈrest/` · **US:** `/əˈrest/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/arr/arres/arrest__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/arr/arres/arrest__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - arrest somebody A man has been arrested in connection with the robbery.
  - A local man has since been arrested on suspicion of murder.
  - Police arrested three suspects.
  - He was arrested on corruption charges.
  - She was arrested and charged with arson.
  - arrest somebody for something She was arrested for drug-related offences.
  - arrest somebody for doing something You could get arrested for doing that.

### [arrival](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/arrival) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈraɪvl/` · **US:** `/əˈraɪvl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/arr/arriv/arrival__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/arr/arriv/arrival__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We apologize for the late arrival of the train.
  - the arrival of the mail in the morning
  - Our estimated time of arrival is 7.15.
  - I need to check our arrival time.
  - on/upon arrival Guests receive dinner on arrival at the hotel.
  - She was declared dead on arrival at the hospital.
  - There are 120 arrivals and departures every day.
  - daily arrivals of new patients
  - They are eagerly awaiting the arrival of their new computer.

### [assignment](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/assignment) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈsaɪnmənt/` · **US:** `/əˈsaɪnmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ass/assig/assignment__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ass/assig/assignment__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Students are required to complete all homework assignments.
  - You will need to complete three written assignments per semester.
  - a business/special assignment
  - I had set myself a tough assignment.
  - on an assignment She is in Greece on an assignment for one of the Sunday newspapers.
  - on assignment one of our reporters on assignment in China

### [assist](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/assist_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈsɪst/` · **US:** `/əˈsɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ass/assis/assist__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ass/assis/assist__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Anyone willing to assist can contact this number.
  - assist in/with something He assisted in the development of the business strategy.
  - We are looking for people who would be willing to assist in the group's work.
  - assist somebody We'll do all we can to assist you.
  - They will be ably assisted by our remarkable staff.
  - The play was directed by Mike Johnson, assisted by Sharon Gale.
  - His task is to advise and assist his colleagues.
  - assist somebody in doing something We will assist you in finding somewhere to live.
  - assist somebody in/with something Two men are assisting the police with their enquiries (= are being questioned by the police).
  - The driver has been assisting police with their investigation.
  - assist somebody to do something a course to assist adults to return to the labour market

### [atmosphere](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/atmosphere) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈætməsfɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈætməsfɪr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/atm/atmos/atmosphere__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/atm/atmos/atmosphere__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Wind power doesn't release carbon dioxide into the atmosphere.
  - greenhouse gases in the earth's atmosphere
  - Volcanoes and hot springs can emit methane into the atmosphere.
  - the upper atmosphere
  - pollution of the atmosphere

### [attach](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/attach) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈtætʃ/` · **US:** `/əˈtætʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/att/attac/attach__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/att/attac/attach__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - attach something I attach a copy of my notes for your information.
  - A copy of his document is attached as Appendix 3 at the end of this book.
  - attach something to something Attach the coupon to the front of your letter.
  - (figurative) They have attached a number of conditions to the agreement (= said that the conditions must be part of the agreement)

### [attitude](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/attitude) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈætɪtjuːd/` · **US:** `/ˈætɪtuːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/att/attit/attitude__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/att/attit/attitude__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - attitude towards somebody/something These societies have to change their attitudes towards women.
  - the government’s attitude towards single parents
  - to have a positive/negative attitude towards somebody/something
  - attitude to somebody/something changes in public attitudes to marriage
  - attitude about something social attitudes about education
  - attitude on something changing attitudes on issues such as gay marriage
  - Youth is simply an attitude of mind.
  - If you want to pass your exams you'd better change your attitude!
  - If they can adopt that kind of attitude then the future looks very promising.
  - You're taking a pretty selfish attitude over this, aren't you?

### [attract](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/attract) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈtrækt/` · **US:** `/əˈtrækt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/att/attra/attract__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/att/attra/attract__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - be attracted by somebody/something I had always been attracted by the idea of working abroad.
  - attract somebody (to somebody/something) What first attracted me to her was her sense of humour.
  - She finds herself increasingly attracted to them and their lifestyle.

### [attraction](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/attraction) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈtrækʃn/` · **US:** `/əˈtrækʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/att/attra/attraction__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/att/attra/attraction__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Buckingham Palace is a major tourist attraction.
  - The main attraction at Giverny is Monet's garden.
  - Attractions at the fair include a mini-circus, clowns, dancers and a jazz band.
  - Feeding the animals proved a popular attraction for visitors to the farm.
  - The library is expected to be a major visitor attraction for the town.

### [authority](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/authority) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɔːˈθɒrəti/` · **US:** `/əˈθɔːrəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/aut/autho/authority__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/aut/autho/authority__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in a position of authority
  - to undermine/challenge somebody's authority
  - authority over somebody/something She now has authority over the people who used to be her bosses.
  - The law states that parents exercise authority over their children.
  - in authority Nothing will be done because no one in authority (= who has a position of power) takes the matter seriously.
  - adult authority figures such as parents and teachers

### [average](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/average_3) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈævərɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈævərɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ave/avera/average__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ave/avera/average__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Economic growth is expected to average 2% next year.
  - Drivers in London can expect to average about 12 miles per hour (= to have that as their average speed).
  - The women averaged 41 points per game.
  - He has averaged 15 goals over the past two seasons.
  - Wages here average less than three dollars an hour.

### [award](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/award_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈwɔːd/` · **US:** `/əˈwɔːrd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/awa/award/award__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/awa/award/award__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - award something Knowing why and how corporations award contracts is vitally important.
  - award something to somebody The judges awarded equal points to both finalists.
  - award somebody something The judges awarded both finalists equal points.
  - He was awarded damages of £50 000.
  - He was posthumously awarded the Victoria Cross.
  - The referee awarded a penalty against them.

### [aware](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/aware) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈweə(r)/` · **US:** `/əˈwer/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/awa/aware/aware__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/awa/aware/aware__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - As you're aware, this is not a new problem.
  - As far as I'm aware, nobody has done anything about it.
  - acutely/painfully/keenly (= very) aware
  - aware of something He was well aware of the problem.
  - She slipped away without him being aware of it.
  - They suddenly became aware of people looking at them.
  - Everybody should be made aware of the risks involved.
  - We're just making the public aware of the issue.
  - I am fully aware of the fact that change needs time.
  - I don't think people are really aware of just how much it costs.
  - aware that… Were you aware that something was wrong?
  - I was aware that she was trembling.


## B

### [backwards](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/backwards) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbækwədz/` · **US:** `/ˈbækwərdz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bac/backw/backwards__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bac/backw/backwards__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I lost my balance and fell backwards.
  - He took a step backwards.
  - I stumbled backwards and fell.

### [bake](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bake_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/beɪk/` · **US:** `/beɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bak/bake_/bake__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bak/bake_/bake__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - bake (something) to bake bread/biscuits/cookies
  - baked potatoes/apples
  - the delicious smell of baking bread
  - The bread is baking in the oven.
  - I've been baking all morning.
  - bake something for somebody I'm baking a birthday cake for Alex.
  - bake somebody something I'm baking Alex a cake.

### [balance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/balance_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbæləns/` · **US:** `/ˈbæləns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bal/balan/balance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bal/balan/balance__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This newspaper maintains a good balance in its presentation of different opinions.
  - Tourists often disturb the delicate balance of nature on the island.
  - His wife's death disturbed the balance of his mind.
  - balance between A and B Try to keep a balance between work and relaxation.
  - in balance It is important to keep the different aspects of your life in balance.

### [balance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/balance_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbæləns/` · **US:** `/ˈbæləns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bal/balan/balance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bal/balan/balance__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - balance on something How long can you balance on one leg?
  - balance something on something The television was precariously balanced on top of a pile of books.
  - She balanced the cup on her knee.
  - He balanced the glasses carefully on the tray.

### [ban](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ban_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/bæn/` · **US:** `/bæn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/ban/ban__/ban__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/ban/ban__/ban__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a smoking ban
  - A majority of people are against the ban.
  - ban on something They are calling for a blanket ban (= complete ban) on the use of phones while driving.
  - They have imposed a total ban on the import of seal skins.
  - There are no plans to lift the ban on the sale of fireworks to children.

### [ban](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ban_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/bæn/` · **US:** `/bæn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/ban/ban__/ban__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/ban/ban__/ban__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ban something The law effectively bans smoking in all public places.
  - a list of banned substances
  - The use of these chemicals in homes was banned outright by the government in 2000.
  - ban something from something a campaign to ban sugary drinks from schools

### [base](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/base_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/beɪs/` · **US:** `/beɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bas/base_/base__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bas/base_/base__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The lamp has a heavy base.
  - the base of a column/glass
  - at the base of something He felt a sharp pain at the base of his spine.

### [base](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/base_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/beɪs/` · **US:** `/beɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bas/base_/base__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bas/base_/base__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - be based in… The organization is now based in Geneva.
  - base something/somebody in… They decided to base the new company in York.
  - base yourself in… We're going to base ourselves in Tokyo and make trips from there.
  - base somebody/something/yourself + adv./prep. I would like to base myself somewhere warmer.

### [basic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/basic_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbeɪsɪk/` · **US:** `/ˈbeɪsɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bas/basic/basic__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bas/basic/basic__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - basic information/facts/ideas
  - the basic principles of law
  - basic life skills
  - basic to something Drums are basic to African music.

### [basis](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/basis) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbeɪsɪs/` · **US:** `/ˈbeɪsɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bas/basis/basis__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bas/basis/basis__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We are in contact on a regular basis.
  - to be employed on a permanent/temporary/part-time basis
  - Fatal accidents occur on our roads on a daily basis.
  - on a day-to-day/weekly/monthly basis
  - Applications will be considered on a case-by-case basis.
  - All members of the committee work on a voluntary basis.

### [battery](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/battery) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbætəri/` · **US:** `/ˈbætəri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bat/batte/battery__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bat/batte/battery__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to replace the batteries
  - a rechargeable battery
  - battery-powered/-operated
  - a car battery
  - The battery is flat (= it is no longer producing electricity).
  - With our product you get longer battery life.
  - The bicycle even has a built-in battery charger for a mobile phone.
  - Isolated farms used wind turbines to charge batteries.
  - a lithium/AA battery

### [battle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/battle_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbætl/` · **US:** `/ˈbætl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bat/battl/battle__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bat/battl/battle__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Napoleon was defeated at the Battle of Waterloo.
  - in battle His father had been killed in battle.
  - to go into battle
  - battle with somebody Hundreds of protesters fought running battles with the police.
  - battle against somebody In 1817 Bolivar won a series of battles against Spanish forces.
  - battle between A and B It ended in a gun battle between police and drug smugglers.

### [beauty](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/beauty) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbjuːti/` · **US:** `/ˈbjuːti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bea/beaut/beauty__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bea/beaut/beauty__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - beauty of something the beauty of the sunset/of poetry/of his singing
  - The woods were designated an area of outstanding natural beauty.
  - beauty products/treatments (= intended to make a person more beautiful)
  - She was a contestant in the Miss World beauty pageant.
  - His voice is a thing of beauty.
  - The sheer beauty of the scenery took my breath away.
  - a woman of great beauty

### [bee](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bee) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/biː/` · **US:** `/biː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bee/bee__/bee__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bee/bee__/bee__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Bees were buzzing in the clover.
  - a swarm of bees
  - a bee sting

### [belief](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/belief) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/bɪˈliːf/` · **US:** `/bɪˈliːf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bel/belie/belief__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bel/belie/belief__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - belief in something/somebody I admire his passionate belief in what he is doing.
  - belief in God/democracy
  - The incident has shaken my belief (= made me have less confidence) in the police.
  - belief that… They share a belief that there is life after death.

### [bell](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bell) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/bel/` · **US:** `/bel/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bel/bell_/bell__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bel/bell_/bell__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A peal of church bells rang out in the distance.
  - a bicycle bell
  - His voice came down the line as clear as a bell.
  - the bell of a trumpet (= the part at the end like a bell in shape)
  - The church bells rang out.
  - a bell-shaped flower
  - Off in the distance a bell tolled two in the morning.
  - a bell tower
  - wedding bells
  - The dancers wore bells on their ankles.

### [bend](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bend_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/bend/` · **US:** `/bend/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/ben/bend_/bend__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/ben/bend_/bend__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a sharp bend in the road
  - You took that bend very fast!
  - He lost control on a bend and crashed into a tree.
  - As the driver came round the bend, she saw the lorry.

### [bend](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bend_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/bend/` · **US:** `/bend/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/ben/bend_/bend__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/ben/bend_/bend__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He bent and kissed her.
  - The doctor told me to avoid bending and stretching.
  - + adv./prep. fields of poppies bending in the wind
  - She suddenly bent over, clutching her stomach.
  - His dark head bent over her.
  - She bent forward to pick up the newspaper.
  - Slowly bend from the waist and bring your head down to your knees.
  - Keep your feet apart, and bend at the waist
  - bend something He bent his head and kissed her.
  - bend something + adv./prep. She bent her head towards him.
  - She was bent over her desk writing a letter.

### [benefit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/benefit_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbenɪfɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈbenɪfɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/ben/benef/benefit__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/ben/benef/benefit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We should spend the money on something that will benefit everyone.
  - The new tax laws will clearly benefit those on low wages.
  - The project will benefit the local community as a whole.

### [better](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/better_3) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbetə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈbetər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bet/bette/better__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bet/bette/better__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the better of the two books
  - I expected better of him (= I thought he would have behaved better).

### [bite](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bite_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/baɪt/` · **US:** `/baɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bit/bite_/bite__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bit/bite_/bite__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The dog gave me a playful bite.

### [bite](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bite_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/baɪt/` · **US:** `/baɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bit/bite_/bite__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bit/bite_/bite__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Does your dog bite?
  - Come here! I won't bite! (= you don't need to be afraid)
  - bite into/through something She bit into a ripe juicy pear.
  - bite somebody/something She was bitten by the family dog.
  - Stop biting your nails!
  - bite off something/sth off He bit off a large chunk of bread/He bit a large chunk of bread off.

### [block](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/block_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/blɒk/` · **US:** `/blɑːk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/blo/block/block__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/blo/block/block__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The houses are made of concrete blocks with tin roofs.
  - block of something a block of ice/wood
  - The wall was made from massive blocks of stone.

### [block](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/block_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/blɒk/` · **US:** `/blɑːk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/blo/block/block__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/blo/block/block__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - After today's heavy snow, many roads are still blocked.
  - a blocked sink
  - A truck had overturned and was completely blocking traffic.

### [board](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/board_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/bɔːd/` · **US:** `/bɔːrd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/boa/board/board__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/boa/board/board__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Passengers are waiting to board.
  - board something He tried to board a plane at Nice airport.
  - to board a bus/train/ship/flight
  - The ship was boarded by customs officials.
  - She boarded a train for Philadelphia.

### [bomb](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bomb_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/bɒm/` · **US:** `/bɑːm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bom/bomb_/bomb__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bom/bomb_/bomb__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a bomb goes off/explodes
  - Hundreds of bombs were dropped on the city.
  - to plant/detonate a bomb
  - a suicide bomb
  - a roadside bomb (= one that is designed to blow up vehicles)
  - a bomb attack/blast
  - extensive bomb damage

### [bomb](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bomb_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/bɒm/` · **US:** `/bɑːm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bom/bomb_/bomb__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bom/bomb_/bomb__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Warplanes bombed targets in and around the capital.
  - They have deliberately bombed civilians.
  - The city was heavily bombed in the war.

### [border](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/border_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbɔːdə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈbɔːrdər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bor/borde/border__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bor/borde/border__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I live in a small town in the US, near the Canadian border.
  - Thousands of people cross the border every day.
  - They took steps to secure the border.
  - to seal/close the border
  - border between A and B They spent a week in a national park on the border between Kenya and Tanzania.
  - (figurative) It is difficult to define the border between love and friendship.
  - border with something The treaty fixed Denmark's new border with Germany.
  - across the border They fled across the border.
  - over the border They live just over the border.
  - on the border The incident happened on Nevada's northern border.
  - at the border They were stopped at the border.
  - along the border There are tensions all along the border.
  - border crossings
  - border patrols/security/guards
  - border controls
  - a border dispute
  - a border town/village/county
  - a border region/area

### [bother](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bother_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbɒðə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈbɑːðər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bot/bothe/bother__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bot/bothe/bother__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘Shall I wait?’ ‘No, don't bother’.
  - I don't know why I bother! Nobody ever listens!
  - If that’s all the thanks I get, I won’t bother in future!
  - bother with/about something It's not worth bothering with (= using) an umbrella—the car's just outside.
  - I don't know why you bother with that crowd (= why you spend time with them).
  - He doesn’t bother much about his appearance.
  - bother to do something He didn't even bother to let me know he was coming.
  - He hadn't even bothered to read the crucial documents.
  - Doctors never bothered to check his blood pressure.
  - bother doing something Why bother asking if you're not really interested?
  - I didn't bother trying to explain my feelings.

### [branch](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/branch_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/brɑːntʃ/` · **US:** `/bræntʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bra/branc/branch__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bra/branc/branch__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She climbed the tree and hid in the branches.
  - the bare branches of a tree in winter

### [brand](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/brand_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/brænd/` · **US:** `/brænd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bra/brand/brand__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bra/brand/brand__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a well-known brand of toothpaste
  - (British English) You pay less for the supermarket's own brand.
  - (North American English) You pay less for the store brand.
  - luxury/premium brands
  - We compared our product with the leading brands.
  - How can we build our brand?
  - brand loyalty (= when customers continue buying the same brand)
  - brand awareness/recognition
  - brand image/values

### [brand](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/brand_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/brænd/` · **US:** `/brænd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bra/brand/brand__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bra/brand/brand__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - brand something The website was not doing a very good job of branding the company.
  - brand something with something Stadiums are branded with corporate logos.
  - brand something (as) something Their products are branded as organic.
  - brand itself (as) something The city is trying to brand itself a world-class capital.

### [brave](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/brave_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/breɪv/` · **US:** `/breɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bra/brave/brave__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bra/brave/brave__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - brave men and women
  - Be brave!
  - I wasn't brave enough to tell her what I thought of her.
  - Desperation had made me brave.
  - She has been incredibly brave.

### [breath](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/breath) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/breθ/` · **US:** `/breθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bre/breat/breath__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bre/breat/breath__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - His breath smelt of garlic.
  - bad breath (= that smells bad)
  - She was very short of breath (= had difficulty breathing).
  - Patients develop a cough and shortness of breath.
  - There was a sharp intake of breath in the courtroom.
  - We had to stop for breath before we got to the top.
  - While there is breath left in my body, I will refuse.

### [breathe](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/breathe) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/briːð/` · **US:** `/briːð/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bre/breat/breathe__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bre/breat/breathe__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He breathed deeply before speaking again.
  - The air was so cold we could hardly breathe.
  - She was beginning to breathe more easily.
  - He was breathing heavily after his exertions.
  - breathe something Most people don't realize that they are breathing polluted air.

### [breathing](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/breathing) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbriːðɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈbriːðɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bre/breat/breathing__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bre/breat/breathing__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Her breathing became steady and she fell asleep.
  - Deep breathing exercises will help you relax.
  - Heavy (= loud) breathing was all I could hear.
  - Firefighters wearing breathing apparatus went into the house.
  - The drug is used to treat breathing difficulties caused by asthma.
  - Are you having difficulty in breathing?

### [bride](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bride) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/braɪd/` · **US:** `/braɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bri/bride/bride__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bri/bride/bride__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a toast to the bride and groom
  - The bride wore an elegant gown of ivory satin.
  - He introduced his new bride.

### [bubble](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bubble_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbʌbl/` · **US:** `/ˈbʌbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bub/bubbl/bubble__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bub/bubbl/bubble__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - champagne bubbles
  - a bubble of oxygen
  - He blew bubbles into the water through a straw.

### [bury](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bury) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈberi/` · **US:** `/ˈberi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bur/bury_/bury__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bur/bury_/bury__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - bury somebody/something They killed her and buried her body.
  - bury somebody/something + adv./prep. He was buried in Highgate Cemetery.
  - (figurative) Their ambitions were finally dead and buried.

### [by](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/by_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/baɪ/` · **US:** `/baɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/by_/by__g/by__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/by_/by__u/by__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Just drive by. Don't stop.
  - He hurried by without speaking to me.
  - Excuse me, I can't get by.
  - Time goes by so quickly.


## C

### [calm](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/calm_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kɑːm/` · **US:** `/kɑːm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cal/calm_/calm__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cal/calm_/calm__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It is important to keep calm in an emergency.
  - He always stays calm under pressure.
  - Try to remain calm.
  - Her voice was surprisingly calm.
  - He was calm and cool in a crisis.
  - They were impressed by her calm demeanour.
  - The city is calm again (= free from trouble and fighting) after yesterday's riots.

### [calm](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/calm_3) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kɑːm/` · **US:** `/kɑːm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cal/calm_/calm__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cal/calm_/calm__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The police appealed for calm.
  - Community leaders have called for calm.
  - The bombings ended a period of relative calm.
  - Many children regarded their school as an oasis of calm away from the war-torn streets.
  - Calm had been restored to the capital.
  - calm of something the calm of a summer evening

### [calm](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/calm_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kɑːm/` · **US:** `/kɑːm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cal/calm_/calm__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cal/calm_/calm__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Have some tea; it'll calm your nerves.
  - The announcement by the government did little to calm the fears of investors.
  - I breathed in slowly, trying to calm myself.
  - Police were called to calm the crowd that gathered.
  - His presence had a calming influence.

### [campaign](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/campaign_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kæmˈpeɪn/` · **US:** `/kæmˈpeɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cam/campa/campaign__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cam/campa/campaign__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an anti-smoking campaign
  - an advertising/a marketing campaign
  - an election campaign
  - the recent presidential campaign
  - campaign for something the campaign for parliamentary reform
  - campaign against something a campaign against ageism in the workplace
  - campaign to do something Today police launched a campaign to reduce road accidents.
  - They began a grass-roots campaign to encourage people to shop locally.
  - to mount/start a campaign
  - to run/lead/spearhead a campaign
  - Both sides have been accused of waging a dirty campaign.
  - the governor's campaign manager
  - The candidates are on the campaign trail (= travelling around to attract support).

### [campaign](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/campaign_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kæmˈpeɪn/` · **US:** `/kæmˈpeɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cam/campa/campaign__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cam/campa/campaign__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The party campaigned vigorously in the north of the country.
  - campaign for somebody/something She has campaigned tirelessly for their release.
  - campaign against somebody/something We have actively campaigned against whaling for the last 15 years.
  - She campaigned on a pro-business platform (= with policies to help business).
  - We campaign on behalf of consumers.
  - campaign to do something They are campaigning to save the area from building development.

### [campus](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/campus) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkæmpəs/` · **US:** `/ˈkæmpəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cam/campu/campus__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cam/campu/campus__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - university/college campuses
  - The university has campuses in Cairns and Brisbane.
  - on campus She lives on campus (= within the main university area).
  - off campus The number of graduate students living off campus has dropped.
  - Students at the main campus have heeded the call to boycott lectures.
  - Wi-fi is also provided in many locations around the campus.
  - The statue stands on the campus of Princeton University.
  - campus life

### [candidate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/candidate) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkændɪdeɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈkændɪdeɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/can/candi/candidate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/can/candi/candidate__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a presidential candidate
  - candidate for something one of the leading candidates for the presidency
  - He is the best candidate for the job.
  - (British English) She stood as a candidate in the local elections.
  - The party is fielding more candidates than ever before.
  - to nominate/select a candidate
  - to endorse a candidate (= say you support them)

### [cap](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cap_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kæp/` · **US:** `/kæp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cap/cap__/cap__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cap/cap__/cap__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to wear a cap.
  - to put on/take off a cap
  - to have a cap on
  - a school cap
  - a peaked cap

### [captain](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/captain_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkæptɪn/` · **US:** `/ˈkæptɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cap/capta/captain__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cap/capta/captain__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Captain Cook
  - The captain gave the order to abandon ship.
  - The captain of the ship invited them to dine at his table.
  - The captain of the plane was already announcing our arrival.
  - Her husband had been a ship's captain.
  - Captain Jones and her co-pilot

### [careless](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/careless) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkeələs/` · **US:** `/ˈkerləs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/car/carel/careless__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/car/carel/careless__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - careless driving
  - a careless worker/driver
  - it is careless of somebody to do something It was careless of me to leave the door open.
  - it is careless to do something It would be careless to ignore the risks.
  - careless of somebody Losing my phone again was very careless of me.
  - careless with something He's very careless with money.
  - careless about something Don't be so careless about spelling.
  - careless about doing something People have become increasingly careless about revealing personal information online.

### [category](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/category) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkætəɡəri/` · **US:** `/ˈkætəɡɔːri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cat/categ/category__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cat/categ/category__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - These are the nominees from each category.
  - in a category He competed in the youngest age category.
  - Students over 25 fall into a different category.
  - The results can be divided into three broad categories.
  - It is not useful to divide schoolchildren into separate categories.
  - The film doesn't fit into any specific category.
  - by category You can filter the results by category.

### [ceiling](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ceiling) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsiːlɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈsiːlɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cei/ceili/ceiling__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cei/ceili/ceiling__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a large room with a high ceiling
  - The walls and ceiling were painted white.
  - A large chandelier hangs from the ceiling.
  - on a ceiling a fly on the ceiling
  - ceiling of something The ceiling of her room was low.
  - She lay on her back staring up at the ceiling.

### [celebration](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/celebration) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌselɪˈbreɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌselɪˈbreɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cel/celeb/celebration__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cel/celeb/celebration__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The occasion was the 40th anniversary celebrations of the orchestra.
  - birthday celebrations
  - wedding/victory celebrations
  - I hope you'll join in the Christmas celebrations.
  - They held a celebration to mark forty years of the lifeboat service.
