  - As part of the celebrations, the school is planning an open day for the public.
  - They were among the crowd that attended the celebration.
  - at a celebration The winners will be honoured at a celebration in Boston next week.

### [central](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/central) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsentrəl/` · **US:** `/ˈsentrəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cen/centr/central__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cen/centr/central__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - central London
  - Central America/Europe/Asia
  - There will be rain later in central and eastern parts of the country.
  - the central area of the brain

### [centre](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/centre_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsentə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈsentər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cen/centr/centre__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cen/centr/centre__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - centre around/round somebody/something State occasions always centred around the king.
  - be centred around/round somebody/something Leisure activities were largely centred around the family.
  - centre on/upon somebody/something The debate centred on an important practical question.
  - be centred on/upon somebody/something Discussions were centred on developments in Eastern Europe.

### [ceremony](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ceremony) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈserəməni/` · **US:** `/ˈserəməʊni/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cer/cerem/ceremony__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cer/cerem/ceremony__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - More than 1 000 people attended the ceremony.
  - an awards/opening ceremony
  - a wedding/marriage/graduation ceremony
  - a religious/civil ceremony
  - The priest had travelled from Australia to perform the ceremony.
  - at a ceremony She was last seen in public at the closing ceremony of the Commonwealth Games
  - Who officiated at the ceremony?
  - in a ceremony They were married in a simple ceremony.
  - ceremony for somebody/something The villagers held a memorial ceremony for those who lost their lives in the war.
  - ceremony to do something There will be a special ceremony to mark the 60th anniversary of the battle.

### [chain](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/chain_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tʃeɪn/` · **US:** `/tʃeɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cha/chain/chain__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cha/chain/chain__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She wore a heavy gold chain around her neck.
  - The mayor wore his chain of office.
  - a bicycle chain
  - in chains The prisoners were kept in chains (= with chains around their arms and legs, to prevent them from escaping).
  - on a chain They kept the dog on a chain all day long.
  - a short length of chain

### [challenge](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/challenge_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtʃælɪndʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈtʃælɪndʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cha/chall/challenge__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cha/chall/challenge__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a tough/major/significant challenge
  - The role will be the biggest challenge of his acting career.
  - She still faces many challenges (= has to deal with them).
  - Destruction of the environment is one of the most serious challenges we face.
  - The greatest challenge facing the region is unemployment.
  - Rapid industrialization presents both challenges and opportunities.
  - challenge of something Countries need to work together to address the challenge of climate change.
  - Schools must meet the challenge of new technology (= deal with it successfully).
  - I’m looking forward to the challenge of my new job.
  - challenge of doing something The gallery has risen to the challenge of exhibiting the works of young artists.
  - challenge for somebody/something The challenge for the government is to find a way of providing affordable housing.
  - challenge to somebody/something Digital piracy continues to pose huge challenges to the industry.

### [champion](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/champion_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtʃæmpiən/` · **US:** `/ˈtʃæmpiən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cha/champ/champion__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cha/champ/champion__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the world/European/national/Olympic champion
  - the defending champion (= the person who was champion the last time and is trying to be champion again)
  - the reigning champion (= the person who is champion now)
  - the world basketball champions
  - a champion sprinter/boxer/swimmer
  - He became the youngest US Open champion for 88 years.
  - champion of something the heavyweight champion of the world
  - a former Italian champion jockey
  - the Commonwealth Games champion in the 5,000 metres
  - They were champions at the Winter Olympics in Chamonix.

### [channel](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/channel_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtʃænl/` · **US:** `/ˈtʃænl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cha/chann/channel__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cha/chann/channel__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a television/TV channel
  - cable/digital/satellite channels
  - a news channel
  - a music/sports/movie channel
  - to change/switch channels
  - on a channel What's on Channel 4 tonight?

### [chapter](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/chapter) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtʃæptə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈtʃæptər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cha/chapt/chapter__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cha/chapt/chapter__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to read/write a chapter
  - He devotes an entire chapter to this topic.
  - I've just finished Chapter 3.
  - I read chapter after chapter of the book.
  - in a chapter in the previous/next/first/last chapter
  - in a later chapter
  - There is a useful summary at the end of the chapter.
  - chapter of something the final chapter of her autobiography
  - the opening chapter of the book
  - chapter in something the most interesting chapter in the book
  - chapter on something Have you read the chapter on the legal system?

### [charge](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/charge_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tʃɑːdʒ/` · **US:** `/tʃɑːrdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cha/charg/charge__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cha/charg/charge__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - admission charges
  - charge for something We have to make a small charge for refreshments.
  - Delivery is free of charge.

### [charge](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/charge_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/tʃɑːdʒ/` · **US:** `/tʃɑːrdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cha/charg/charge__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cha/charg/charge__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The fees charged by some companies are excessive.
  - charge something for something What did they charge for the repairs?
  - The restaurant charges £40 for the set menu.
  - They are charging a reasonable price for the album.
  - charge somebody for something We won't charge you for delivery.
  - charge somebody something He only charged me half price.
  - charge somebody something for something She charged me £300 for the car.
  - charge something at something Calls are charged at 36p per minute.
  - charge for something Do you think museums should charge for admission?
  - charge to do something The bank doesn't charge to stop a payment.
  - charge somebody to do something They charge you to change money into euros.

### [cheap](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cheap_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/tʃiːp/` · **US:** `/tʃiːp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/che/cheap/cheap__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/che/cheap/cheap__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I got this dress cheap in a sale.
  - You can buy it cheaper elsewhere.
  - They're selling fabrics cheap this week.
  - Two people can live cheaper than one.
  - private equity companies that borrow cheap

### [cheat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cheat_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tʃiːt/` · **US:** `/tʃiːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/che/cheat/cheat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/che/cheat/cheat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You little cheat!
  - Are you accusing me of being a cheat?
  - a plan to crack down on tax cheats
  - benefit/welfare cheats
  - attempts to eliminate drug cheats from the sport
  - I've been a liar and a cheat.

### [cheat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cheat_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/tʃiːt/` · **US:** `/tʃiːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/che/cheat/cheat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/che/cheat/cheat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - cheat somebody/something She is accused of attempting to cheat the taxman.
  - We will crack down on people who try to cheat the system.
  - Many people feel cheated by the election process.
  - cheat somebody out of something He cheated investors out of billions of dollars.

### [cheerful](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cheerful) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtʃɪəfl/` · **US:** `/ˈtʃɪrfl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/che/cheer/cheerful__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/che/cheer/cheerful__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You're not your usual cheerful self today.
  - a cheerful smile/voice/face/disposition
  - to look/seem/sound cheerful
  - He felt bright and cheerful and full of energy.
  - You’re in a cheerful mood.
  - He's always been a cheerful, hard-working employee.

### [chemical](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/chemical_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkemɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˈkemɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/che/chemi/chemical__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/che/chemi/chemical__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - changes in the chemical composition of the atmosphere

### [chemical](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/chemical_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkemɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˈkemɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/che/chemi/chemical__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/che/chemi/chemical__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - toxic chemicals
  - Toy manufacturers have been banned from using the chemicals.
  - exposure to hazardous chemicals
  - Farmers are being urged to reduce their use of chemicals and work with nature to combat pests.
  - The condition may be caused by an imbalance of chemicals in the brain.

### [chest](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/chest) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tʃest/` · **US:** `/tʃest/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/che/chest/chest__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/che/chest/chest__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - chest pains
  - She gasped for breath, her chest heaving.
  - She folded her arms across her chest
  - a chest infection
  - in the chest symptoms such as wheezing or a tightness in the chest
  - The bullet hit him in the chest.
  - a hairy chest
  - a gorilla beating his chest
  - She pulled her knees to her chest, and hugged them tight.

### [childhood](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/childhood) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtʃaɪldhʊd/` · **US:** `/ˈtʃaɪldhʊd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/chi/child/childhood__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/chi/child/childhood__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - childhood, adolescence, and adulthood
  - He spent his childhood in India.
  - She had a happy childhood.
  - the problem of childhood obesity
  - childhood memories/experiences
  - in/during (somebody's) childhood in early childhood
  - His interest in farming developed during his childhood in Shropshire.
  - memories from childhood
  - books you remember from your childhood
  - We have known each other since childhood.
  - a TV show he's loved since his childhood
  - a childhood friend
  - her childhood home
  - He married his childhood sweetheart.
  - childhood cancer/leukaemia/asthma
  - funds for early childhood education

### [claim](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/claim_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kleɪm/` · **US:** `/kleɪm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cla/claim/claim__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cla/claim/claim__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - claim about somebody/something The company had made false claims about its products.
  - claim of something to reject/dismiss/deny claims of discrimination
  - a report examining claims of corrupt links between politicians
  - claim that… The singer has denied the magazine's claim that she is leaving the band.

### [claim](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/claim_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kleɪm/` · **US:** `/kleɪm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cla/claim/claim__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cla/claim/claim__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - claim (that)… He claims (that) he was not given a fair hearing.
  - claim to be/do something I don't claim to be an expert.
  - She claimed to hate all art.
  - claim somebody/something to be/do something She's not the saint that many have claimed her to be.
  - After disappointing box office results, newspapers claimed the show to be a failure
  - claim something Scientists are claiming a major breakthrough in the fight against cancer.
  - it is claimed that… It was claimed that some doctors were working 80 hours a week.
  - + speech ‘I did not tell any lies,’ she claimed.

### [clause](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/clause) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/klɔːz/` · **US:** `/klɔːz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cla/claus/clause__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cla/claus/clause__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in a clause There are languages that require the subject to come before the object in a clause.
  - It is the object of the verb of the clause.
  - Commas are used to separate clauses of sentences.
  - the second clause in the sentence

### [clear](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/clear_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/klɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/klɪr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cle/clear/clear__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cle/clear/clear__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - clear something The settlers cleared the land and planted crops.
  - It was several hours before the road was cleared after the accident.
  - It's your turn to clear the table (= to take away the dirty plates, etc. after a meal).
  - They started clearing a path through the dense jungle.
  - The work of clearing the debris is in its final stages.
  - She cleared a space on the sofa for him to sit down.
  - I had cleared my desk before I left.
  - clear A of B I cleared my desk of papers.
  - The streets had been cleared of snow.
  - clear B from/off A Clear all those papers off the desk.
  - The remains of the snow had been cleared from the streets.

### [click](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/click_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/klɪk/` · **US:** `/klɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cli/click/click__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cli/click/click__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a mouse click
  - It only took me a couple of clicks to find what I was looking for.
  - The clunky interface requires too many clicks to accomplish anything.
  - click on something a double click on the filename
  - at the click of something A vast amount of information now available at the click of a mouse.

### [click](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/click_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/klɪk/` · **US:** `/klɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cli/click/click__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cli/click/click__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Click here to add your opinion to the survey.
  - click something Click the OK button to start.
  - Just click the link below.
  - click on something I clicked on the link to the next page of the website.
  - click for something Click for more images.

### [client](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/client) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈklaɪənt/` · **US:** `/ˈklaɪənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cli/clien/client__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cli/clien/client__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She's a well-known lawyer with many famous clients.
  - potential/prospective clients
  - It is our job to act on behalf of the client.
  - We will advise the client on the best way to solve the problem.
  - Social workers must always consider the best interests of their clients.
  - Corporate clients usually want their work done much faster than private clients.

### [climb](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/climb_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/klaɪm/` · **US:** `/klaɪm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cli/climb/climb__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cli/climb/climb__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an exhausting climb
  - It's an hour's climb to the summit.
  - After a steep climb we reached a plateau.
  - climb up something We began our climb up the hill.

### [close](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/close2_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/kləʊs/` · **US:** `/kləʊs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/clo/close/close__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/clo/close/close__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - + adv./prep. They sat close together.
  - A second police car followed close behind.
  - They are edging closer to an agreement.
  - Don't come too close!
  - He moved closer so that he could hear her better.
  - I couldn't get close enough to see.
  - The days were growing colder as winter drew closer.
  - Stay close to me!
  - She held Tom close and pressed her cheek to his.

### [cloth](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cloth) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/klɒθ/` · **US:** `/klɔːθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/clo/cloth/cloth__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/clo/cloth/cloth__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - woollen/cotton/linen cloth
  - a piece of cloth
  - His bandages had been made from strips of cloth.
  - the cloth industry/trade
  - a cloth bag

### [clue](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/clue_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kluː/` · **US:** `/kluː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/clu/clue_/clue__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/clu/clue_/clue__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The burglar left no clues.
  - clue about something They are searching for clues about the kidnapping.
  - clue (as) to something The police think the CCTV recording may offer clues to the identity of the killer.

### [coach](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/coach_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəʊtʃ/` · **US:** `/kəʊtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coa/coach/coach__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coa/coach/coach__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - coach somebody He has coached the team for five years.
  - She has coached hundreds of young singers.
  - coach somebody for something Her father coached her for the Olympics.
  - coach somebody in something Youngsters will be coached in the basics of the game.
  - coach something He coaches basketball and soccer.

### [coal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/coal) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəʊl/` · **US:** `/kəʊl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coa/coal_/coal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coa/coal_/coal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I put more coal on the fire.
  - a lump of coal
  - coal mining
  - She sat by the coal fire in the kitchen.
  - Plans were being drawn up for the privatization of the coal industry.
  - These men had spent their lives breathing coal dust.

### [coin](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/coin_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kɔɪn/` · **US:** `/kɔɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coi/coin_/coin__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coi/coin_/coin__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - gold coins
  - a pound/euro/dollar coin
  - They flipped a coin to see who should go first.
  - You might as well toss a coin to decide.
  - A coin toss has decided the lucky winner.

### [collection](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/collection) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈlekʃn/` · **US:** `/kəˈlekʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/col/colle/collection__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/col/colle/collection__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He wanted to share his vast art collection with the world.
  - a stamp/coin/record collection
  - collection of something an extensive collection of war photographs
  - The painting comes from his private collection.

### [coloured](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/coloured_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌləd/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌlərd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/col/colou/coloured__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/col/colou/coloured__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - brightly coloured balloons
  - He uses ink and coloured pencils in his drawings.
  - The street was ablaze with coloured lights.
  - vases made of coloured glass
  - dark-coloured jeans
  - She was wearing a cream-coloured suit.
  - The words are written in a different-coloured ink.

### [combine](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/combine_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈbaɪn/` · **US:** `/kəmˈbaɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/combi/combine__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/combi/combine__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - combine to do something Hydrogen and oxygen combine to form water.
  - Several factors had combined to ruin our plans.
  - combine with something (to do something) Hydrogen combines with oxygen to form water.
  - Archaeological and historical evidence combine to create a picture of what life must have been like at that time.
  - combine something a style that combines elements of tap, ballet and modern dance
  - I like to travel and make films, and I'm now able to combine the two.
  - combine A with B Combine the eggs with a little flour.
  - combine A and B (together) Combine the eggs and the flour.
  - combine something into something Small units, such as words, can be combined into larger units, such as clauses.

### [comment](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/comment_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒment/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːment/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/comme/comment__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/comme/comment__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He refused to comment until after the trial.
  - She said she was unable to comment further.
  - comment on/upon something to comment on a case/report
  - I don't feel I can comment on their decision.
  - We were just commenting on how well you look.
  - He said nothing when asked to comment on the allegations.
  - comment about something She declined to comment about the current situation in the party.
  - comment (to somebody) that… A spokesperson commented that levels of carbon dioxide were very high.
  - comment (to somebody) + speech ‘Not his best performance,’ she commented to the woman sitting next to her.

### [commercial](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/commercial_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈmɜːʃl/` · **US:** `/kəˈmɜːrʃl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/comme/commercial__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/comme/commercial__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - residential and commercial properties
  - a commercial building/development
  - (especially North American English) commercial real estate
  - commercial activities
  - They have offices in the commercial heart of the city.
  - She is developing the commercial side of the organization.
  - a commercial vehicle (= one that is used for carrying goods or passengers who pay)

### [commercial](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/commercial_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈmɜːʃl/` · **US:** `/kəˈmɜːrʃl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/comme/commercial__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/comme/commercial__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a TV/television commercial
  - a radio commercial
  - a Republican campaign commercial
  - The marketing campaign is starting with four online commercials.
  - in a commercial actors in commercials
  - She began her career appearing in commercials.
  - commercial for something He did a number of commercials for major brands.

### [commit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/commit) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈmɪt/` · **US:** `/kəˈmɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/commi/commit__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/commi/commit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to commit a crime/an offence
  - to commit murder/fraud/adultery
  - to commit an act of violence/terrorism
  - to commit an error/a sin
  - to commit atrocities
  - appalling crimes committed against innocent children
  - Most crimes are committed by young men.
  - The men are being held on suspicion of conspiracy to commit armed robbery.

### [communication](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/communication) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəˌmjuːnɪˈkeɪʃn/` · **US:** `/kəˌmjuːnɪˈkeɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/commu/communication__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/commu/communication__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - communication between A and B Good communication between team leaders and members is essential.
  - communication with somebody attempts to improve communication with customers
  - The campaign will appear across all communication channels.
  - helping students develop their communication skills
  - non-verbal communication such as gestures or facial expressions
  - in communication We are in regular communication by email.

### [comparison](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/comparison) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈpærɪsn/` · **US:** `/kəmˈpærɪsn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compa/comparison__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compa/comparison__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - For Durkheim, comparison was the most important method of analysis in sociology.
  - comparison with somebody/something Comparison with other oil-producing countries is extremely interesting.
  - The two situations bear no comparison (= they are not at all similar).
  - for comparison I enclose the two plans for comparison.

### [competitive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/competitive) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈpetətɪv/` · **US:** `/kəmˈpetətɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compe/competitive__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compe/competitive__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - competitive games/sports
  - Graduates have to fight for jobs in an increasingly competitive market.
  - Those businesses are operating in an extremely competitive environment.
  - The company has succeeded in a highly competitive industry.

### [competitor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/competitor) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈpetɪtə(r)/` · **US:** `/kəmˈpetɪtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compe/competitor__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compe/competitor__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Over 200 competitors entered the race.
  - the youngest competitor in the event
  - the most successful South African competitor at the games
  - competitor for something There are six remaining competitors for the grand prize of $50 000.

### [complaint](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/complaint) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈpleɪnt/` · **US:** `/kəmˈpleɪnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compl/complaint__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compl/complaint__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a formal complaint
  - She said the police had immediately investigated the complaint.
  - complaint about somebody/something I'd like to make a complaint about the noise.
  - (formal) to file/lodge (= make) a complaint
  - The most common complaint is about poor service.
  - complaint of something The Tribunal upheld his complaint of discrimination.
  - complaint against somebody/something I believe you have a complaint against one of our nurses.
  - complaint to somebody They said their complaints to the police were often not acted on.
  - complaint from somebody Council bosses said they would respond to complaints from residents.
  - complaint from somebody about something We received a number of complaints from customers about the lack of parking facilities.
  - complaint by somebody ‘Complaints by patients are now at their lowest level,’ she said.
  - complaint (that…) a complaint that he had been unfairly treated
  - My only complaint is that the website is a little difficult to use.
  - We have a clear and effective complaints procedure.

### [complex](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/complex_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒmpleks/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːmpleks/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compl/complex__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compl/complex__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a complex problem/issue/process/system
  - a complex argument/subject
  - complex machinery
  - the complex structure of the human brain
  - Scientists need a better understanding of the complex interactions that cause hurricanes to intensify.
  - We live in an increasingly complex world.
  - a highly complex situation

### [concentrate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/concentrate_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒnsntreɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːnsntreɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconcentrate__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconcentrate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I can't concentrate with all that noise going on.
  - concentrate on something I struggled to concentrate on my job because I was worried about my son.
  - concentrate on doing something She tried to concentrate on reading her book but couldn't.
  - concentrate something Nothing concentrates the mind better than the knowledge that you could die tomorrow (= it makes you think very clearly).
  - concentrate something on something After 1926 she concentrated her energies on her medical practice.
  - concentrate something on doing something I decided to concentrate all my efforts on finding somewhere to live.

### [conclude](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/conclude) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈkluːd/` · **US:** `/kənˈkluːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconclude__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconclude__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - conclude something It is too early for us to conclude anything.
  - conclude something from something What do you conclude from that?
  - conclude (that)… The report concluded (that) the cheapest option was to close the laboratory.
  - conclude from something that… He concluded from their remarks that they were not in favour of the plan.
  - it is concluded that… It was concluded that the level of change necessary would be low.
  - + speech ‘So it should be safe to continue,’ he concluded.

### [conclusion](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/conclusion) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈkluːʒn/` · **US:** `/kənˈkluːʒn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconclusion__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconclusion__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We can safely draw some conclusions from our discussion.
  - He arrived at a different conclusion.
  - There is abundant evidence to support this conclusion.
  - Other studies have yielded the same conclusion.
  - The final section summarizes the conclusions of the study.
  - conclusion (that)… I've come to the conclusion that he's not the right person for the job.
  - New evidence might lead to the conclusion that we are wrong.
  - conclusion about/on somebody/something It is too soon to reach any conclusions about voting trends.

### [confident](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/confident) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒnfɪdənt/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːnfɪdənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfident__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfident__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was in a relaxed, confident mood.
  - Beneath his confident and charming exterior, lurked a mass of insecurities.
  - confident about something I was actually fairly confident about my chances.
  - confident about doing something The teacher wants the children to feel confident about asking questions when they don't understand.
  - confident in something He'd learned to be confident in his ability to handle anything life threw at him.

### [confirm](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/confirm) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈfɜːm/` · **US:** `/kənˈfɜːrm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfirm__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfirm__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - confirm something His guilty expression confirmed my suspicions.
  - to confirm a diagnosis/report
  - to confirm results/findings
  - Rumours of job losses were later confirmed.
  - We have yet to confirm the identities of the victims.
  - She said she could not confirm or deny the allegations.
  - The authorities refused to confirm any details.
  - confirm (that)… Police sources confirmed that ten people had been arrested at the march.
  - confirm to somebody/something that… A government official confirmed to the newspaper that Britain was pushing hard for an end to the arms embargo.
  - confirm what/when, etc… Can you confirm what happened?
  - it is confirmed that… It has been confirmed that an official complaint was made to the council.

### [confuse](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/confuse) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈfjuːz/` · **US:** `/kənˈfjuːz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfuse__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfuse__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - confuse somebody These two sets of statistics are guaranteed to confuse the public.
  - confuse somebody with something They confused me with conflicting accounts of what happened.

### [confused](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/confused) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈfjuːzd/` · **US:** `/kənˈfjuːzd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfused__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconfused__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'm confused—say all that again.
  - I'm getting really confused now. What has that got to do with it?
  - He was depressed and in a confused state of mind.
  - a confused look/expression
  - confused about something I was confused about my feelings for him.
  - People are confused about all the different labels on food these days.
  - confused by something She seemed confused by the question.

### [connection](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/connection) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈnekʃn/` · **US:** `/kəˈnekʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconn/xconnection__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconn/xconnection__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - connection between A and B Scientists have established a connection between cholesterol levels and heart disease.
  - connection with something a direct/close/strong connection with something
  - His resignation must have some connection with the recent scandal.
  - connection to something Consumers are more likely to buy something from a person or shop they feel a personal connection to.
  - How did you make the connection (= realize that there was a connection between two facts that did not seem to be related)?

### [consequence](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/consequence) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒnsɪkwəns/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːnsɪkwens/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsequence__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsequence__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Remember that actions have consequences.
  - to suffer/face the consequences of something
  - dire/disastrous/negative/adverse consequences
  - Have you considered the possible consequences?
  - Reform often produces unintended consequences.
  - They must be prepared to accept the consequences of their actions.
  - A White House spokesman said that civilian casualties were an inevitable consequence of war.
  - consequence for somebody/something This decision could have serious consequences for the industry.
  - as a consequence (of something) Two hundred people lost their jobs as a direct consequence of the merger.
  - with… consequences He drove too fast, with tragic consequences.

### [consist](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/consist) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsɪst/` · **US:** `/kənˈsɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsist__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsist__us_1.mp3)

### [consume](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/consume) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsjuːm/` · **US:** `/kənˈsuːm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsume__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsume__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The electricity industry consumes large amounts of fossil fuels.
  - 25 per cent of the world’s population consumes 80 per cent of the planet’s resources.

### [consumer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/consumer) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsjuːmə(r)/` · **US:** `/kənˈsuːmər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsumer__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsumer__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - consumer spending/demand
  - links between producers and consumers
  - Health-conscious consumers want more information about the food they buy.
  - a consumer society (= one where buying and selling is considered to be very important)
  - a consumer watchdog (= somebody whose job is to protect consumers' rights)
  - The consumer (= consumers as a group) is well served by competition.
  - This tool may be considered too expensive for the average consumer.
  - consumer of something consumers of news/electricity/healthcare
  - providers and consumers of public services
  - We need to educate consumers about this technology.

### [contact](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/contact_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒntækt/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːntækt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontact__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontact__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - contact with somebody I don't have much contact with my uncle.
  - We've been unable to establish radio contact with the pilot.
  - She's lost contact with (= no longer sees or writes to) her son.
  - I finally made contact with (= succeeded in speaking to or meeting) her in Paris.
  - contact between A and B There is little contact between the two organizations.
  - in contact We're no longer in regular contact.
  - in contact with somebody The Foreign Office is currently in close contact with the Indian authorities.
  - Have you kept in contact with any of your friends from college (= do you still see them or speak or write to them)?
  - The organization put me in contact with other people in a similar position (= gave me their addresses or phone numbers).
  - Anyone with any information about the incident is asked to get in contact with the police (= phone them or go to see them).
  - two people avoiding eye contact (= avoiding looking directly at each other)
  - Please ensure we have your contact number (= phone number) in case we need to reschedule.
  - I'll give you my contact details (= phone number, email address, etc.)
  - The society's website provides contact information.

### [contact](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/contact_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒntækt/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːntækt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontact__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontact__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I've been trying to contact you all day.
  - Witnesses to the accident are asked to contact the police.
  - Anyone interested is requested to contact a member of the committee.
  - contact somebody/something for something For further information, contact your local careers office.
  - contact somebody/something on something (British English) He can be contacted on 01865…
  - contact somebody/something at something (North American English) He can be contacted at 212…
  - contact somebody/something by something Anyone with tickets will be contacted by phone, letter or email and offered a full refund.

### [container](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/container) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈteɪnə(r)/` · **US:** `/kənˈteɪnər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontainer__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontainer__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a plastic container
  - in a container Food will last longer if kept in an airtight container.
  - container of something These women carry heavy containers of water over long distances.
  - container for something containers for nuclear materials

### [content](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/content1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒntent/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːntent/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontent__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He tipped the contents of the bag onto the table.
  - Fire has caused severe damage to the contents of the building.
  - She hadn't read the letter and so was unaware of its contents.
  - She picked up the glass and drank the contents.

### [continuous](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/continuous) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈtɪnjuəs/` · **US:** `/kənˈtɪnjuəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontinuous__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontinuous__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Recovery after the accident will be a continuous process that may take several months.
  - The organization aims to create a culture of continuous improvement .
  - a continuous stream of data
  - a continuous flow of knowledge, information and understanding
  - The rain has been almost continuous for weeks.
  - She was in continuous employment until the age of sixty-five.

### [contrast](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/contrast_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒntrɑːst/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːntræst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontrast__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontrast__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The two cities make an interesting contrast.
  - contrast between A and B There is an obvious contrast between the cultures of East and West.
  - in contrast In contrast, the south suffered very little hurricane damage.
  - in contrast to somebody/something The situation when we arrived was in marked contrast to the news reports.
  - The poverty of her childhood stands in total contrast to her life in Hollywood.
  - The company lost $7 million this quarter in contrast to a profit of $6.2 million a year earlier.
  - contrast with somebody/something to show a sharp/stark/striking contrast with something
  - contrast in something A wool jacket complements the silk trousers and provides an interesting contrast in texture.
  - by contrast When you look at their new system, ours seems very old-fashioned by contrast.
  - contrast of A and B Careful contrast of the two plans shows some important differences.

### [contrast](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/contrast_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈtrɑːst/` · **US:** `/kənˈtræst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontrast__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontrast__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - contrast A and B The poem contrasts youth and age.
  - Compare and contrast the two novels (= discuss how they are similar and how they are different).
  - contrast A with B It is interesting to contrast the British legal system with the American one.

### [convenient](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/convenient) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈviːniənt/` · **US:** `/kənˈviːniənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconv/xconvenient__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconv/xconvenient__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Fruit is a convenient source of vitamins and energy.
  - A bicycle is often more convenient than a car in towns.
  - I can't see him now—it isn't convenient.
  - I'll call back at a more convenient time.
  - Flying is the most convenient way to travel in such a large country as China.
  - (disapproving) He used his wife's birthday as a convenient excuse for not going to the meeting.
  - convenient for somebody/something He said meeting in Adelaide would be convenient for him as well.
  - When would be convenient for a visit?
  - it is convenient to do something It is very convenient to pay by credit card.
  - it is convenient for somebody to do something It's much more convenient for me to drive there.
  - convenient to do You'll find these meals quick and convenient to prepare.

### [convince](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/convince) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈvɪns/` · **US:** `/kənˈvɪns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconv/xconvince__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconv/xconvince__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - convince somebody/yourself Are the prime minister's assurances enough to convince the public?
  - I'd be very surprised if this argument convinces anybody.
  - to try/manage/fail to convince somebody/yourself
  - She has managed to convince even the sceptics.
  - convince somebody/yourself of something You'll need to convince them of your enthusiasm for the job.
  - convince somebody/yourself (that)… I’d convinced myself (that) I was right.
  - Will the president be able to convince voters that he deserves a second term?
  - The experience convinced him that Europe was on the brink of a revolution.

### [cool](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cool_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kuːl/` · **US:** `/kuːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coo/cool_/cool__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coo/cool_/cool__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Glass contracts as it cools.
  - Melt the chocolate and allow it to cool slightly.
  - cool something The cylinder is cooled by a jet of water.
  - The evening breeze cooled her face.

### [costume](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/costume) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒstjuːm/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːstuːm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cos/costu/costume__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cos/costu/costume__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an exhibition of Victorian costumes
  - She wore a traditional costume of bright pink silk.
  - in… costume a painting of a woman in Welsh costume
  - Many locals dressed in period costume (= typical of a particular time in history) for the celebrations.

### [cottage](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cottage) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒtɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːtɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cot/cotta/cottage__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cot/cotta/cottage__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a thatched cottage
  - (British English) a holiday cottage
  - in a cottage James lived in a charming country cottage with roses around the door.

### [cotton](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cotton_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒtn/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːtn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cot/cotto/cotton__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cot/cotto/cotton__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They grew cotton, tobacco, corn and fruit.
  - bales of cotton
  - cotton fields/plantations/plants

### [count](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/count_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kaʊnt/` · **US:** `/kaʊnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cou/count/count__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cou/count/count__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - If the election result is close, there will be a second count.
  - The bus driver did a quick count of the empty seats.
  - by somebody's count By my count there are eight tracks here that aren't on the band's three albums.

### [countryside](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/countryside) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌntrisaɪd/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌntrisaɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cou/count/countryside__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cou/count/countryside__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The surrounding countryside is windswept and rocky.
  - magnificent views over open countryside
  - the beautiful countryside of Wales
  - Everyone should enjoy the right of access to the countryside.
  - in the countryside the quieter pace of life in the countryside

### [court](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/court_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kɔːt/` · **US:** `/kɔːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cou/court/court__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cou/court/court__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the civil/criminal courts
  - Her lawyer made a statement outside the court.
  - in court Relatives of the dead girl were in court.
  - She will appear in court tomorrow.
  - to court They took their landlord to court for breaking the contract.
  - The case took five years to come to court (= to be heard by the court).
  - There wasn't enough evidence to bring the case to court (= start a trial).
  - out of court The case was settled out of court (= a decision was reached without a trial).
  - He won the court case and was awarded damages.
  - She can't pay her tax and is facing court action.
  - During the court hearing, the prosecutor said she would seek maximum prison sentences.

### [cover](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cover_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌvə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌvər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cov/cover/cover__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cov/cover/cover__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Brighten up your room with some colourful cushion covers.
  - cover for something a plastic waterproof cover for the pushchair

### [covered](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/covered) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌvəd/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌvərd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cov/cover/covered__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cov/cover/covered__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - His face was covered in blood.
  - I was completely covered in mud.
  - The walls were covered with pictures.

### [cream](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cream_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kriːm/` · **US:** `/kriːm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cre/cream/cream__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cre/cream/cream__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a cream linen suit
  - a cream carpet
  - The walls were cream and the ceiling was painted white.

### [criminal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/criminal_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkrɪmɪnl/` · **US:** `/ˈkrɪmɪnl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cri/crimi/criminal__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cri/crimi/criminal__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - criminal offences/activities
  - a criminal act punishable by up to five years in jail
  - They were charged with criminal damage (= the crime of damaging somebody's property deliberately).
  - She has been cleared of criminal negligence (= the illegal act of somebody failing to do something that they should do, with the result that somebody else is harmed).

### [cruel](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cruel_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkruːəl/` · **US:** `/ˈkruːəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cru/cruel/cruel__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cru/cruel/cruel__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He was known to be a cruel dictator.
  - cruel to somebody/something I can't stand people who are cruel to animals.
  - Her eyes were cruel and hard.
  - Sometimes you have to be cruel to be kind (= make somebody suffer because it will be good for them later).
  - an extremely cruel regime

### [cultural](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cultural) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌltʃərəl/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌltʃərəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cul/cultu/cultural__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cul/cultu/cultural__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Teachers need to be aware of cultural differences.
  - America's cultural diversity
  - a cultural institution/centre
  - cultural events
  - a cultural exchange between Japan and France
  - a time of rapid social and cultural change.
  - The industry needs a cultural shift to address the gender pay gap.
  - your cultural identity/background
  - His character represents the traditional cultural values of China.
  - Many immigrants preserve their cultural traditions.
  - the rich cultural history of New England

### [currency](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/currency) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌrənsi/` · **US:** `/ˈkɜːrənsi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cur/curre/currency__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cur/curre/currency__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - trading in foreign currencies
  - the single European currency
  - You'll need some cash in local currency but you can also use your credit card.

### [current](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/current_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌrənt/` · **US:** `/ˈkɜːrənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cur/curre/current__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cur/curre/current__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The necklace would be worth over $5 000 at current prices.
  - the current situation
  - Oil prices are expected to remain at current levels.
  - What's the budget for the current year?
  - Under the current system, the entire process takes about two weeks.

### [currently](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/currently) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌrəntli/` · **US:** `/ˈkɜːrəntli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cur/curre/currently__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cur/curre/currently__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The hourly charge is currently £35.
  - Currently, over 500 students are enrolled on the course.
  - All the options are currently available.
  - She's currently working on a book about painters.
  - This matter is currently being discussed.

### [curtain](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/curtain_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɜːtn/` · **US:** `/ˈkɜːrtn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cur/curta/curtain__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cur/curta/curtain__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to draw/pull/close the curtains (= to pull them across the window so they cover it)
  - She opened her curtains and looked out.
  - It was ten in the morning but the curtains were still drawn (= closed).
  - a pair of curtains
  - She looked up and saw a face peering through the curtains.

### [custom](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/custom_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌstəm/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌstəm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cus/custo/custom__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cus/custo/custom__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's a local custom.
  - an ancient custom
  - a burial/marriage custom
  - a tribal custom
  - She grew up with Chinese customs and traditions.
  - according to custom According to custom, one son inherited all the family property.
  - custom of doing something Widows observed the custom of wearing black.
  - it is the custom for somebody to do something It is the custom in that country for women to marry young.

### [cut](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cut_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kʌt/` · **US:** `/kʌt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cut/cut__/cut__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cut/cut__/cut__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - cuts and bruises on the face
  - Blood poured from the deep cut on his arm.


## D

### [daily](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/daily_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdeɪli/` · **US:** `/ˈdeɪli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dai/daily/daily__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dai/daily/daily__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The machines are inspected twice daily.
  - News stories are updated daily.
  - The temperature was recorded daily.

### [damage](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/damage_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdæmɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈdæmɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dam/damag/damage__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dam/damag/damage__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - serious/severe damage
  - The building suffered extensive damage by fire in 1925.
  - permanent/irreparable/irreversible damage
  - The accident caused no permanent damage.
  - She suffered minor brain damage at birth.
  - liver/nerve/kidney damage
  - flood/storm/smoke/water/fire damage
  - damage to something The earthquake caused damage to property estimated at $6 billion.
  - The storm didn't do much damage.
  - Industry could inflict further damage on the island's ecology.
  - It could take years to repair the damage.
  - Make sure you insure your camera against loss or damage.

### [damage](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/damage_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdæmɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈdæmɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dam/damag/damage__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dam/damag/damage__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The church was badly damaged by the 1997 earthquake.
  - Smoking seriously damages your health.
  - to be severely/heavily damaged in the fire
  - Our car was damaged beyond repair in the crash.
  - She fears the allegations could permanently damage her reputation.
  - She was psychologically damaged by her experiences.

### [deal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/deal_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/diːl/` · **US:** `/diːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dea/deal_/deal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dea/deal_/deal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to sign/strike/finalize/close a deal
  - to negotiate/broker/agree/reach a deal
  - She is travelling to New York to seal the deal (= conclude it).
  - a deal with somebody We did a deal with the management on overtime.
  - I'll make a deal with you—I'll work evenings if you'll work weekends.
  - (informal) He is trying to cut a deal (= make one) with the rebels.
  - The deal fell through (= no agreement was reached).
  - I got a good deal on the car (= bought it cheaply).
  - They were hoping for a better pay deal.
  - Both sides are willing to agree on a peace deal.
  - Listen. This is the deal (= this is what we have agreed and are going to do).

### [decade](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/decade) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈkeɪd/` · **US:** `/dɪˈkeɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dec/decad/decade__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dec/decad/decade__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the early decades of the nineteenth century
  - The nineties were a decade of rapid advances.
  - a career spanning five decades
  - over a decade He's changed a lot over the past decade.
  - for decades The war may be short but the environmental impacts could last for decades.

### [decorate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/decorate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdekəreɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈdekəreɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dec/decor/decorate__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dec/decor/decorate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - decorate something with something They decorated the room with flowers and balloons.
  - decorate something The cake was decorated to look like a car.
  - The family gets together on Christmas Eve to decorate the tree.

### [deep](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/deep_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/diːp/` · **US:** `/diːp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dee/deep_/deep__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dee/deep_/deep__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Dig deeper!
  - + adv./prep. The miners were trapped deep underground.
  - whales that feed deep beneath the waves
  - deep in the forest
  - He stood with his hands deep in his pockets.
  - He gazed deep into her eyes.
  - They sat and talked deep into the night (= until very late).
  - The plane landed deep behind enemy lines.
  - (figurative) to sink deeper into poverty/debt

### [define](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/define) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈfaɪn/` · **US:** `/dɪˈfaɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/def/defin/define__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/def/defin/define__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - define something The term ‘normal’ is difficult to define.
  - define something as something Life imprisonment is defined as 60 years under state law.

### [definite](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/definite_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdefɪnət/` · **US:** `/ˈdefɪnət/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/def/defin/definite__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/def/defin/definite__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Can you give me a definite answer by tomorrow?
  - No definite conclusions can be drawn from this study.
  - They have very definite ideas on how to bring up children.
  - definite that… Is it definite that he's leaving?
  - I've heard rumours, but nothing definite.
  - for definite I'm not sure—I can find out for definite if you like.
  - That's definite then, is it?

### [definition](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/definition) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌdefɪˈnɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌdefɪˈnɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/def/defin/definition__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/def/defin/definition__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The dictionary provides clear, simple definitions.
  - definition of something the dictionary definition of this term
  - a precise/strict definition of a word
  - We need a good working definition of ‘pollution’.

### [deliver](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/deliver) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈlɪvə(r)/` · **US:** `/dɪˈlɪvər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/del/deliv/deliver__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/del/deliv/deliver__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - deliver something I get my food delivered from the supermarket to save time.
  - deliver something to somebody/something Leaflets have been delivered to every household.
  - deliver (to somebody/something) We promise to deliver within 48 hours.
  - We deliver to our stores seven days a week.

### [departure](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/departure) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈpɑːtʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/dɪˈpɑːrtʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dep/depar/departure__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dep/depar/departure__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - His sudden departure threw the office into chaos.
  - rumours of her imminent/impending departure
  - departure from… They had received no news of him since his departure from the island.
  - departure for… The day of their departure for London was growing closer.
  - departure to… She postponed her departure to Scotland.
  - before departure Flights should be confirmed 48 hours before departure.

### [despite](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/despite) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈspaɪt/` · **US:** `/dɪˈspaɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/des/despi/despite__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/des/despi/despite__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Her voice was shaking despite all her efforts to control it.
  - Despite applying for hundreds of jobs, he is still out of work.
  - She was good at physics despite the fact that she found it boring.

### [destination](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/destination_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌdestɪˈneɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌdestɪˈneɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/des/desti/destination__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/des/desti/destination__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - popular tourist/holiday destinations like the Bahamas
  - to arrive at/reach your destination
  - Our luggage was checked all the way through to our final destination.

### [determine](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/determine) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈtɜːmɪn/` · **US:** `/dɪˈtɜːrmɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/det/deter/determine__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/det/deter/determine__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - determine something An inquiry was set up to determine the cause of the accident.
  - Try to determine the extent of the problem.
  - determine what, whether, etc… We set out to determine exactly what happened that night.
  - It is difficult to determine when drama first appeared in India.
  - it is determined that… It was determined that she had died of natural causes.

### [determined](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/determined) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈtɜːmɪnd/` · **US:** `/dɪˈtɜːrmɪnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/det/deter/determined__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/det/deter/determined__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The opposition to her plan made her more determined than ever.
  - determined to do something I'm determined to succeed.
  - determined that… They were quite determined that he wasn't going to do it.

### [development](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/development) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈveləpmənt/` · **US:** `/dɪˈveləpmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dev/devel/development__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dev/devel/development__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a baby’s development in the womb
  - This is a perfectly normal stage of development.
  - the development of basic skills such as literacy and numeracy
  - The company can offer a number of opportunities for career development.

### [diagram](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/diagram_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdaɪəɡræm/` · **US:** `/ˈdaɪəɡræm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dia/diagr/diagram__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dia/diagr/diagram__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The results are shown in diagram 2.
  - diagram of something a diagram of the wiring system
  - He drew a diagram of the camp.

### [diamond](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/diamond) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdaɪmənd/` · **US:** `/ˈdaɪmənd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dia/diamo/diamond__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dia/diamo/diamond__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a diamond ring/necklace
  - She was wearing her diamonds (= jewellery with diamonds in it).
  - a six carat diamond
  - a diamond mine
  - The lights shone like diamonds.

### [difficulty](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/difficulty) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɪfɪkəlti/` · **US:** `/ˈdɪfɪkəlti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dif/diffi/difficulty__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dif/diffi/difficulty__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the difficulties of English syntax
  - a patient with breathing difficulties
  - They encountered numerous difficulties while making the film.
  - She had been experiencing technical difficulties.
  - I think we've managed to overcome most of the practical difficulties.
  - I know the kinds of difficulties faced by parents and teachers.
  - difficulties with something We've run into difficulties with the new project.
  - difficulty with something I'm having difficulty with the engine.
  - in difficulties He was often in financial difficulties.
  - in difficulty He got into difficulty while swimming and had to be rescued.
  - It was a time fraught with difficulties and frustration.

### [direct](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/direct_3) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/daɪˈrekt/` · **US:** `/daɪˈrekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dir/direc/direct__gb_7.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dir/direc/direct__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - direct to something We flew direct to Hong Kong.
  - The 10.40 goes direct to Leeds.
  - You can now fly direct from Birmingham airport.

### [direct](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/direct_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/daɪˈrekt/` · **US:** `/daɪˈrekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dir/direc/direct__gb_7.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dir/direc/direct__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
