  - fold something up He folded the map up and put it in his pocket.
  - fold something down The blankets had been folded down.
  - fold something back Don't fold back pages to mark your place in a book.
  - fold something over Fold the dough over on top of itself.

### [folk](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/folk_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/fəʊk/` · **US:** `/fəʊk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fol/folk_/folk__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fol/folk_/folk__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We visited an exhibition of folk art.
  - a dance that originated in English folk tradition

### [folk](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/folk_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fəʊk/` · **US:** `/fəʊk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fol/folk_/folk__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fol/folk_/folk__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ordinary working-class folk
  - I'd like a job working with old folk or kids.
  - the folks back home (= from the place where you come from)
  - Folks say that he is a hard man.
  - Like most folks, I enjoy a decent western.

### [following](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/following_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɒləʊɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈfɑːləʊɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fol/follo/following__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fol/follo/following__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The following is a summary of events.
  - The following have been chosen to take part: Watts, Hodges and Lennox.
  - Your report must include the following: the artist's name and the title of the song.

### [force](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/force_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fɔːs/` · **US:** `/fɔːrs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/for/force/force__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/for/force/force__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The release of the hostages could not be achieved without the use of force.
  - If persuasion doesn't work, they use force.
  - The government has relied on brute force to respond to public discontent.
  - by force The rioters were taken away by force.
  - The empire was held together by military force.
  - plans to seize power by force of arms (= by military force)

### [force](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/force_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/fɔːs/` · **US:** `/fɔːrs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/for/force/force__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/for/force/force__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - be forced into doing something The President was forced into resigning.
  - be forced to do something The President was forced to resign.
  - I was forced to take a taxi because the last bus had left.
  - Massoud was forced to flee for his life.
  - force somebody/yourself to do something She forced herself to be polite to them.
  - be forced into something He was forced into exile with his family.
  - force somebody into something Ill health forced him into early retirement.
  - be forced out of something Residents were forced out of their homes.
  - force somebody out of something The high cost of insurance is forcing some out of business.
  - force somebody He didn't force me—I wanted to go.
  - force yourself (informal, humorous) ‘I shouldn't really have any more.’ ‘Go on—force yourself!’
  - force something Public pressure eventually forced a change in the government's position.

### [forever](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/forever) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/fərˈevə(r)/` · **US:** `/fərˈevər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/for/forev/forever__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/for/forev/forever__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'll love you forever!
  - After her death, their lives changed forever.
  - Just keep telling yourself that it won't last forever.
  - The local dairy industry will soon be lost forever.
  - The final death toll may forever remain a mystery.

### [frame](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/frame_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/freɪm/` · **US:** `/freɪm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fra/frame/frame__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fra/frame/frame__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a picture/photo frame
  - aluminium window frames
  - I'm going to paint the door frame white.
  - She leaned against the frame of the door.
  - in a frame pictures in gold frames

### [frame](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/frame_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/freɪm/` · **US:** `/freɪm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fra/frame/frame__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fra/frame/frame__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - be framed The photograph had been framed.
  - He was presented with a framed certificate.
  - (be) framed in something mirrors framed in silver and gold

### [freeze](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/freeze_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/friːz/` · **US:** `/friːz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fre/freez/freeze__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fre/freez/freeze__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Water freezes at 0°C.
  - It's so cold that even the river has frozen.
  - freeze something The cold weather had frozen the ground.
  - freeze + adj. The clothes froze solid on the washing line.
  - freeze something + adj. The frost had frozen the ground hard.
  - Last year's cold spell froze the lake solid.

### [frequently](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/frequently) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfriːkwəntli/` · **US:** `/ˈfriːkwəntli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fre/frequ/frequently__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fre/frequ/frequently__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Buses run frequently between the city and the airport.
  - some of the most frequently asked questions about the internet
  - It was a word he used frequently.
  - Her father, a pilot, was frequently absent from home.

### [friendship](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/friendship) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfrendʃɪp/` · **US:** `/ˈfrendʃɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fri/frien/friendship__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fri/frien/friendship__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They formed a close friendship at college.
  - a lasting/lifelong friendship
  - to forge/develop/cultivate a friendship
  - friendship with somebody He seemed to have already struck up (= begun) a friendship with Jo.
  - friendship between A and B It's the story of an extraordinary friendship between a boy and a seal.

### [frighten](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/frighten) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfraɪtn/` · **US:** `/ˈfraɪtn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fri/frigh/frighten__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fri/frigh/frighten__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - frighten (somebody) Sorry, I didn't mean to frighten you.
  - (informal) He used to frighten me to death (= a lot), zooming about on his go-cart.
  - She's not easily frightened.
  - She doesn't frighten easily (= it is not easy to make her afraid).
  - it frightens somebody to think, see, hear, etc… It really frightens me to think how this might have ended.
  - it frightens somebody that… It frightened him that she could see so easily into his private thoughts.

### [frightened](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/frightened) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfraɪtnd/` · **US:** `/ˈfraɪtnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fri/frigh/frightened__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fri/frigh/frightened__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a frightened child
  - Don't be frightened.
  - He sounded frightened.
  - frightened of something What are you frightened of?
  - frightened of doing something I'm frightened of walking home alone in the dark.
  - frightened about something I was nervous and frightened about the future.
  - frightened about doing something She said she was frightened about having the baby.
  - frightened to do something I'm too frightened to ask him now.
  - frightened that… She was frightened that the plane would crash.
  - frightened for somebody I'm frightened for him (= that he will be hurt, etc.).
  - (informal) I'd never do that. I'd be frightened to death.

### [frightening](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/frightening) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfraɪtnɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈfraɪtnɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fri/frigh/frightening__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fri/frigh/frightening__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a frightening experience/prospect/thought
  - a truly frightening horror movie
  - The noise was frightening.
  - it is frightening to think, see, hear, etc… It's frightening to think it could happen again.
  - frightening to somebody It was all very frightening to a small boy.
  - frightening for somebody This is extremely frightening for elderly people.

### [frozen](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/frozen_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfrəʊzn/` · **US:** `/ˈfrəʊzn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fro/froze/frozen__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fro/froze/frozen__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - frozen peas/fish/pizza
  - frozen yogurt/desserts
  - a major producer of canned and frozen foods

### [fry](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fry_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/fraɪ/` · **US:** `/fraɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fry/fry__/fry__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fry/fry__/fry__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - fry (something) fried fish
  - We had fried chicken for dinner.
  - the smell of bacon frying
  - fry something in something Fry the onions gently in oil.

### [fuel](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fuel_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfjuːəl/` · **US:** `/ˈfjuːəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fue/fuel_/fuel__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fue/fuel_/fuel__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - diesel/jet/rocket fuel
  - Most of the houses are heated with solid fuel (= wood, coal, etc.).
  - The plant reprocesses spent fuel from nuclear power stations.
  - The car has a 65-litre fuel tank.
  - a car with high fuel consumption
  - New engine designs are improving fuel efficiency.
  - We aim to cut emissions and boost fuel economy.
  - We use fuel oil to heat our house.
  - steadily rising fuel prices
  - Domestic fuel bills are set to rise again in the autumn.

### [function](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/function_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfʌŋkʃn/` · **US:** `/ˈfʌŋkʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fun/funct/function__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fun/funct/function__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The club serves a useful function as a meeting place.
  - to fulfil/perform a function
  - What is your function in the department?
  - bodily functions (= for example eating, sex, using the toilet)
  - The function of the heart is to pump blood through the body.
  - This design aims for harmony of form and function.

### [fur](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fur) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fɜː(r)/` · **US:** `/fɜːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fur/fur__/fur__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fur/fur__/fur__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The cat carefully licked its fur.
  - She stroked the soft fur on the dog's back.

### [further](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/further_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɜːðə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈfɜːrðər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fur/furth/further__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fur/furth/further__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We had walked a bit further than I had realized.
  - I'm too tired to go any further.
  - further north/south/east/west
  - Two miles further on we came to a small town.
  - The hospital is further down the road.
  - Can you stand a bit further away?


## G

### [garage](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/garage_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɡærɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ɡəˈrɑːdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gar/garag/garage__gb_6.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gar/garag/garage__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a double garage (= one for two cars)
  - a house with a detached/an attached/an integral garage
  - a bus garage
  - an underground garage (= for example under an office building)
  - in a garage Don't forget to put the car in the garage.
  - Did you shut the garage door?

### [gather](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/gather) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɡæðə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈɡæðər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gat/gathe/gather__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gat/gathe/gather__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A crowd soon gathered.
  - + adv./prep. His supporters gathered in the main square.
  - Can you all gather round? I've got something to tell you.
  - The whole family gathered together at Ray's home.
  - be gathered + adv./prep. They were all gathered round the TV.
  - A large crowd was gathered outside the studio.
  - The kids were gathered together in one room.

### [generally](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/generally) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdʒenrəli/` · **US:** `/ˈdʒenrəli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gen/gener/generally__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gen/gener/generally__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The initiative was generally considered a success.
  - He is generally regarded as the most important American painter of his generation.
  - It was not until 1908 that Freud's importance began to be generally recognized.
  - The plan was generally welcomed.
  - It is generally accepted/agreed/believed that…
  - The new drug will be generally available from January.
  - He was a generally unpopular choice for captain.

### [generation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/generation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌdʒenəˈreɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌdʒenəˈreɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gen/gener/generation__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gen/gener/generation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the younger/older generation
  - My generation have grown up with the internet.
  - I often wonder what future generations will make of our efforts.
  - The hopes and dreams of youth today are different from those of previous generations.
  - generation of somebody Her books have delighted generations of children.

### [generous](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/generous) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdʒenərəs/` · **US:** `/ˈdʒenərəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gen/gener/generous__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gen/gener/generous__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The gallery was named after its most generous benefactor.
  - a generous donation/gift/offer
  - generous with something You've been incredibly generous with your time.
  - generous to somebody He was generous to his friends and family.
  - generous in something She was generous in her praise for her co-stars.
  - generous in doing something They were very generous in giving help
  - it is generous of somebody Thank you for your donation. It was very generous of you.
  - it is generous of somebody to do something It was extremely generous of him to offer to pay for us both.

### [gentle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/gentle) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdʒentl/` · **US:** `/ˈdʒentl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gen/gentl/gentle__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gen/gentl/gentle__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a kind and gentle man
  - Terry was a gentle soul.
  - He looks scary but he's really a gentle giant.
  - She was the gentlest of nurses.
  - a gentle voice/laugh/touch
  - a gentle reminder that your next payment is due on Friday
  - He lived in a gentler age than ours.
  - gentle with somebody/something Be gentle with her!

### [gentleman](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/gentleman) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdʒentlmən/` · **US:** `/ˈdʒentlmən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gen/gentl/gentlemen__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gen/gentl/gentleman__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You acted like a true gentleman.
  - Thank you—you're a real gentleman.
  - He was always the perfect gentleman.
  - He's no gentleman!
  - He was too much of a gentleman to ask them for any money.

### [ghost](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ghost_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡəʊst/` · **US:** `/ɡəʊst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gho/ghost/ghost__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gho/ghost/ghost__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Do you believe in ghosts (= believe that they exist)?
  - ghost of somebody The ghost of her father had come back to haunt her.
  - He looked as if he had seen a ghost (= looked very frightened)
  - The ghost hunters have so far found nothing.

### [giant](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/giant_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdʒaɪənt/` · **US:** `/ˈdʒaɪənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gia/giant/giant__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gia/giant/giant__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The match was shown on a giant screen outside the town hall.
  - a giant step towards achieving independence
  - a giant squid
  - a giant-size box of tissues

### [giant](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/giant_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdʒaɪənt/` · **US:** `/ˈdʒaɪənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gia/giant/giant__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gia/giant/giant__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - tales of giants and ogres

### [glad](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/glad) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɡlæd/` · **US:** `/ɡlæd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gla/glad_/glad__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gla/glad_/glad__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘I passed the test!’ ‘I’m so glad.’
  - She was glad when the meeting was over.
  - glad about something ‘He doesn't need the pills any more.’ ‘I'm glad about that.’
  - glad to know, hear, see… I'm glad to hear you're feeling better.
  - glad (that)… I’m glad (that) you’re feeling better.
  - He was glad he'd come.
  - I'm just glad you’re safe!
  - glad to do something I've never been so glad to see anyone in my life!
  - I'm glad to meet you. I've heard a lot about you.
  - He talked so much that they were really glad to see the back of him (= when he left).
  - glad for somebody I'm so glad for him because he's such a nice lad.

### [global](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/global) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɡləʊbl/` · **US:** `/ˈɡləʊbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/glo/globa/global__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/glo/globa/global__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This year the global economy will grow by about 4 per cent.
  - a global financial/economic crisis
  - the struggle to stay competitive in global markets
  - the country's ability to compete on a global scale
  - global trade/capitalism
  - the global war on terror
  - the global ban on commercial whaling
  - a global network of 64 national, regional and international organizations

### [glove](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/glove) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡlʌv/` · **US:** `/ɡlʌv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/glo/glove/glove__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/glo/glove/glove__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a pair of gloves
  - rubber/latex gloves
  - gardening/boxing gloves
  - The burglar must have been wearing gloves.
  - She peeled off her glove to reveal a wedding ring.

### [go](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/go_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡəʊ/` · **US:** `/ɡəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/go_/go__g/go__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/go_/go__u/go__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Whose go is it?
  - It's your go.
  - ‘How much is it to play?’ ‘It's 50p a go.’
  - go on something Can I have a go on your new bike?

### [goods](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/goods) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡʊdz/` · **US:** `/ɡʊdz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/goo/goods/goods__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/goo/goods/goods__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to produce/buy/sell goods
  - manufactured/imported goods
  - luxury goods
  - electrical/sporting goods
  - cheap/expensive goods
  - leather/cotton goods
  - tax on goods and services
  - increases in the prices of goods

### [grade](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/grade_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡreɪd/` · **US:** `/ɡreɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gra/grade/grade__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gra/grade/grade__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - (British English) She got good grades in her exams.
  - (North American English) She got good grades on her exams.
  - 70 per cent of students achieved Grade C or above.

### [graduate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/graduate_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɡrædʒuət/` · **US:** `/ˈɡrædʒuət/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gra/gradu/graduate__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gra/gradu/graduate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - job prospects for graduates
  - a university/college graduate
  - a recent graduate from Coventry University
  - an Oxford/a Yale graduate
  - graduate of something a graduate of Oxford/Yale
  - engineering/science graduates
  - graduate in something a graduate in history
  - a graduate student
  - (North American English) graduate programs/degrees/studies

### [graduate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/graduate_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɡrædʒueɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈɡrædʒueɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gra/gradu/graduate__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gra/gradu/graduate__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She taught in France after she graduated.
  - graduate in something Only three students graduated in Czech studies last year.
  - graduate from something She graduated from Harvard this year.
  - graduate with something He graduated with a BA in English in 2018.
  - He graduated from York with a degree in Psychology.
  - graduate something (North American English) She graduated college last year.

### [grain](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/grain) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡreɪn/` · **US:** `/ɡreɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gra/grain/grain__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gra/grain/grain__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Russia sold 12 million tons of grain abroad last year.
  - America’s grain exports
  - The journal reports that eating whole grains protects against diabetes.
  - grain of something a few grains of rice

### [grateful](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/grateful) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɡreɪtfl/` · **US:** `/ˈɡreɪtfl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gra/grate/grateful__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gra/grate/grateful__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Thank you for doing this. I really am so grateful.
  - grateful to somebody We are very grateful to all those who contributed.
  - grateful for something She said she was grateful for the support shown by the community.
  - grateful to somebody for something I am extremely grateful to all the teachers for their help.
  - grateful to do something She seems to think I should be grateful to have a job at all.
  - grateful (that…) He was grateful that she didn't tell his parents about the incident.
  - Grateful thanks are due to the following people for their help…
  - Kate gave him a grateful smile.

### [growth](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/growth) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡrəʊθ/` · **US:** `/ɡrəʊθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gro/growt/growth__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gro/growt/growth__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Lack of water will stunt the plant's growth.
  - Remove dead leaves to encourage new growth.
  - nutrients for plant growth
  - a concern with personal (= mental and emotional) growth and development
  - It may be the most effective way to inhibit the growth of cancer cells
  - growth hormones (= that make somebody/something grow faster)

### [guard](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/guard_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡɑːd/` · **US:** `/ɡɑːrd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gua/guard/guard__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gua/guard/guard__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - prison/border guards
  - The building is protected by armed guards.
  - The prisoner slipped past the guards on the gate and escaped.
  - A guard was posted outside the building.

### [guard](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/guard_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɡɑːd/` · **US:** `/ɡɑːrd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gua/guard/guard__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gua/guard/guard__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Armed officers guarded the entrance.
  - The dog was guarding its owner's luggage.
  - political leaders guarded by the police
  - You can't get in; the whole place is guarded.
  - (figurative) The recipe is a closely guarded secret.

### [guilty](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/guilty) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɡɪlti/` · **US:** `/ˈɡɪlti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gui/guilt/guilty__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gui/guilt/guilty__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Matt and Chrissy both looked equally guilty.
  - John had a guilty look on his face.
  - I had a guilty conscience and could not sleep.
  - guilty about doing something I feel very guilty about leaving her.
  - guilty about something parents who feel guilty about the small amount of time they spend with their children
  - guilty that… I feel almost guilty that so many good things are happening to us.


## H

### [hand](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hand_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/hænd/` · **US:** `/hænd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/han/hand_/hand__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/han/hand_/hand__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - hand something to somebody She handed the letter to me.
  - hand somebody something She handed me the letter.

### [hang](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hang_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/hæŋ/` · **US:** `/hæŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/han/hang_/hang__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/han/hang_/hang__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - hang something + adv./prep. Hang your coat on the hook.
  - Activists hung a banner from the roof of the building.
  - hang something up Shall I hang your coat up?
  - hang something (out) (British English) Have you hung out the washing?
  - (North American English) Have you hung the wash?
  - hang adv./prep. There were several expensive suits hanging in the wardrobe.
  - Clothes hung from hooks on the walls.
  - His school bag was still hanging on the back of the door.

### [happiness](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/happiness) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhæpinəs/` · **US:** `/ˈhæpinəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hap/happi/happiness__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hap/happi/happiness__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to find true happiness
  - Fame did not bring her happiness.
  - the pursuit of happiness
  - Her eyes shone with happiness.
  - Their grandchildren are a constant source of happiness.

### [hardly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hardly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhɑːdli/` · **US:** `/ˈhɑːrdli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/har/hardl/hardly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/har/hardl/hardly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There's hardly any tea left.
  - Hardly anyone has bothered to reply.
  - She hardly ever calls me (= almost never).
  - We hardly know each other.
  - I had hardly seen my father since that day.
  - It hardly seems fair to put all the blame on him.
  - Hardly a day goes by without my thinking of her (= I think of her almost every day).
  - At the time, I hardly spoke any French.
  - There was hardly a cloud in the sky.

### [hate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hate_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/heɪt/` · **US:** `/heɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hat/hate_/hate__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hat/hate_/hate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She gave him a look of real hate.
  - a strange relationship built on love and hate
  - hate for somebody/something She was full of hate for the people who had betrayed her.
  - a hate campaign (= cruel comments made about somebody over a period of time in order to damage their reputation)

### [head](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/head_3) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/hed/` · **US:** `/hed/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hea/head_/head__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hea/head_/head__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Where are we heading?
  - Where are you two headed?
  - Let's head back home.
  - They woke early and headed off for breakfast.
  - She headed for the door.
  - We headed north.
  - Head towards the mall.
  - The boat was heading out to sea.
  - (figurative) Can you forecast where the economy is heading?

### [headline](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/headline_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhedlaɪn/` · **US:** `/ˈhedlaɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hea/headl/headline__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hea/headl/headline__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a newspaper headline
  - under a headline They ran the story under the headline ‘Home at last!’.
  - in the headlines The scandal was in the headlines for several days.
  - headline about somebody/something an alarming headline about possible terrorist attacks

### [heating](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/heating) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhiːtɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈhiːtɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hea/heati/heating__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hea/heati/heating__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to put/turn the heating on
  - Who turned the heating off?
  - What type of heating do you have?
  - underfloor heating
  - a solar/gas-fired heating system
  - heating bills/costs
  - heating oil

### [heavily](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/heavily) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhevɪli/` · **US:** `/ˈhevɪli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hea/heavi/heavily__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hea/heavi/heavily__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It was raining heavily.
  - to drink heavily
  - to be bleeding heavily
  - to be heavily taxed
  - heavily armed police (= carrying a lot of weapons)
  - a heavily pregnant woman (= one whose baby is nearly ready to be born)
  - They are both heavily involved in politics.
  - He relies heavily on his parents.
  - She has been heavily criticized in the press.
  - Their music is heavily influenced by heavy metal and punk rock.
  - Two Swedish companies invested heavily in the Russian energy business.
  - These companies depend heavily on government support.
  - The country's economy is heavily dependent on foreign investment and aid.
  - The book draws heavily on case studies in Ethiopia.

### [helicopter](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/helicopter) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhelɪkɒptə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈhelɪkɑːptər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hel/helic/helicopter__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hel/helic/helicopter__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a police/rescue helicopter
  - an attack helicopter
  - by helicopter He was rushed to the hospital by helicopter.
  - a helicopter pilot
  - a helicopter gunship

### [highlight](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/highlight_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhaɪlaɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈhaɪlaɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hig/highl/highlight__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hig/highl/highlight__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - One of the highlights of the trip was seeing the Taj Mahal.
  - The highlights of the match will be shown later this evening.
  - My dance class is the highlight of my week.
  - The highlight of the evening was a violin solo by five-year-old Mia.

### [highlight](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/highlight_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhaɪlaɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈhaɪlaɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hig/highl/highlight__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hig/highl/highlight__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The report highlighted the importance of exercise to maintain a healthy body.
  - Students highlighted the need for better communication between staff.
  - to highlight the major problems/issues
  - This study highlights the fact that couples are keen to assist others.

### [highly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/highly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhaɪli/` · **US:** `/ˈhaɪli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hig/highl/highly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hig/highl/highly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It is highly unlikely that she'll be late.
  - She had a highly successful career as a portrait painter.
  - The US car market is highly competitive.
  - highly critical/sensitive
  - a highly effective design
  - His choice of subject is highly significant.

### [hire](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hire_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhaɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈhaɪər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hir/hire_/hire__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hir/hire_/hire__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was hired three years ago.
  - The firm has a proud record of hiring disabled people.
  - He does the hiring and firing in our company.
  - We’re not hiring right now.

### [historic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/historic) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/hɪˈstɒrɪk/` · **US:** `/hɪˈstɔːrɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/his/histo/historic__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/his/histo/historic__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the restoration of historic buildings
  - a historic monument/landmark
  - Take a tour of historic sites in the old city.
  - Visit historic Chester.
  - It was a great fight, a historic moment in Irish sport.
  - Murals depict the region's historic events.
  - a truly historic occasion
  - The area is of special historic interest.
  - The party has won a historic victory at the polls.

### [historical](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/historical) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/hɪˈstɒrɪkl/` · **US:** `/hɪˈstɔːrɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/his/histo/historical__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/his/histo/historical__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You must place these events in their historical context.
  - stories based on historical fact
  - This book provides a historical perspective.
  - one of the greatest historical figures of all time, Alexander the Great

### [honest](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/honest) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒnɪst/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːnɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hon/hones/honest__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hon/hones/honest__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an honest man/woman
  - They were honest, hard-working people, trying to farm on their own two feet.

### [horrible](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/horrible) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhɒrəbl/` · **US:** `/ˈhɔːrəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hor/horri/horrible__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hor/horri/horrible__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What horrible weather!
  - The coffee tasted absolutely horrible.
  - a horrible mess/smell/noise
  - I've got a horrible feeling she lied to us.
  - I feel horrible about what happened.
  - He looks horrible with that new haircut.
  - He admits that he made a horrible mistake when he left her.
  - it is horrible to do something It was horrible to have to leave my family behind.
  - it is horrible doing something It was horrible sitting there all on my own.

### [horror](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/horror) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhɒrə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈhɔːrər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hor/horro/horror__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hor/horro/horror__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The thought of being left alone filled her with horror.
  - in horror People watched in horror as the plane crashed to the ground.
  - with horror She realized with horror that somebody had broken into her house.
  - to somebody's horror To his horror, he realized that he couldn't escape (= it upset him very much).
  - horror at something He expressed his horror at the idea that he was in some way to blame.
  - She recoiled in horror at the sight of an enormous spider.
  - horror at doing something She revealed her horror at discovering the truth about her husband.

### [host](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/host_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/həʊst/` · **US:** `/həʊst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hos/host_/host__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hos/host_/host__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Ian, our host, introduced us to the other guests.
  - Sonja stayed with an Argentine host family.

### [hunt](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hunt_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/hʌnt/` · **US:** `/hʌnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hun/hunt_/hunt__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hun/hunt_/hunt__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Lions sometimes hunt alone.
  - hunt something Whales are still being hunted and killed in the Arctic.
  - hunt for something Cavemen had to hunt for their food.
  - They watched the bald eagles hunting for fish.
  - hunt something for something The animals are hunted for their fur.
  - Turtles are illegally hunted for their eggs, meat and shells.

### [hurricane](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hurricane) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhʌrɪkən/` · **US:** `/ˈhɜːrəkeɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hur/hurri/hurricane__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hur/hurri/hurricane__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A powerful hurricane hit the Florida coast.
  - Hurricane Sandy devastated the area.
  - The hurricane season does not end until November.
  - hurricane victims
  - hurricane-force winds
  - The proceeds will go to hurricane relief.

### [hurry](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hurry_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhʌri/` · **US:** `/ˈhɜːri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hur/hurry/hurry__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hur/hurry/hurry__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Take your time—there's no hurry.
  - What's the hurry? The train doesn't leave for an hour.
  - in your hurry to do something In my hurry to leave, I forgot my passport.

### [hurry](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hurry_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhʌri/` · **US:** `/ˈhɜːri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hur/hurry/hurry__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hur/hurry/hurry__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You'll have to hurry if you want to catch that train.
  - We need to hurry or we'll be late for school.
  - hurry to do something The kids hurried to open their presents.


## I

### [identity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/identity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/aɪˈdentəti/` · **US:** `/aɪˈdentəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ide/ident/identity__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ide/ident/identity__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The police are trying to discover the identity of the killer.
  - to establish/reveal/confirm the identity of somebody/something
  - Their names have been changed for the purposes of this article to protect their identities.
  - Their identities were kept secret.
  - Do you have any proof of identity?
  - The thief used a false identity.
  - He falls in love with her but can't reveal his true identity.
  - Each object in the collection has a unique identity number.
  - She went through an identity crisis in her teens (= was not sure of who she was or of her place in society).

### [ignore](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ignore) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪɡˈnɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/ɪɡˈnɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ign/ignor/ignore__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ign/ignor/ignore__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He ignored all the ‘No Smoking’ signs and lit up a cigarette.
  - I made a suggestion but they chose to ignore it.
  - We cannot afford to ignore their advice.
  - to ignore a warning/a problem/an issue
  - She deliberately ignored my question and changed the subject.
  - We can't ignore the fact that there is a huge problem here.
  - His work was largely ignored and forgotten for 30 years.
  - Much of social science research simply ignores these sectors.
  - The government has completely ignored the wishes of the public.

### [illegal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/illegal_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈliːɡl/` · **US:** `/ɪˈliːɡl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ill/illeg/illegal__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ill/illeg/illegal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - illegal drugs/firearms/substances
  - She promised to crack down on illegal immigration.
  - illegal immigrants/aliens
  - His illegal activities landed him in jail several times.
  - illegal mining/fishing/gambling
  - These proposals are unethical and possibly illegal.
  - it is illegal to do something It's illegal to drive through a red light.
  - it is illegal for somebody to do something It is illegal for anyone under 18 to possess fireworks in a public place.

### [imaginary](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/imaginary) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈmædʒɪnəri/` · **US:** `/ɪˈmædʒɪneri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ima/imagi/imaginary__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ima/imagi/imaginary__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The equator is an imaginary line around the middle of the earth.
  - I had an imaginary friend when I was a child.
  - As children they invented the imaginary world of Gondal.
  - We must listen to their problems, real or imaginary.

### [immediate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/immediate) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈmiːdiət/` · **US:** `/ɪˈmiːdiət/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/imm/immed/immediate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/imm/immed/immediate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an immediate reaction/response
  - to take immediate action
  - RAM stores information for immediate access.
  - This decision will have an immediate impact on students.
  - The company announced the immediate availability of an updated version of the software.

### [immigrant](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/immigrant) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪmɪɡrənt/` · **US:** `/ˈɪmɪɡrənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/imm/immig/immigrant__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/imm/immig/immigrant__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - immigrant from… immigrants from other European countries
  - immigrant to… immigrants to the United States
  - Immigrants and their descendants have played a vital role in building this nation.
  - The president vowed to continue deporting illegal immigrants.
  - The program protects undocumented immigrants who came to the US as children.
  - immigrant workers/communities/groups

### [impact](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/impact_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪmpækt/` · **US:** `/ˈɪmpækt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/imp/impac/impact__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/imp/impac/impact__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a positive/a negative/an adverse impact
  - a significant/major/huge impact
  - to have/make an impact
  - impact of something to reduce/minimize the impact of something
  - the environmental impact of tourism
  - impact of doing something the economic impact of leaving the European Union
  - impact on somebody/something Her speech made a profound impact on everyone.
  - She has had a lasting impact on the lives of many of her students.
  - The project's impact on the environment should be minimal.
  - impact of something on somebody/something The report assesses the impact of the disease on mortality and population growth.
  - impact from something It is too early to notice any impact from the recent changes to the rules.
  - an environmental impact assessment
  - a victim impact statement

### [impact](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/impact_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪmˈpækt/` · **US:** `/ɪmˈpækt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/imp/impac/impact__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/imp/impac/impact__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - impact on/upon somebody/something Her father's death impacted greatly on her childhood years.
  - These restrictions will impact on all farmers in Ireland.
  - impact somebody/something environmental factors that may negatively impact children's development
  - The company's performance was impacted by the high value of the pound.

### [import](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/import_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪmpɔːt/` · **US:** `/ˈɪmpɔːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/imp/impor/import__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/imp/impor/import__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - import from… food imports from abroad
  - import into/to… cheap imports into the United States

### [import](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/import_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪmˈpɔːt/` · **US:** `/ɪmˈpɔːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/imp/impor/import__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/imp/impor/import__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - import something The country has to import most of its raw materials.
  - import something from… customs imported from the West
  - import something into/to… the cost of importing petroleum products into the country
  - import something (from…) (into/to…) goods imported from Japan into the US

### [importance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/importance) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪmˈpɔːtns/` · **US:** `/ɪmˈpɔːrtns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/imp/impor/importance__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/imp/impor/importance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She stressed the importance of careful preparation.
  - to emphasize/highlight the importance of something
  - He understands the importance of getting the details right.
  - They attach great importance to the project.
  - the relative importance of the two ideas
  - of importance (to somebody) It's a matter of the utmost importance to me.
  - to be of paramount/vital importance
  - State your reasons in order of importance.
  - He was very aware of his own importance (= of his status).

### [impression](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/impression) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪmˈpreʃn/` · **US:** `/ɪmˈpreʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/imp/impre/impression__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/imp/impre/impression__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - My first impression of him was favourable.
  - She gives the impression of being very busy.
  - impression that… I did not get the impression that they were unhappy about the situation.
  - I had the impression that it was all done in a hurry.
  - My impression is that there are still a lot of problems.
  - Try and smile. You don't want to give people the wrong impression (= that you are not friendly).
  - He does not want to give a false impression of progress.
  - If you want to create the right impression, I suggest you wear a suit.

### [impressive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/impressive) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪmˈpresɪv/` · **US:** `/ɪmˈpresɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/imp/impre/impressive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/imp/impre/impressive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an impressive performance
  - an impressive array/list/collection
  - an impressive display of skill
  - He has an impressive record of 63 wins from 71 fights.
  - an impressive building with a huge tower
  - one of the most impressive novels of recent years
  - Several of the younger players also looked impressive.
  - She was very impressive in the interview.

### [improvement](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/improvement) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪmˈpruːvmənt/` · **US:** `/ɪmˈpruːvmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/imp/impro/improvement__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/imp/impro/improvement__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The economy has shown significant improvement over the past 9 months.
  - We expect to see further improvement over the coming year.
  - Sales figures continue to show signs of improvement.
  - There were areas that needed improvement.
  - improvement in something There is a need for continuous improvement in performance.
  - I think there is room for improvement in any organization.

### [incredibly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/incredibly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈkredəbli/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈkredəbli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inc/incre/incredibly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inc/incre/incredibly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It was all incredibly difficult.
  - organizations doing incredibly important work
  - incredibly stupid/boring
  - incredibly powerful/strong
  - incredibly lucky/talented/beautiful

### [indeed](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/indeed) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈdiːd/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈdiːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ind/indee/indeed__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ind/indee/indeed__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Thank you very much indeed!
  - I was very sad indeed to hear of your father's death.
  - The last three months have been very good indeed for the company.

### [indicate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/indicate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪndɪkeɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈɪndɪkeɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ind/indic/indicate__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ind/indic/indicate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - indicate something Initial tests indicate the presence of oxygen.
  - The survey data indicate a general satisfaction with the process.
  - He has declared support for women, but his actions have indicated otherwise.
  - indicate that… Research indicates that eating habits are changing fast.
  - These findings clearly indicate that the scheme has improved pedestrian safety.
  - The study/evidence/report indicates that…
  - Kingston-upon-Thames, as the name indicates, is situated on the banks of the Thames.
  - indicate how, what etc… Our results indicate how misleading it could be to rely on this method.

### [indirect](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/indirect) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪndaɪˈrekt/` · **US:** `/ˌɪndaɪˈrekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ind/indir/indirect__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ind/indir/indirect__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the indirect effects of the war
  - to find something out by indirect methods
  - The building collapsed as an indirect result of the heavy rain.
  - There would be some benefit, however indirect, to the state.
  - indirect costs (= costs that are not directly connected with making a product, for example training, heating, rent, etc.)

### [indoor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/indoor) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪndɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈɪndɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ind/indoo/indoor__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ind/indoo/indoor__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an indoor swimming pool
  - indoor games
  - Poor indoor air quality can result in increased risk of asthma and allergies.

### [indoors](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/indoors) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪnˈdɔːz/` · **US:** `/ˌɪnˈdɔːrz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ind/indoo/indoors__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ind/indoo/indoors__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I prefer to stay indoors in this really hot weather.
  - Let's go indoors.
  - I was indoors watching TV when I heard shouting in the street.
  - Many herbs can be grown indoors.
  - She added that in Hong Kong most cats were kept indoors.

### [influence](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/influence_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪnfluəns/` · **US:** `/ˈɪnfluəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inf/influ/influence__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inf/influ/influence__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - His early work shows the influence of Cézanne and Matisse.
  - influence on somebody/something He had considerable influence on younger sculptors.
  - Peer group members can exert a strong influence on each other's activities.
  - Such media pressure exercises influence on policymakers.
  - influence of somebody/something on somebody/something the influence of the climate on agricultural production
  - What exactly is the influence of social media on children?
  - The religious community wished to be independent of outside influence.

### [influence](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/influence_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪnfluəns/` · **US:** `/ˈɪnfluəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inf/influ/influence__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inf/influ/influence__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - influence somebody/something His writings have influenced the lives of millions.
  - Her writing was strongly influenced by her Catholic upbringing.
  - His film is heavily influenced by other film-makers.
  - Don't let me influence you either way.
  - Do bird-watchers influence the behaviour of the birds they are watching?
  - I do not wish to influence your decision one way or the other.
  - influence how, whether, etc… The wording of questions can influence how people answer.
  - influence somebody to do something She was influenced to take up voluntary work by her teacher.
  - influence somebody in something Her parents tried to influence her in her choice of university.

### [ingredient](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ingredient) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈɡriːdiənt/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈɡriːdiənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ing/ingre/ingredient__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ing/ingre/ingredient__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Mix all the ingredients in a bowl.
  - Our skin cream contains only natural ingredients.
  - The only active ingredient in this medicine is aspirin.
  - Avocado is the main ingredient of the Mexican dish.
  - ingredient for something Coconut is a basic ingredient for many curries.

### [injure](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/injure) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪndʒə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈɪndʒər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inj/injur/injure__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inj/injur/injure__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He injured his knee playing hockey.
  - Three people were killed and five injured in the crash.
  - She injured herself during training.
  - She was seriously injured in a riding accident.
  - During the Second World War he was badly injured by a bomb.
  - He was hit by an unmarked police car and severely injured.
  - The man was critically injured after falling from a ladder.

### [injured](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/injured) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪndʒəd/` · **US:** `/ˈɪndʒərd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inj/injur/injured__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inj/injur/injured__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - His injured leg prevented him from walking.
  - Luckily, she isn't injured.
  - Carter is playing in place of the injured O'Reilly.
  - The boy and another seriously injured person were evacuated by air ambulance
  - He tried to pull his critically injured friend from the burning vehicle.
  - He was conscious, but severely injured.
  - her badly injured ankle

### [innocent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/innocent_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪnəsnt/` · **US:** `/ˈɪnəsnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inn/innoc/innocent__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inn/innoc/innocent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They have imprisoned an innocent man.
  - innocent of something She was found innocent of any crime.
  - innocent of doing something I was convinced that he was innocent of taking the money.
  - He was the innocent party (= person) in the dispute.
  - There must be an innocent explanation for her behaviour.

### [intelligence](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/intelligence) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈtelɪdʒəns/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈtelɪdʒəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/intel/intelligence__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/intel/intelligence__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a person of high/average/low intelligence
  - He didn't even have the intelligence to call for an ambulance.
  - As a director, he tends to underestimate the intelligence of his audience.
  - Please don't insult my intelligence by lying to me.

### [intend](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/intend) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈtend/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈtend/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/inten/intend__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/inten/intend__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We finished later than we had intended.
  - intend to do something I fully intended (= definitely intended) to pay for the damage.
  - I never intended to hurt you.
  - The train we had originally intended to catch had already left.
  - intend somebody/something to do something The writer clearly intends his readers to identify with the main character.
  - intend doing something (British English) I don't intend staying long.
  - intend something The company intends a slow-down in expansion.
  - intend somebody something He intended her no harm (= it was not his plan to harm her).
  - it is intended that… It is intended that production will start next month.
  - intend that… We intend that production will start next month.

### [intention](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/intention) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈtenʃn/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈtenʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/inten/intention__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/inten/intention__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - intention of doing something I have no intention of going to the wedding.
  - I have every intention of paying her back what I owe her.
  - with the intention of doing something He left England with the intention of travelling in Africa.
  - intention to do something He has announced his intention to retire.
  - The Prime Minister declared her intention to remain in office despite declining support.
  - The original intention was to devote three months to the project.
  - intention in doing something Her intentions in making this proposal are clear.
  - intention that… It was not my intention that she should suffer.
  - She's full of good intentions but they rarely work out.
  - I did it with the best (of) intentions (= meaning to help), but I only succeeded in annoying them.

### [invest](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/invest) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈvest/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈvest/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inv/inves/invest__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inv/inves/invest__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Now is a good time to invest.
  - invest in something She advised us to invest in the property market.
  - invest something If you want to invest your money, you are probably better off with bonds.
  - invest something in something He invested his life savings in his daughter's business.
  - She personally invested $980 000 in the company.

### [investigate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/investigate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈvestɪɡeɪt/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈvestɪɡeɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inv/inves/investigate__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inv/inves/investigate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The FBI has been called in to investigate.
  - the senior investigating officer
  - (informal) ‘What was that noise?’ ‘I'll go and investigate.’
  - investigate something Detectives are currently investigating possible links between the murders.
  - to investigate a crime/a complaint/an incident
  - The allegations have not yet been thoroughly investigated.
  - The report calls for a commission to investigate the matter further.
  - The case will be investigated in detail to determine whether the claims are true.
  - investigate what, how, etc… Police are investigating what happened.

### [involved](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/involved) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈvɒlvd/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈvɑːlvd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inv/invol/involved__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inv/invol/involved__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Some people tried to stop the fight but I didn't want to get involved.
  - It can be helpful to talk about your worries to someone who is not directly involved.
  - involved in something He became actively involved in politics.
  - How many vehicles were involved in the crash?
  - They were involved in a road accident.
  - involved with something Several people have been involved with the project from the beginning.

### [iron](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/iron_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈaɪən/` · **US:** `/ˈaɪərn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/iro/iron_/iron__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/iro/iron_/iron__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - iron gates/bars/railings
  - a hut with a corrugated iron roof
  - the iron and steel industries
  - iron ore (= rock containing iron)
  - foods that are rich in iron
  - patients with iron deficiency (= not enough iron in their blood)
  - iron tablets (= containing iron prepared as a medicine)
  - (figurative) She had a will of iron (= it was very strong).

### [iron](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/iron_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈaɪən/` · **US:** `/ˈaɪərn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/iro/iron_/iron__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/iro/iron_/iron__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He was ironing when I arrived.
  - iron something I'll need to iron that dress before I can wear it.

### [issue](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/issue_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪʃuː/` · **US:** `/ˈɪʃuː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/iss/issue/issue__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/iss/issue/issue__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a key/major issue
  - This is a big issue; we need more time to think about it.
  - Teacher education has become a political issue.
  - They discussed a number of important environmental issues.
  - They are talking about issues relating to the safety of children online.
  - Her work deals with issues of race and identity.
  - to explore/debate/examine an issue
  - The union plans to raise the issue of overtime.
  - We really need to focus on the key issues and not get sidetracked.
  - You're just avoiding the issue.
  - Don't confuse the issue.
  - The meeting included discussion of a range of issues.
  - What you say is interesting, but it does not affect the question at issue here.

### [IT](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/it_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌaɪ ˈtiː/` · **US:** `/ˌaɪ ˈtiː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/i_t/i_t__/i_t__1_gb_1_abbr.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/i_t/i_t__/i_t__1_us_1_abbr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the increasing use of IT in all aspects of today's society
  - in IT I've worked in IT for 30 years.
  - IT professionals/staff/managers
  - the IT industry/sector
  - an IT company/department
  - IT infrastructure/security


## J

### [journal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/journal) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdʒɜːnl/` · **US:** `/ˈdʒɜːrnl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/j/jou/journ/journal__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/j/jou/journ/journal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a scientific/an academic journal
  - the British Medical Journal
  - the Journal of Experimental Psychology
  - a trade/professional/specialist journal
  - an online journal
  - a journal article
  - in a journal The results of the study are expected to be published in a peer-reviewed journal next month.

### [judge](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/judge_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dʒʌdʒ/` · **US:** `/dʒʌdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/j/jud/judge/judge__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/j/jud/judge/judge__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a High Court judge
  - a federal/district judge
  - the presiding trial judge
  - to appoint a judge
  - before a judge The case comes before Judge Cooper next week.
  - He will appear before a judge tomorrow.
  - The judge sentenced him to five years in prison.
  - The judge ruled that the evidence was inadmissible.

### [judge](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/judge_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dʒʌdʒ/` · **US:** `/dʒʌdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/j/jud/judge/judge__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/j/jud/judge/judge__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - As far as I can judge, all of them are to blame.
  - judging by something Judging by her last email, they are having a wonderful time.
  - to judge from something To judge from what he said, he was very disappointed.
  - judge somebody/something You shouldn't judge her too harshly.
  - judge somebody/something on something Schools should not be judged only on exam results.
  - Each painting must be judged on its own merits.
  - judge somebody/something + noun The tour was judged a great success.
  - judge somebody/something to be/do something The concert was judged to have been a great success.
  - judge somebody/something + adj. They judged it wise to say nothing.
  - judge that… He judged that the risk was too great.
  - it is judged that… It was judged that the risk was too great.
  - judge how, what, etc… It was hard to judge how great the risk was.


## K

### [keen](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/keen_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kiːn/` · **US:** `/kiːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/k/kee/keen_/keen__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/k/kee/keen_/keen__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - keen to do something John was very keen to help.
  - keen for somebody/something to do something His father was keen for him to go to university.
  - He is keen for the deal to proceed.
  - keen that… We are keen that our school should get involved too.
  - keen on doing something I wasn't too keen on going to the party.

### [key](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/key_3) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kiː/` · **US:** `/kiː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/k/key/key__/key__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/k/key/key__/key__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - key something I was busy keying data.
  - key something in Key in your password.
  - key something into something The information is then keyed into a computer.

### [keyboard](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/keyboard_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkiːbɔːd/` · **US:** `/ˈkiːbɔːrd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/k/key/keybo/keyboard__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/k/key/keybo/keyboard__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The program locks the keyboard until a password is given.
  - keyboard shortcuts

### [kick](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/kick_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kɪk/` · **US:** `/kɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/k/kic/kick_/kick__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/k/kic/kick_/kick__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the first kick of the game
  - He barely got a kick of the ball the whole match.
  - She gave him a kick on the shin.
  - If the door won't open, give it a kick.
  - He aimed a kick at the dog.

### [kick](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/kick_1) *(verb)*
