- **UK:** `/kɪk/` · **US:** `/kɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/k/kic/kick_/kick__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/k/kic/kick_/kick__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Stop kicking—it hurts!
  - kick somebody/something She was punched and kicked by her attackers.
  - kick somebody/something + adv./prep./adj. The boys were kicking a ball around in the yard.
  - Vandals had kicked the door down.
  - She had to kick the door open because her hands were full.
  - kick + adv./prep. She kicked at the loose pebbles by the roadside.

### [killing](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/killing_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɪlɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈkɪlɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/k/kil/killi/killing__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/k/kil/killi/killing__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Their leader condemned the torture and killing of innocent civilians.
  - The mass killing (= killing of a large number of people) occurred when soldiers opened fire on protesters.
  - a verdict of unlawful killing
  - He is responsible for the brutal killing of thousands of animals.
  - No motive for the killing has yet been established.

### [kind](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/kind_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kaɪnd/` · **US:** `/kaɪnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/k/kin/kind_/kind__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/k/kin/kind_/kind__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a very kind and helpful person
  - a kind heart/face
  - a kind action/gesture/comment
  - You've been very kind.
  - kind to somebody/something kind to animals
  - (figurative) Soft water is kinder to your hair.
  - (figurative) The weather was very kind to us.
  - kind to do something Was it kind to let her find out this way?
  - He was kind enough to take time out of his busy schedule to come here today.
  - it is kind of somebody I think it was very kind of her.
  - it is kind of somebody to do something It was really kind of you to help me.
  - (formal) Thank you for your kind words.
  - The poems are reprinted here by kind permission of the author.
  - (formal) ‘Do have another.’ ‘That's very kind of you (= thank you).’
  - We need a kinder, gentler society.
  - If an animal is badly injured, often the kindest thing to do is to destroy it painlessly.

### [kiss](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/kiss_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kɪs/` · **US:** `/kɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/k/kis/kiss_/kiss__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/k/kis/kiss_/kiss__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Come here and give me a kiss!
  - a kiss on the cheek
  - We were greeted with hugs and kisses.
  - As the train drew away he blew her a kiss (= kissed his hand and pretended to blow the kiss towards her).

### [kiss](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/kiss_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kɪs/` · **US:** `/kɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/k/kis/kiss_/kiss__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/k/kis/kiss_/kiss__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They stood in a doorway kissing (= kissing each other).
  - Do people in Britain kiss when they meet?
  - kiss somebody/something Go and kiss your mother goodnight.
  - He bent forward to kiss her cheek.
  - She kissed him on both cheeks.
  - He lifted the trophy up and kissed it.

### [knock](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/knock_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/nɒk/` · **US:** `/nɑːk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/k/kno/knock/knock__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/k/kno/knock/knock__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - knock on/at something There was a knock at the door.
  - I was startled by a loud knock on the car window.


## L

### [label](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/label_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈleɪbl/` · **US:** `/ˈleɪbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lab/label/label__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lab/label/label__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Always read the label carefully.
  - price/address labels
  - on a label The washing instructions are on the label.

### [label](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/label_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈleɪbl/` · **US:** `/ˈleɪbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lab/label/label__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lab/label/label__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - label something Make sure that your luggage is clearly labelled.
  - label something with something We carefully labelled each item with the contents and the date.
  - label something + adj. The file was labelled ‘Private’.

### [laboratory](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/laboratory) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ləˈbɒrətri/` · **US:** `/ˈlæbrətɔːri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lab/labor/laboratory__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lab/labor/laboratory__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a clinical/research laboratory
  - to send a specimen to the laboratory for analysis
  - in a/the laboratory The effects of weathering can be simulated in the laboratory.
  - laboratory experiments/tests
  - The athletes' reflexes were tested under laboratory conditions.

### [lack](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lack_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/læk/` · **US:** `/læk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lac/lack_/lack__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lac/lack_/lack__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - lack of something a lack of understanding/knowledge
  - Passengers complained about the lack of information when flights were cancelled.
  - Her enthusiasm compensated for her apparent lack of experience.
  - He was suffering from a complete lack of confidence.
  - There was no lack of volunteers.
  - because of a lack of something The project was abandoned because of a total lack of support.
  - due to (a) lack of something He was released due to lack of evidence.
  - through lack of something The trip was cancelled through lack of interest.
  - despite the lack of something Fans of classic comedy should be happy with this disc, despite the lack of extras.
  - Money and the fear of its lack are present in many of his poems.

### [lack](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lack_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/læk/` · **US:** `/læk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lac/lack_/lack__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lac/lack_/lack__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to lack confidence/experience/resources/power
  - The team lacked the skill to compete at the highest level.
  - She has the natural ability that her brother lacks.

### [latest](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/latest_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈleɪtɪst/` · **US:** `/ˈleɪtɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lat/lates/latest__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lat/lates/latest__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the latest craze/fashion/trend
  - his latest book/film/album
  - the latest polls/reports
  - Have you heard the latest news?
  - the latest unemployment figures
  - The government declined to comment on the latest developments.
  - the latest version of the software
  - The latest round of talks was productive.

### [lay](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lay_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/leɪ/` · **US:** `/leɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lay/lay__/lay__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lay/lay__/lay__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - lay somebody/something + adv./prep. He laid a hand on my arm.
  - Relatives laid wreaths on the grave.
  - She laid the baby down gently on the bed.
  - Red roses were laid at the memorial.
  - She noticed some paintings laid against the far wall.
  - The horse laid back its ears.
  - lay something His mother visited the murder scene yesterday to lay flowers.
  - lay somebody/something + adj. The cloth should be laid flat.

### [layer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/layer_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/leə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈleɪər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lay/layer/layer__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lay/layer/layer__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - layer (of something) A thin layer of dust covered everything.
  - Ducks have a thick layer of fat to keep them warm.
  - How many layers of clothing are you wearing?
  - Sunlight thickens the outer layer of the skin.
  - in layers The paint is applied in layers.

### [lead](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lead1_2#lead_hm1_sng_21) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/liːd/` · **US:** `/liːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lea/lead_/lead__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lea/lead_/lead__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She took the lead in the second lap.
  - to hold (onto)/regain the lead
  - in the lead The Democrats now appear to be in the lead.
  - into the lead He has gone into the lead.
  - The lead car is now three minutes ahead of the rest of the field.

### [leading](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/leading1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈliːdɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈliːdɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lea/leadi/leading__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lea/leadi/leading__us_5.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a leading expert/authority/figure/member
  - a leading contender/candidate
  - a leading manufacturer/company/newspaper
  - the team's leading scorer for the past two seasons
  - the leading provider of telecommunications services in Denmark
  - She was offered the leading role in the new TV series.
  - The leading causes of death are heart disease and cancer.

### [leaf](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/leaf_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/liːf/` · **US:** `/liːf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lea/leaf_/leaf__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lea/leaf_/leaf__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - lettuce/cabbage/oak leaves
  - the dead leaves of autumn/the fall
  - The leaves on the trees were beginning to turn from green to orange.
  - in leaf Few trees are in leaf yet.
  - The trees are just coming into leaf.

### [leather](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/leather) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈleðə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈleðər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lea/leath/leather__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lea/leath/leather__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a leather jacket
  - The soles are made of leather.
  - a leather-bound book
  - in leather I'm looking for a pair of boots in dark brown leather.

### [legal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/legal) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈliːɡl/` · **US:** `/ˈliːɡl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/leg/legal/legal__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/leg/legal/legal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the legal profession/system
  - to take/seek legal advice
  - a legal adviser/expert/team
  - legal fees/costs
  - They are currently facing a long legal battle in the US courts.
  - Campaigners have threatened to mount a legal challenge on human rights grounds.

### [leisure](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/leisure) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈleʒə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈliːʒər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lei/leisu/leisure__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lei/leisu/leisure__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - These days we have more money and more leisure to enjoy it.
  - Make the most of your leisure time!

### [length](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/length) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/leŋkθ/` · **US:** `/leŋkθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/len/lengt/length__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/len/lengt/length__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Measure the length of the line from A to B.
  - to estimate/calculate/increase/reduce the length of something
  - in length The river is 300 miles in length.
  - The snake usually reaches a length of 100 cm.
  - Safety barriers will be installed along the full length of the road.
  - He ran the entire length of the beach.
  - Look at the length of that queue!
  - He has to hold newspapers at arm's length to focus on the print.

### [level](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/level_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlevl/` · **US:** `/ˈlevl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lev/level/level__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lev/level/level__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Pitch the tent on level ground.
  - Add a level tablespoon of flour (= enough to fill the spoon but not so much that it goes above the level of the edge of the spoon).

### [lie](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lie2_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/laɪ/` · **US:** `/laɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lie/lie__/lie__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lie/lie__/lie__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to tell a lie
  - The whole story is nothing but a pack of lies.
  - That's an outright lie!
  - a barefaced/bald-faced/blatant lie (= a lie that is deliberate and shocking)
  - lie about somebody/something How dare you spread such vicious lies about other people?
  - He said he would not respond to the lies about his war record.
  - lie about doing something an elaborate lie about being attacked by two masked men

### [lie](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lie2_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/laɪ/` · **US:** `/laɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lie/lie__/lie__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lie/lie__/lie__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You could see from his face that he was lying.
  - lie to somebody Don't lie to me!
  - lie about something She lies about her age.
  - lie about doing something He lied about having a university degree.
  - The camera cannot lie (= give a false impression).
  - He was unable to lie convincingly.

### [like](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/like_4) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/laɪk/` · **US:** `/laɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lik/like_/like__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lik/like_/like__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We all have different likes and dislikes.
  - He is more than happy to discuss his life, his likes and his career.
  - She is very cosmopolitan in her likes and attitudes.

### [limit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/limit_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlɪmɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈlɪmɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lim/limit/limit__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lim/limit/limit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They imposed a strict spending limit.
  - His speed was double the legal limit.
  - Do not exceed the recommended limit of 6g of salt per day.
  - limit on something The EU has set strict limits on levels of pollution.
  - within limits to keep government spending within acceptable limits
  - over the limit You can't drive—you're over the limit (= you have drunk more alcohol than is legal when driving).

### [limit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/limit_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlɪmɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈlɪmɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lim/limit/limit__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lim/limit/limit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - measures to limit carbon dioxide emissions
  - The amount of money you have to spend will limit your choice.
  - Try to limit the number of trips you make.
  - to limit somebody's ability/freedom/power to do something

### [lip](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lip) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/lɪp/` · **US:** `/lɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lip/lip__/lip__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lip/lip__/lip__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The assistant pursed her lips.
  - your upper/lower/top/bottom lip
  - on the lips She kissed him on the lips.
  - He raised the bottle to his lips and took a large swig from it.
  - She always has a cigarette dangling from her lips.

### [liquid](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/liquid_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlɪkwɪd/` · **US:** `/ˈlɪkwɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/liq/liqui/liquid__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/liq/liqui/liquid__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - liquid nitrogen
  - liquid soap
  - The detergent comes in powder or liquid form.
  - a bar selling snacks and liquid refreshment (= drinks)
  - (humorous) a liquid lunch (= alcoholic drinks, rather than food)

### [liquid](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/liquid_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlɪkwɪd/` · **US:** `/ˈlɪkwɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/liq/liqui/liquid__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/liq/liqui/liquid__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She poured the dark brown liquid down the sink.
  - restrictions on passengers carrying liquids in their hand luggage
  - a flammable liquid
  - the transition from liquid to vapour

### [literature](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/literature) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlɪtrətʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈlɪtrətʃʊr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lit/liter/literature__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lit/liter/literature__us_7.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - English/American/French literature
  - children's literature
  - great works of literature
  - For most people, the desire to study literature begins with a love of reading.

### [live](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/live2_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/laɪv/` · **US:** `/laɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/liv/live_/live__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/liv/live_/live__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - live animals
  - the number of live births (= babies born alive)
  - We saw a real live rattlesnake!
  - I need to talk to a live person (= not a recording or computer).

### [live](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/live2_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/laɪv/` · **US:** `/laɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/liv/live_/live__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/liv/live_/live__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The show is going out live.
  - We'll be reporting live from Beijing.
  - Three great local bands will be playing live.

### [living](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/living_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlɪvɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈlɪvɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/liv/livin/living__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/liv/livin/living__us_5.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - all living things
  - living organisms
  - the finest living pianist
  - The elephant is the biggest living land animal.

### [living](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/living_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlɪvɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈlɪvɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/liv/livin/living__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/liv/livin/living__us_5.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She earns her living as a freelance journalist.
  - to make a decent/good/meagre living
  - for a living What do you do for a living?
  - She had to drop out of college to work for a living.
  - to scrape a living from part-time tutoring
  - His father struggled to make a living by farming.
  - It is possible to make a living out of writing.

### [local](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/local_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈləʊkl/` · **US:** `/ˈləʊkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/loc/local/local__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/loc/local/local__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The locals are very friendly.
  - She’s beginning to sound like one of the locals.
  - The victim was a local who jogged regularly in the park.

### [locate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/locate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ləʊˈkeɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈləʊkeɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/loc/locat/locate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/loc/locat/locate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The mechanic located the fault immediately.
  - Rescue planes are trying to locate the missing sailors.
  - I twisted my head to either side, attempting to immediately locate and identify the horrible stench.
  - We tried to locate the source of the sound.

### [located](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/located) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ləʊˈkeɪtɪd/` · **US:** `/ˈləʊkeɪtɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/loc/locat/located__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/loc/locat/located__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a small town located 30 miles south of Chicago
  - The offices are conveniently located just a few minutes from the main station.
  - The town is strategically located midway between Belfast and Dublin.
  - a small, centrally located hotel

### [location](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/location) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ləʊˈkeɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ləʊˈkeɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/loc/locat/location__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/loc/locat/location__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a honeymoon in a secret location
  - Mobile phones can determine our exact location and relay it to the police in an emergency.
  - The harsh climate and remote location made mining even more difficult.
  - the country's strategic geographical/geographic location

### [lonely](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lonely) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈləʊnli/` · **US:** `/ˈləʊnli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lon/lonel/lonely__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lon/lonel/lonely__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She lives alone and often feels lonely.

### [loss](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/loss) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/lɒs/` · **US:** `/lɔːs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/los/loss_/loss__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/los/loss_/loss__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I want to report the loss of a package.
  - Insure your instrument against loss or damage.
  - weight loss
  - hearing/memory loss
  - The closure of the factory will lead to a number of job losses.
  - When she died I was filled with a sense of loss.
  - loss of something He suffered a loss of confidence.
  - loss of earnings/income (= the money you do not earn because you are prevented from working)
  - The loss of her job was a terrible blow.

### [luxury](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/luxury_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlʌkʃəri/` · **US:** `/ˈlʌkʃəri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lux/luxur/luxury__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lux/luxur/luxury__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a luxury hotel/car/apartment
  - luxury goods
  - expensive luxury brands/items

### [luxury](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/luxury_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlʌkʃəri/` · **US:** `/ˈlʌkʃəri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lux/luxur/luxury__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lux/luxur/luxury__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a life of unimaginable luxury
  - in luxury Now we'll be able to live in luxury for the rest of our lives.


## M

### [mad](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mad_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/mæd/` · **US:** `/mæd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mad/mad__/mad__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mad/mad__/mad__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You must be mad to risk it.
  - It was a mad idea.
  - ‘I'm going to buy some new clothes.’ ‘Well, don't go mad (= spend more than is sensible).’
  - You'd be raving mad to miss this annual music extravaganza.

### [magic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/magic_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmædʒɪk/` · **US:** `/ˈmædʒɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mag/magic/magic__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mag/magic/magic__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a magic spell/charm/potion
  - There is no magic formula for passing exams—only hard work.

### [magic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/magic_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmædʒɪk/` · **US:** `/ˈmædʒɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mag/magic/magic__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mag/magic/magic__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Do you believe in magic?
  - a place of secret shadows and ancient magic
  - He sensed the presence of powerful dark magic (= evil magic).
  - as if by magic He suddenly appeared as if by magic.
  - like magic A passage was cleared through the crowd like magic.

### [mainly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mainly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmeɪnli/` · **US:** `/ˈmeɪnli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mai/mainl/mainly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mai/mainl/mainly__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They eat mainly fruit and nuts.
  - ‘Where do you export to?’ ‘France, mainly.’
  - mainly because of something The population almost doubles in summer, mainly because of the jazz festival.
  - mainly due to something The rise in inflation is mainly due to higher food prices.
  - People come to libraries mainly to borrow books.
  - During the day it’ll become mainly dry with clear skies.

### [mall](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mall_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/mæl/` · **US:** `/mɔːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mal/mall_/mall__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mal/mall_/mall__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Let's go to the mall.
  - Teenagers would go hang at the mall after school.
  - Check out outlet malls (= ones which sell goods at reduced prices) and discount stores for good deals.
  - The friends were shopping at a mall in Columbus.

### [management](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/management) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmænɪdʒmənt/` · **US:** `/ˈmænɪdʒmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/man/manag/management__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/man/manag/management__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a career in management
  - hotel/project management
  - a management training course
  - the day-to-day management of the business
  - The report blames bad management.
  - Effective financial management is essential.
  - The company's top-down management style made decision-making slow and inflexible.

### [market](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/market_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɑːkɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈmɑːrkɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mar/marke/market__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mar/marke/market__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - market something The company utilizes every media tool available to market its products.
  - The drug had been successfully marketed in Germany.
  - market something as something It is marketed as a low-alcohol wine.
  - market something to somebody School meals need to be marketed to children in the same way as other food.

### [marketing](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/marketing) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɑːkɪtɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈmɑːrkɪtɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mar/marke/marketing__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mar/marke/marketing__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a marketing campaign/strategy
  - a marketing manager/director/department
  - digital/internet/online marketing
  - Direct marketing already provides a number of ways to target specific individuals.
  - They hope to create a buzz through viral marketing.
  - She works in sales and marketing.
  - marketing of something The marketing of produce by village women has brought economic benefits.
  - This law regulates the sale and marketing of food on a statewide basis.
  - Clients hired us to handle their online marketing.
  - Persuasion is one of the most valuable skills in marketing.
  - A printed newsletter is still an excellent marketing tool.
  - We have a different marketing plan for each of our channels.
  - The latest campaign is a joint marketing effort by dairy processors.
  - a clever marketing ploy

### [marriage](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/marriage) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmærɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈmærɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mar/marri/marriage__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mar/marri/marriage__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a happy/an unhappy marriage
  - All of her children's marriages ended in divorce.
  - She has two children by a previous marriage.
  - This is his second marriage.
  - Same-sex marriages are now recognized in many countries.

### [meanwhile](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/meanwhile_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmiːnwaɪl/` · **US:** `/ˈmiːnwaɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mea/meanw/meanwhile__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mea/meanw/meanwhile__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Leave the cake to cool completely. Meanwhile, make the topping.
  - The storm is expected to move north today. Concerns are mounting meanwhile for a missing surfer.

### [measure](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/measure_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmeʒə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈmeʒər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mea/measu/measure__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mea/measu/measure__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - safety/austerity measures
  - a temporary/an emergency measure
  - a series/number/range of measures
  - a package of measures aimed at cutting pollution
  - New security measures were implemented to prevent further violence.
  - as a… measure Police in riot gear were in attendance as a precautionary measure.
  - measure to do something We must take preventive measures to reduce crime in the area.
  - The government is introducing tougher measures to combat crime.
  - measure against something They are demanding harsher measures against racism.

### [measure](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/measure_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmeʒə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈmeʒər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mea/measu/measure__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mea/measu/measure__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - measure something/somebody a device that measures the level of radiation in the atmosphere
  - Blood pressure and heart rate should be measured before treatment.
  - A nurse visited country schools to weigh and measure children.
  - measuring equipment/instruments
  - measure something in something A ship's speed is measured in knots.
  - measure somebody/something for something He's gone to be measured for a new suit.
  - measure how much, how long, etc… A dipstick is used to measure how much oil is left in an engine.

### [medium](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/medium_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmiːdiəm/` · **US:** `/ˈmiːdiəm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/med/mediu/medium__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/med/mediu/medium__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There are three sizes—small, medium and large.
  - a man of medium height/build
  - a medium steak (= one cooked quite well but not too much)
  - Cook over a medium heat for 15 minutes.
  - a medium dry white wine
  - Choose medium to large tomatoes.

### [mental](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mental_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmentl/` · **US:** `/ˈmentl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/men/menta/mental__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/men/menta/mental__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the mental process of remembering
  - Do you have a mental picture of what it will look like?
  - The actor doesn't match the mental image I had of the character.
  - I made a mental note to talk to her about it.
  - He has a complete mental block (= difficulty in understanding or remembering) when it comes to physics.
  - These musicians have prodigious mental capacities.
  - The experience caused her huge amounts of mental suffering.

### [mention](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mention_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmenʃn/` · **US:** `/ˈmenʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/men/menti/mention__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/men/menti/mention__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He made no mention of her work.
  - The concert didn't even get a mention in the newspapers.
  - We have several other products worthy of mention.
  - at the mention of something/somebody I went white at the mention of her name.
  - The hashtag is getting an average of seven million mentions a day on the site.

### [mess](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mess_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/mes/` · **US:** `/mes/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mes/mess_/mess__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mes/mess_/mess__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in a mess The room was in a mess.
  - The kids made a mess in the bathroom.
  - ‘What a mess!’ she said, surveying the scene after the party.
  - My hair's a real mess!

### [mild](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mild_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/maɪld/` · **US:** `/maɪld/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mil/mild_/mild__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mil/mild_/mild__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a mild form of the disease
  - a mild case of flu
  - The symptoms were mild.
  - a mild punishment/criticism
  - It's safe to take a mild sedative.
  - Use a soap that is mild on the skin.

### [mine](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mine_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/maɪn/` · **US:** `/maɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/min/mine_/mine__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/min/mine_/mine__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a copper/diamond mine
  - mine owners/workers
  - They were appalled at the poor working conditions in the mines.
  - At its peak, the mine produced 5 000 tons of coal a day.

### [mix](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mix_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/mɪks/` · **US:** `/mɪks/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mix/mix__/mix__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mix/mix__/mix__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's a school with a good social and ethnic mix of children.
  - The town offers a fascinating mix of old and new.
  - Their music is an eclectic mix of regional styles.
  - I love the city's heady mix of cultures.
  - a pair of wool mix socks (= made of wool and other materials)
  - a party with just the right mix of people

### [mix](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mix_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/mɪks/` · **US:** `/mɪks/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mix/mix__/mix__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mix/mix__/mix__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Oil and water do not mix.
  - mix with something Oil does not mix with water.
  - Grief mixed with fear and rage as the people surveyed the ruins of their homes.
  - mix A and B (together) Mix all the ingredients together in a bowl.
  - If you mix blue and yellow, you get green.
  - mix A with B I don't like to mix business with pleasure (= combine social events with doing business).
  - mix A into B Smith often mixed sand into her paint to create a textured surface.

### [mixture](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mixture) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɪkstʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈmɪkstʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mix/mixtu/mixture__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mix/mixtu/mixture__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was a curious mixture, part grand lady, part wild child.
  - mixture of A and B The city is a mixture of old and new buildings.
  - We listened to the news with a mixture of surprise and horror.
  - Anger is often caused by frustration or embarrassment, or a mixture of the two.
  - pastries filled with a mixture of nuts, sugar and rose water

### [mood](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mood) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/muːd/` · **US:** `/muːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/moo/mood_/mood__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/moo/mood_/mood__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in a … mood She's in a good mood today (= happy and friendly).
  - He's always in a bad mood (= unhappy, or angry and impatient).
  - to be in a foul/festive mood
  - Wait until he’s in a better mood before you ask him.
  - in the mood for (doing) something I'm just not in the mood for a party tonight.
  - He was in no mood for being polite to visitors.
  - in the mood (to do something) I'm not really in the mood to go out tonight.
  - Let’s not talk about it now. I’m not in the mood.

### [move](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/move_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/muːv/` · **US:** `/muːv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mov/move_/move__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mov/move_/move__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Don't make a move!
  - Every move was painful.
  - She felt he was watching her every move.
  - He performed some energetic dance moves.

### [mud](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mud_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/mʌd/` · **US:** `/mʌd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mud/mud__/mud__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mud/mud__/mud__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The car wheels got stuck in the mud.
  - Your boots are covered in mud.
  - Heavy rain caused thick, deep mud on the site.
  - mud bricks/huts (= made of dried mud)
  - nests made of mud or clay

### [murder](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/murder_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɜːdə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈmɜːrdər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mur/murde/murder__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mur/murde/murder__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He was found guilty of murder.
  - She has been charged with the attempted murder of her husband.
  - Who is responsible for this brutal murder?
  - to commit (a) murder
  - He was arrested on suspicion of murder.
  - a murder case/investigation/charge/trial
  - The rebels were responsible for the mass murder of 400 civilians.
  - the families of the murder victims
  - What was the murder weapon?
  - The play is a murder mystery.

### [murder](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/murder_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɜːdə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈmɜːrdər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mur/murde/murder__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mur/murde/murder__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He denies murdering his wife's lover.
  - The boy was brutally murdered.
  - The murdered woman was well known in the area.
  - She was convicted of attempting to murder her husband.

### [muscle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/muscle_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmʌsl/` · **US:** `/ˈmʌsl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mus/muscl/muscle__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mus/muscl/muscle__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a calf/neck/thigh muscle
  - All of this put strain on the heart muscle.
  - to pull/tear/strain a muscle
  - He poses and flexes his muscles in the mirror.
  - This exercise will work the muscles of the lower back.
  - Contract and relax the muscles in your fingers a few times.
  - She tried to relax her tense muscles.
  - He felt every muscle in his body tighten.
  - He didn't move a muscle (= stood completely still).
  - Lift weights to build muscle.
  - muscle fibre/tissue/mass

### [musical](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/musical_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmjuːzɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˈmjuːzɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mus/music/musical__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mus/music/musical__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a Broadway/Hollywood musical
  - the star of the hit West End musical
  - a musical based on the biblical story of Job
  - a cycle of classic Rodgers and Hammerstein musicals

### [mystery](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mystery) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɪstri/` · **US:** `/ˈmɪstəri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mys/myste/mystery__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mys/myste/mystery__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It is one of the great unsolved mysteries of this century.
  - Their motives remain a mystery.
  - She tried to solve the mystery of the boy's sudden death.
  - It is a mystery to somebody why, how, etc… It's a complete mystery to me why they chose him.


## N

### [nail](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nail_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/neɪl/` · **US:** `/neɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nai/nail_/nail__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nai/nail_/nail__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Stop biting your nails!
  - nail clippers

### [narrative](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/narrative_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnærətɪv/` · **US:** `/ˈnærətɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nar/narra/narrative__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nar/narra/narrative__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - narrative fiction
  - Discuss the role of time in the narrative structure of the Iliad.
  - He is best known for his narrative poem of the Civil War.

### [narrative](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/narrative_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnærətɪv/` · **US:** `/ˈnærətɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nar/narra/narrative__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nar/narra/narrative__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a gripping narrative of their journey up the Amazon
  - a collection of personal narratives
  - She has woven a strong personal narrative filled with lively anecdotes.
  - a historical narrative of the United States
  - The film lacks a coherent, compelling narrative.

### [nation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈneɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˈneɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nat/natio/nation__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nat/natio/nation__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - European/Arab/Asian nations
  - The West African nation of Togo has presidential elections this week.
  - Developing nations need affordable access to drugs and medication.
  - Fewer and fewer people in industrialized nations actually know where their food comes from.
  - Leaders of the nations signed a declaration of support.
  - This is an important moment in our nation's history.
  - Germans celebrated yesterday in Berlin, the nation's capital.

### [native](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/native_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈneɪtɪv/` · **US:** `/ˈneɪtɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nat/nativ/native__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nat/nativ/native__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - your native land/country/city
  - Her native language is Korean.
  - It is a long time since he has visited his native Chile.

### [native](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/native_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈneɪtɪv/` · **US:** `/ˈneɪtɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nat/nativ/native__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nat/nativ/native__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a native of New York
  - The 18 year-old Rome native excels in ballet, hip-hop and tap.

### [naturally](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/naturally) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnætʃrəli/` · **US:** `/ˈnætʃrəli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nat/natur/naturally__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nat/natur/naturally__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Naturally, I get upset when things go wrong.
  - After a while, we naturally started talking about the children.
  - ‘Did you complain about the noise?’ ‘Naturally.’
  - It was late, so naturally her mother was worried.
  - We heard a loud crash, so naturally we went outside to investigate.

### [necessarily](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/necessarily) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnesəsərəli/` · **US:** `/ˌnesəˈserəli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nec/neces/necessarily__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nec/neces/necessarily__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The number of places available is necessarily limited.
  - Servants necessarily had close contact with their employers.

### [need](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/need_3) *(modal verb)*
- **UK:** `/niːd/` · **US:** `/niːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nee/need_/need__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nee/need_/need__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - need (not) do something You needn't bother asking Rick—I know he's too busy.
  - I need hardly tell you (= you must already know) that the work is dangerous.
  - If she wants anything, she need only ask.
  - All you need bring are sheets.
  - need (not) have done something You needn't have worried (= it was not necessary for you to worry, but you did)—it all turned out fine.
  - Need you have paid so much?

### [needle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/needle_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈniːdl/` · **US:** `/ˈniːdl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nee/needl/needle__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nee/needl/needle__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a needle and thread
  - the eye (= hole) of a needle
  - Can you thread this needle?
  - a packet of small needles

### [neighbourhood](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/neighbourhood) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈneɪbəhʊd/` · **US:** `/ˈneɪbərhʊd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nei/neigh/neighbourhood__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nei/neigh/neighbourhood__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We grew up in the same neighbourhood.
  - a poor/quiet/residential neighbourhood
  - an old working-class neighbourhood
  - Manhattan is divided into distinct neighborhoods.
  - the neighbourhood police
  - He shouted so loudly that the whole neighbourhood could hear him.

### [neither](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/neither_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈniːðə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈnaɪðər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nei/neith/neither__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nei/neith/neither__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He didn't remember and neither did I.
  - I hadn't been to New York before and neither had Jane.
  - ‘I can't understand a word of it.’ ‘Neither can I.’
  - (informal) ‘I don't know.’ ‘Me neither.’

### [net](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/net_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/net/` · **US:** `/net/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/net/net__/net__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/net/net__/net__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Unfortunately the animals are often caught in fishing nets.
  - She's always peering through the net curtains, spying on the neighbours.

### [next](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/next_3) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/nekst/` · **US:** `/nekst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nex/next_/next__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nex/next_/next__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - One moment he wasn't there, the next he was.
  - the week after next
  - The next to appear was his wife.

### [nor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nor) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/nɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/nɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nor/nor__/nor__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nor/nor__/nor__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She seemed neither surprised nor worried.
  - He wasn't there on Monday. Nor on Tuesday, for that matter.
  - (formal) Not a building nor a tree was left standing.

### [nor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nor) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/nɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/nɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nor/nor__/nor__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nor/nor__/nor__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She seemed neither surprised nor worried.
  - He wasn't there on Monday. Nor on Tuesday, for that matter.
  - (formal) Not a building nor a tree was left standing.

### [normal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/normal_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnɔːml/` · **US:** `/ˈnɔːrml/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nor/norma/normal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nor/norma/normal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - above/below normal The rainfall has been above normal for the time of year.
  - Sales to December are well below normal.
  - (back) to normal Things soon returned to normal.
  - The service will be back to normal next week.
  - as normal Life continued as normal.
  - After the disruption, everyone tried to carry on as normal.

### [northern](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/northern) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnɔːðən/` · **US:** `/ˈnɔːrðərn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nor/north/northern__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nor/north/northern__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the northern slopes of the mountains
  - the northern part of Syria
  - northern Scotland
  - a northern accent
  - He grew up in the northern city of Lille.
  - a concert of Northern European music
  - This week marks the height of summer in the northern hemisphere.

### [note](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/note_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/nəʊt/` · **US:** `/nəʊt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/not/note_/note__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/not/note_/note__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - note something Note the fine early Baroque altar inside the chapel.
  - note (that)… Please note (that) the office will be closed on Monday.
  - note how, where, etc… Note how these animals sometimes walk with their tails up in the air.
  - it is noted that… It should be noted that dissertations submitted late will not be accepted.
  - be noted for something We are also noted for our delicious home-made patisserie.

### [now](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/now_2) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/naʊ/` · **US:** `/naʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/now/now__/now__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/now/now__/now__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Now that the kids have left home we've got a lot of extra space.

### [nuclear](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nuclear) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnjuːkliə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈnuːkliər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nuc/nucle/nuclear__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nuc/nucle/nuclear__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a nuclear power plant/station
  - China and India only produce a very small percentage of their electricity from nuclear facilities.
  - the nuclear industry
  - nuclear-powered submarines
  - a nuclear reactor
  - nuclear waste/fuel


## O

### [obvious](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/obvious) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒbviəs/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːbviəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/obv/obvio/obvious__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/obv/obvio/obvious__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I know you don't like her but try not to make it so obvious.
  - He agreed with obvious pleasure.
  - obvious (that…) It soon became obvious that the machine did not work.
  - It's painfully obvious that the budget was low for this movie.
  - It's obvious a change is needed.
  - obvious to somebody that… It was obvious to everyone that the child had been badly treated.
  - obvious to somebody The lessons seem fairly obvious to anyone who doesn't work in the Administration.
  - obvious from something that… It's obvious from what she said that something is wrong.
  - For obvious reasons, I'd prefer not to give my name.
  - The reasons for this decision were not immediately obvious.
  - The solution to weight loss is also blindingly obvious - whatever you eat, eat less.
  - obvious how, what, etc. It was far from obvious how they were going to get off the island.

### [obviously](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/obviously) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒbviəsli/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːbviəsli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/obv/obvio/obviously__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/obv/obvio/obviously__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Obviously, we don't want to spend too much money.
  - Diet and exercise are obviously important.
  - I wanted to do well, obviously, but I wasn't nervous because I don't see exams as the be all and end all.
  - She responded 'Oh well, obviously I disagree with him'.
  - This massive storm obviously had a huge impact on air travellers.

### [occasion](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/occasion_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈkeɪʒn/` · **US:** `/əˈkeɪʒn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/occ/occas/occasion__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/occ/occas/occasion__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - on an occasion on this/that occasion
  - I've met him on several occasions.
  - They have been seen together on two separate occasions.
  - On one occasion, she called me in the middle of the night.
  - Only on rare occasions do I go out socially.
  - occasion when… I can remember very few occasions when he had to cancel because of ill health.
  - He used the occasion to announce further tax cuts.
  - I have stated on numerous occasions what options are open.
  - Harry has mentioned this on many previous occasions.

### [occur](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/occur) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈkɜː(r)/` · **US:** `/əˈkɜːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/occ/occur/occur__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/occ/occur/occur__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - When exactly did the incident occur?
  - Something unexpected occurred.
  - Three major events occurred in my life that year.
  - These chemical changes occur naturally.
  - More deaths occur in winter.

### [odd](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/odd) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɒd/` · **US:** `/ɑːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/odd/odd__/odd__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/odd/odd__/odd__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They're very odd people.
  - There's something odd about that man.
  - That painting looks very odd.
  - a teenage girl with rather odd hair
  - After a while she noticed something quite odd.
  - When we got inside something struck me as odd.
  - It's an odd little movie.
  - it is/seems odd that… It seems odd that nobody noticed anything wrong.
  - It's most odd that (= very odd that) she hasn't written.
  - The odd thing was that he didn't recognize me.
  - it is odd to do something It's odd to think I will never see her again.

### [official](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/official_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈfɪʃl/` · **US:** `/əˈfɪʃl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/off/offic/official__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/off/offic/official__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an official announcement/decision/statement
  - according to official figures/statistics
  - The news is not yet official.
  - the official biography of the President
  - An official inquiry has been launched into the cause of the accident.
  - The country's official language is Spanish.
  - The film's official website is also very informative.
  - I intend to lodge an official complaint (= to complain to somebody in authority).

### [old-fashioned](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/old-fashioned) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌəʊld ˈfæʃnd/` · **US:** `/ˌəʊld ˈfæʃnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/old/old_f/old_fashioned_1_gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/old/old_f/old_fashioned__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - old-fashioned clothes/styles/methods/equipment
  - These sweets are still made in the old-fashioned way.
  - The Winnedumah Hotel has old-fashioned charm.
  - His clothes were curiously old-fashioned.
  - Critics regarded her films as hopelessly old-fashioned.
  - The whole room looked old-fashioned.
  - The hotel is quaint and old-fashioned.

### [once](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/once_2) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/wʌns/` · **US:** `/wʌns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/onc/once_/once__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/onc/once_/once__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We didn't know how we would cope once the money had gone.
  - The water is fine once you're in!

### [operation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/operation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɒpəˈreɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌɑːpəˈreɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ope/opera/operation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ope/opera/operation__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Will I need to have an operation?
  - He underwent a three-hour heart operation.
  - operation to do something He had an operation to remove a growth.
  - operation on somebody/something (to do something) an operation on her lung to remove a tumour
  - operation for something Doctors performed an emergency operation for appendicitis last night.

### [organized](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/organized) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɔːɡənaɪzd/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːrɡənaɪzd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/org/organ/organized__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/org/organ/organized__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an organized body of workers
  - an organized system of childcare
  - He was part of an organized criminal gang.
  - 39% of males stated that they regularly participated in organized sport compared with 17% of females.

### [organizer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/organizer) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɔːɡənaɪzə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːrɡənaɪzər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/org/organ/organizer__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/org/organ/organizer__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the organizers of the festival
  - an event organizer
  - The race organizers hope to raise money for charity.

### [original](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/original_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈrɪdʒənl/` · **US:** `/əˈrɪdʒənl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ori/origi/original__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ori/origi/original__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This painting is a copy; the original is in Madrid.
  - Send out the photocopies and keep the original.
  - At that price, you could have bought an original!
  - He copied paintings of famous artists and passed them off as originals.
  - Can you think of a movie remake that was better than the original?
  - The film stays romantically faithful to the 1963 original.
  - Most items in the collection are exact copies of the originals.

### [originally](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/originally) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈrɪdʒənəli/` · **US:** `/əˈrɪdʒənəli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ori/origi/originally__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ori/origi/originally__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The school was originally very small.
  - Originally, we had intended to go to Italy, but then we won the trip to Greece.
  - Laib originally planned to be a doctor.
  - She comes originally from York.
  - The dam project was originally conceived in 1977.
  - They originally thought she had a muscular injury.
  - Chloe hails originally from Texas.
  - The book was originally published in 1935.
  - The work was originally scheduled to begin in July.

### [ought](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ought-to) *(modal verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɔːt tu/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːt tu/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/aug/aught/aught__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/aug/aught/aught__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They ought to apologize.
  - They ought to have apologized (= but they didn't).
  - Such things ought not to be allowed.
  - He oughtn't to have been driving so fast.
  - (formal) ‘Ought I to write to say thank you?’ ‘Yes, I think you ought (to).’

### [ours](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ours) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈaʊəz/` · **US:** `/ˈaʊərz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/our/ours_/ours__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/our/ours_/ours__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Their house is very similar to ours, but ours is bigger.
  - No, those are Ellie's kids. Ours are upstairs.
  - He's a friend of ours.

### [outdoor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/outdoor) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈaʊtdɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈaʊtdɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/out/outdo/outdoor__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/out/outdo/outdoor__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - outdoor pursuits/recreation/activities
  - They sell camping equipment, hiking gear and outdoor clothing.
  - an outdoor swimming pool
  - I'm not really the outdoor type (= I prefer indoor activities).
  - a cafe with indoor and outdoor seating

### [outdoors](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/outdoors_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌaʊtˈdɔːz/` · **US:** `/ˌaʊtˈdɔːrz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/out/outdo/outdoors__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/out/outdo/outdoors__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The rain prevented them from eating outdoors.
  - The seeds may be sown outdoors in the spring.
  - It is often too cold to venture outdoors.
  - The children were encouraged to paint and draw both indoors and outdoors.


## P

### [pack](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pack_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pæk/` · **US:** `/pæk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pac/pack_/pack__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pac/pack_/pack__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - pack of something a pack of cigarettes/gum
  - You can buy the envelopes in packs of ten.
  - a family pack (= one big enough for a family) of crisps

### [package](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/package_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpækɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈpækɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pac/packa/package__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pac/packa/package__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A large package has arrived for you.

### [painful](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/painful) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpeɪnfl/` · **US:** `/ˈpeɪnfl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pai/painf/painful__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pai/painf/painful__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Is your back still painful?
  - a painful death
  - My ankle is still too painful to walk on.
  - He took a painful blow on the shoulder.
  - It was a difficult and painful operation.

### [pale](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pale_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/peɪl/` · **US:** `/peɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pal/pale_/pale__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pal/pale_/pale__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a pale complexion
  - She was tall with a slender figure and pale skin.
  - pale with fear/shock
  - to go/turn/grow pale
  - You look pale. Are you OK?
  - Her face had grown deathly pale.
  - The ordeal left her looking pale and drawn.

### [pan](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pan1_1#pan1_sng_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pæn/` · **US:** `/pæn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pan/pan__/pan__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pan/pan__/pan__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - pots and pans
  - a large stainless steel pan
  - Heat a large non-stick saute pan over a medium heat.
  - Remove the pan from the heat and add the sugar.
  - a nonstick pan

### [participate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/participate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/pɑːˈtɪsɪpeɪt/` · **US:** `/pɑːrˈtɪsɪpeɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/par/parti/participate__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/par/parti/participate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Anyone who wishes to participate is welcome.
  - participate in something She didn't participate in the discussion.
  - to participate in a study/programme
  - to participate in an activity/event
  - We encourage students to participate fully in the running of the college.
  - The women actively participated in the creation of the artwork.
  - Other countries were invited to participate in the project.
  - All employees at least 21 years old are eligible to participate.
  - Details of the competition are available at all participating stores.

### [particularly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/particularly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/pəˈtɪkjələli/` · **US:** `/pərˈtɪkjələrli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/par/parti/particularly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/par/parti/particularly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - particularly useful/important/interesting
  - I particularly like the colour of the walls.
  - The story focuses particularly on the character of Nelly.
  - Traffic is bad, particularly in the city centre.
  - I enjoyed the play, particularly the second half.
  - The task was not particularly (= not very) difficult.
  - ‘Did you enjoy it?’ ‘No, not particularly (= not very much).’
  - I don't particularly care (= I don't care very much) what happens to him.

### [pass](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pass_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pɑːs/` · **US:** `/pæs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pas/pass_/pass__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pas/pass_/pass__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a boarding pass (= for a plane)
  - There is no admittance without a security pass.
  - We had backstage passes to the show.

### [passion](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/passion) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpæʃn/` · **US:** `/ˈpæʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pas/passi/passion__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pas/passi/passion__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I admire the passion and commitment shown by the players.
  - with passion She argued her case with considerable passion.
  - a crime of passion (= caused by strong feelings of sexual jealousy)
  - Passions were running high (= people were angry and emotional) at the meeting.
  - He's a man of violent passions.

### [path](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/path) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pɑːθ/` · **US:** `/pæθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pat/path_/path__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pat/path_/path__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a dirt/gravel/concrete path
  - Follow the path through the woods.
  - along/down a path They walked along the cliff path to the next town.
  - The path led up a steep hill.
  - a coastal path

### [payment](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/payment) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpeɪmənt/` · **US:** `/ˈpeɪmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pay/payme/payment__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pay/payme/payment__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What method of payment do you prefer?
  - payment in cash/by cheque
  - They asked for payment in advance.
  - There will be a penalty for late payment of bills.
  - to receive/accept/demand/withhold payment
  - payment for something payment for goods/services
  - on payment of something The public has right of access, on payment of a fee.

### [peaceful](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/peaceful) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpiːsfl/` · **US:** `/ˈpiːsfl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pea/peace/peaceful__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pea/peace/peaceful__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a peaceful protest/demonstration
  - a peaceful resolution to the war
  - They hope for a peaceful settlement of the dispute.
  - The only way ahead was via diplomatic and peaceful means.
  - people of different religions living in peaceful coexistence
  - The time without her in the house had been blissfully peaceful.

### [percentage](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/percentage) *(noun)*
