- **Examples:**
  - He pushed against the rock with all his strength.
  - It may take a few weeks for you to build up your strength again.
  - She gradually regained strength in her legs.
  - He had a physical strength that matched his outward appearance.
  - strength to do something She didn't have the strength to walk any further.

### [string](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/string_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/strɪŋ/` · **US:** `/strɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/str/strin/string__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/str/strin/string__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a piece/length/ball of string
  - He wrapped the package in brown paper and tied it with string.
  - The key is hanging on a string by the door.

### [strongly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/strongly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈstrɒŋli/` · **US:** `/ˈstrɔːŋli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/str/stron/strongly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/str/stron/strongly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a strongly worded protest
  - He was strongly opposed to the idea.
  - This is an issue I feel strongly about.
  - This decision was strongly criticized by both sides.
  - Nearly all of them strongly agree that education is the key to success.
  - I strongly disagree with this statement.
  - We believe strongly in the importance of taking personal responsibility for our own actions.
  - We strongly urge the British government to take the necessary action.
  - I strongly suspect that this whole story is fictional.
  - The company says it strongly believes in the importance of organic agriculture.

### [studio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/studio) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈstjuːdiəʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈstuːdiəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/stu/studi/studio__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/stu/studi/studio__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a television/recording studio
  - a studio audience (= one in a studio, that can be seen or heard as a programme is broadcast)
  - She has released eight studio albums.

### [stuff](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stuff_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/stʌf/` · **US:** `/stʌf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/stu/stuff/stuff__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/stu/stuff/stuff__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What's all that sticky stuff on the carpet?
  - The chairs were covered in some sort of plastic stuff.
  - This hot sauce is good stuff.
  - I don't know how you can eat that stuff!
  - They sell stationery and stuff (like that).
  - Where's all my stuff (= my possessions)?
  - Could you move all that stuff off the table?
  - I want to buy some expensive tech stuff.

### [substance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/substance) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsʌbstəns/` · **US:** `/ˈsʌbstəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sub/subst/substance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sub/subst/substance__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a sticky substance
  - a chemical/radioactive/hazardous substance
  - Some frogs produce toxic substances in their skin.

### [successfully](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/successfully) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/səkˈsesfəli/` · **US:** `/səkˈsesfəli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/suc/succe/successfully__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/suc/succe/successfully__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She had already successfully completed these courses.
  - They successfully defended their championship title.
  - Companies have to be able to compete successfully in international markets.
  - This arrangement has operated successfully for the past six years.

### [sudden](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sudden) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsʌdn/` · **US:** `/ˈsʌdn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sud/sudde/sudden__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sud/sudde/sudden__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - News of his sudden and unexpected death came as a great shock.
  - a sudden change in temperature
  - There were several sudden bursts of gunfire outside.
  - It was only decided yesterday. It's all been very sudden.
  - Don't make any sudden movements.
  - His death was very sudden.

### [suffer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/suffer) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsʌfə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈsʌfər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/suf/suffe/suffer__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/suf/suffe/suffer__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I hate to see animals suffering.
  - The country has suffered greatly at the hands of its corrupt government.
  - suffer from something to suffer from a disorder/a disease/an illness/a condition
  - patients suffering from depression/cancer
  - Increasing numbers of children are suffering from mental health problems.
  - He suffers from asthma.

### [suit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/suit_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/suːt/` · **US:** `/suːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sui/suit_/suit__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sui/suit_/suit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - suit somebody/something If we met at two, would that suit you?
  - If you want to go by bus, that suits me fine.
  - Customers will be offered solutions that best suit their needs.
  - He can be very helpful, but only when it suits him.
  - A variety of dishes were available to suit all tastes.
  - it suits somebody to do something It suits me to start work at a later time.

### [suitable](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/suitable) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsuːtəbl/` · **US:** `/ˈsuːtəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sui/suita/suitable__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sui/suita/suitable__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a suitable candidate
  - a suitable habitat/location/site
  - suitable for something/somebody This programme is not suitable for children.
  - a suitable place for a picnic
  - The documentary is particularly suitable for classroom use.
  - suitable to do something I don't have anything suitable to wear for the party.
  - Would now be a suitable moment to discuss my report?

### [summarize](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/summarize) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsʌməraɪz/` · **US:** `/ˈsʌməraɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sum/summa/summarize__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sum/summa/summarize__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This essay briefly summarizes some of our approaches.
  - The results of the research are summarized at the end of the chapter.
  - To summarize, the main conclusions are as follows…

### [summary](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/summary_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsʌməri/` · **US:** `/ˈsʌməri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sum/summa/summary__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sum/summa/summary__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a news summary
  - summary of something The following is a summary of our conclusions.
  - What follows is a brief summary of the findings.
  - He has provided a useful summary of the main categories.
  - a two-page summary of a government report
  - The book provides useful summaries of the arguments.
  - in summary In summary, this was a disappointing performance.

### [supply](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/supply_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/səˈplaɪ/` · **US:** `/səˈplaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sup/suppl/supply__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sup/suppl/supply__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Advances in agriculture increased the food supply.
  - Demand for skilled labour outstrips supply.
  - We ordered a month's supply of oil.
  - We cannot guarantee adequate supplies of raw materials.

### [supply](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/supply_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/səˈplaɪ/` · **US:** `/səˈplaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sup/suppl/supply__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sup/suppl/supply__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - supply something to somebody/something The company supplied sports equipment to schools.
  - supply somebody/something with something The company supplied schools with sports equipment.
  - supply somebody/something The well stopped supplying water for the village many years ago.
  - foods supplying our daily vitamin needs
  - This information was kindly supplied by the manufacturer.
  - Please supply your email address.
  - He was charged with intent to supply (= planning to sell) class A drugs.

### [supporter](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/supporter) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/səˈpɔːtə(r)/` · **US:** `/səˈpɔːrtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sup/suppo/supporter__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sup/suppo/supporter__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a strong/loyal/staunch/ardent supporter
  - Labour supporters
  - The magazine is free to members and supporters.
  - About a hundred of his supporters rallied to demand justice.

### [surely](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/surely) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈʃɔːli/` · **US:** `/ˈʃʊrli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sur/surel/surely__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sur/surel/surely__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Surely we should do something about it?
  - It's surely only a matter of time before he is found, isn't it?
  - Surely by now, things could be organized better?
  - Oil prices will surely have some impact on trade.

### [surface](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/surface_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɜːfɪs/` · **US:** `/ˈsɜːrfɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sur/surfa/surface__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sur/surfa/surface__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We need a flat, smooth surface to play the game on.
  - an uneven road surface
  - a broad leaf with a large surface area
  - Surface water (= rainwater that has collected on the surface of a road) made driving conditions hazardous.
  - Teeth have a hard surface layer called enamel.

### [survive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/survive) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/səˈvaɪv/` · **US:** `/sərˈvaɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sur/survi/survive__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sur/survi/survive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was the last surviving member of the family.
  - Of the six people injured in the crash, only two survived.
  - Many of these teachers are struggling to survive financially.
  - He must betray his friend in order to survive.
  - The children had to survive by begging and stealing.
  - (humorous) ‘How are you these days?’ ‘Oh, surviving.’
  - Don't worry, it's only a scratch—you'll survive.
  - survive from something Some strange customs have survived from earlier times.
  - survive on something I can't survive on £40 a week (= it is not enough for my basic needs).
  - They spent two months in the jungle, surviving on small animals and fruit.
  - survive as something He survived as party leader until his second election defeat.

### [swim](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/swim_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/swɪm/` · **US:** `/swɪm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/swi/swim_/swim__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/swi/swim_/swim__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Let's go for a swim.
  - How about a quick swim before breakfast?
  - She got up early and went for a swim.
  - a sponsored swim

### [switch](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/switch_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/swɪtʃ/` · **US:** `/swɪtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/swi/switc/switch__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/swi/switc/switch__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - switch (over) to something We're in the process of switching over to a new system of invoicing.
  - switch (over) from something The company is offering good deals to customers who switch over from a competitor.
  - switch (over) from something to something He switched over from acting to journalism.
  - switch (over) If you'd like to pay monthly rather than annually, it's easy to switch over.
  - I found a better phone plan so I asked if I could switch.
  - switch between A and B Press these two keys to switch between documents on screen.
  - switch something (over) to something The meeting has been switched to next week.
  - switch something (over) from something It takes a few days to switch your account over from another bank.
  - switch something (over) from something to something He switched his allegiance from the British to the French.
  - I decided to switch my focus from swimming to cycling.
  - switch something over Your broadband will be switched over in less than a week.
  - switch something He switched sides halfway through the debate.

### [symptom](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/symptom) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɪmptəm/` · **US:** `/ˈsɪmptəm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sym/sympt/symptom__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sym/sympt/symptom__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Symptoms include a headache and sore throat.
  - If symptoms persist, consult your doctor.
  - symptom of something to experience symptoms of anxiety and depression
  - to show/develop symptoms of illness
  - to relieve/alleviate symptoms


## T

### [tail](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tail_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/teɪl/` · **US:** `/teɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tai/tail_/tail__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tai/tail_/tail__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The dog ran up, wagging its tail.
  - My dog loves to chase his tail.
  - The male has beautiful tail feathers.
  - It was black from its nose to the tip of its tail.

### [talent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/talent) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtælənt/` · **US:** `/ˈtælənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tal/talen/talent__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tal/talen/talent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The festival showcases the talent of young musicians.
  - to possess/have exceptional musical talent
  - The team is known for nurturing raw talent.
  - a man of many talents
  - talent for (doing) something She displayed her talent for comedy at the event.
  - She showed considerable talent for getting what she wanted.
  - a talent contest/competition (= in which people perform, to show how well they can sing, dance, etc.)

### [talented](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/talented) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtæləntɪd/` · **US:** `/ˈtæləntɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tal/talen/talented__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tal/talen/talented__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a talented player/musician/artist
  - Some of these young musicians are incredibly talented.

### [tape](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tape_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/teɪp/` · **US:** `/teɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tap/tape_/tape__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tap/tape_/tape__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - adhesive/sticky tape
  - Fit the carpet using double-sided tape.

### [tax](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tax_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tæks/` · **US:** `/tæks/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tax/tax__/tax__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tax/tax__/tax__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to pay your taxes
  - to raise/cut taxes
  - tax on something a 20% tax on income
  - in tax to pay over £1 000 in tax
  - before/after tax profits before/after tax
  - tax increases/cuts
  - an increase in tax rates
  - property/corporate tax revenue

### [tax](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tax_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/tæks/` · **US:** `/tæks/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tax/tax__/tax__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tax/tax__/tax__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - tax somebody/something His declared aim was to tax the rich.
  - Cigarettes are heavily taxed by the government.
  - Any interest payments are taxed as part of your income.
  - Luxury goods are taxed at a rate of 20%.
  - be taxed on something You will be taxed on all your income.

### [technical](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/technical) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈteknɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˈteknɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tec/techn/technical__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tec/techn/technical__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We offer free technical support for those buying our software.
  - The organization provides technical assistance to farmers.
  - technical skills/expertise.
  - technical education
  - technical drawing (= especially taught as a school subject)
  - This is a purely technical issue.

### [technique](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/technique) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tekˈniːk/` · **US:** `/tekˈniːk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tec/techn/technique__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tec/techn/technique__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - management techniques
  - modern surgical techniques
  - Researchers used advanced techniques to analyse the brain scans.
  - (formal) to employ/apply a technique
  - We have developed a new technique that corrects the problem.
  - technique for doing something Teachers learn various techniques for dealing with problem students.

### [tend](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tend) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/tend/` · **US:** `/tend/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/ten/tend_/tend__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/ten/tend_/tend__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Women tend to live longer than men.
  - People tend to be happier if they are in a long-term relationship.
  - When I'm tired, I tend to make mistakes.
  - It tends to get very cold here in the winter.
  - People tend to think that the problem will never affect them.
  - I tend to agree with many of the points you make.
  - I tend to focus on dance, but acting and singing are equally important to me.
  - Large class size will tend to increase the use of the lecture.

### [tent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tent) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tent/` · **US:** `/tent/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/ten/tent_/tent__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/ten/tent_/tent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to put up/take down a tent
  - to pitch (= put up) a tent
  - Food will be served in the hospitality tent (= for example at an outdoor show).
  - Everyone was sleeping outside in tents.

### [that](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/that_4) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ðæt/` · **US:** `/ðæt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tha/that_/that__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tha/that_/that__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I can't walk that far (= as far as that).
  - If they are that close, they will be here in minutes.
  - If only it were that simple.

### [theirs](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/theirs) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ðeəz/` · **US:** `/ðerz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/the/their/theirs__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/the/their/theirs__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Theirs are the children with very fair hair.
  - It's a favourite game of theirs.

### [theme](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/theme_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/θiːm/` · **US:** `/θiːm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/the/theme/theme__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/the/theme/theme__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the central/main/key/major theme
  - Births are a recurring theme in Leigh's work.
  - Hot temperatures were a common theme over the past couple of weeks.
  - theme of something The stories are all variations on the theme of unhappy marriage.
  - North American literature is the main theme of this year's festival.
  - The President stressed a favourite campaign theme—greater emphasis on education.
  - The naked male figure was always the central theme of Greek art.
  - Through sculptures Chen explored themes of illness, exile and cultural difference.

### [theory](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/theory) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈθɪəri/` · **US:** `/ˈθɪri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/the/theor/theory__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/the/theor/theory__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - theory of something the theory of evolution/relativity
  - scientific/economic theory
  - The theories were tested on a sample of the population.
  - theory about something He developed a new theory about the cause of stomach ulcers.
  - theory on something theories on human behaviour
  - theory behind something She has written a book explaining the theory behind her art.
  - according to a theory According to the theory of relativity, nothing can travel faster than light.

### [therefore](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/therefore) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈðeəfɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈðerfɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/the/there/therefore__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/the/there/therefore__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He's only 17 and therefore not eligible to vote.
  - There is still much to discuss. We shall, therefore, return to this item at our next meeting.

### [this](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/this_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ðɪs/` · **US:** `/ðɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/thi/this_/this__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/thi/this_/this__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's about this high (= as high as I am showing you with my hands).
  - I didn't think we'd get this far.
  - They never expected to be quite this successful.
  - I honestly can't remember when I last enjoyed a film this much.

### [though](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/though_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ðəʊ/` · **US:** `/ðəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tho/thoug/though__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tho/thoug/though__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Our team lost. It was a good game though.
  - ‘Have you ever been to Australia?’ ‘No. I'd like to, though.’
  - At first, it can all be a bit confusing. Don't worry though, my instructions page is here to help!
  - She wanted to talk to him. First, though, she had to talk to Clayton.

### [though](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/though_1) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/ðəʊ/` · **US:** `/ðəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tho/thoug/though__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tho/thoug/though__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Anne was fond of Tim, though he often annoyed her.
  - Though she gave no sign, I was sure she had seen me.
  - His clothes, though old and worn, looked clean and of good quality.
  - Strange though it may sound, I was pleased it was over.

### [throat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/throat) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/θrəʊt/` · **US:** `/θrəʊt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/thr/throa/throat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/thr/throa/throat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a sore throat
  - A sob caught in his throat.
  - He held the knife to her throat.
  - Their throats had been cut.
  - They caught a pig and Jack slit (= cut) its throat.
  - He quickly stuffed a hot dog down his throat.

### [throughout](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/throughout) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/θruːˈaʊt/` · **US:** `/θruːˈaʊt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/thr/throu/throughout__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/thr/throu/throughout__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They export their products to markets throughout the world.
  - The house was painted white throughout.

### [throughout](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/throughout) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/θruːˈaʊt/` · **US:** `/θruːˈaʊt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/thr/throu/throughout__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/thr/throu/throughout__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They export their products to markets throughout the world.
  - The house was painted white throughout.

### [tight](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tight_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/taɪt/` · **US:** `/taɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tig/tight/tight__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tig/tight/tight__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He kept a tight grip on her arm.
  - She twisted her hair into a tight knot.
  - The screw was so tight that it wouldn't move.
  - Dave's arms were tight around her waist.

### [till](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/till_1) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/tɪl/` · **US:** `/tɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/til/till_/till__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/til/till_/till__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We're open till 6 o'clock.
  - Can't you wait till we get home?
  - Just wait till you see it. It's great.

### [till](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/till_1) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/tɪl/` · **US:** `/tɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/til/till_/till__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/til/till_/till__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We're open till 6 o'clock.
  - Can't you wait till we get home?
  - Just wait till you see it. It's great.

### [tin](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tin) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tɪn/` · **US:** `/tɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tin/tin__/tin__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tin/tin__/tin__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Open the tin and drain the tuna.
  - tin of something a tin of beans/soup
  - Next, add two tins of tomatoes.
  - a sardine tin

### [tiny](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tiny) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtaɪni/` · **US:** `/ˈtaɪni/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tin/tiny_/tiny__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tin/tiny_/tiny__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a tiny baby
  - We come from a tiny little town in upstate New York.
  - Brenda felt a tiny bit bored.
  - Only a tiny minority hold such extreme views.
  - Most of the land is owned by a tiny fraction of the population.
  - Even a tiny amount of food may trigger an allergic reaction.
  - a tiny village/town/island

### [tip](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tip_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/tɪp/` · **US:** `/tɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tip/tip__/tip__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tip/tip__/tip__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Americans were always welcome because they tended to tip heavily.
  - tip somebody Did you remember to tip the waiter?
  - tip somebody something She tipped the porter a dollar.

### [toe](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/toe_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/təʊ/` · **US:** `/təʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/toe/toe__/toe__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/toe/toe__/toe__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the big/little toe (= the largest/smallest toe)
  - I stubbed my toe on the step.
  - Can you touch your toes (= by bending over while keeping your legs straight)?
  - She wiggled her fingers and toes thoughtfully.
  - on your toes She stood on her toes and kissed him.
  - They are ground birds with long toes and claws.

### [tongue](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tongue_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tʌŋ/` · **US:** `/tʌŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/ton/tongu/tongue__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/ton/tongu/tongue__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He clicked his tongue to attract their attention.
  - She ran her tongue over her lips.
  - It's very rude to stick your tongue out at people.

### [total](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/total_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtəʊtl/` · **US:** `/ˈtəʊtl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tot/total/total__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tot/total/total__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Their total cost was $18 000.
  - This brought the total number of accidents so far this year to 113.
  - The club has a total membership of 300.
  - the total profit/sales/revenue/income/expenditure/value
  - Try to cut down on the total amount of fat that you eat.
  - The total student population at Cambridge University is around 13,000 students.

### [total](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/total_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtəʊtl/` · **US:** `/ˈtəʊtl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tot/total/total__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tot/total/total__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a total of something You got 47 points on the written examination and 18 on the oral, making a total of 65.
  - His businesses are worth a combined total of $3 billion.
  - Out of a total of 15 games, they only won 2.
  - He was jailed for a total of six months.
  - in total The repairs came to over £500 in total (= including everything).
  - The number of employees has tripled, bringing the total to 400.
  - Rainfall totals for the month were below normal.
  - The UK had the largest share of the European art market - 56% of the total.

### [totally](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/totally) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtəʊtəli/` · **US:** `/ˈtəʊtəli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tot/total/totally__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tot/total/totally__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They come from totally different cultures.
  - I'm still not totally convinced that he knows what he's doing.
  - This behaviour is totally unacceptable.
  - He totally ignored us.
  - It's important to focus totally on what you're doing.
  - (especially North American English, informal) ‘She's so cute!’ ‘Totally!’ (= I agree)
  - (informal) It's a totally awesome experience.

### [touch](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/touch_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tʌtʃ/` · **US:** `/tʌtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tou/touch/touch__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tou/touch/touch__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the sense of touch
  - by touch They had to identify various materials by touch.

### [tour](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tour_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/tɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/tʊr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tou/tour_/tour__gb_5.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tou/tour_/tour__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - tour something He toured America with his one-man show.
  - She toured the country promoting her book.
  - (+ adv./prep.) We spent four weeks touring around Europe.
  - He's toured across Europe, the UK and North America.
  - She is currently touring with her new band.
  - He no longer tours.

### [trade](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/trade_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/treɪd/` · **US:** `/treɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tra/trade/trade__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tra/trade/trade__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - international/foreign/global/world trade
  - trade in something the international trade in oil
  - the illegal trade in wildlife
  - the arms/drugs trade
  - The Senate has approved a trade agreement with Latin American nations.
  - trade between/with somebody/something Trade between the two countries has increased.
  - an international trade barrier to GM food
  - Market prices and trade liberalization have increased prices.
  - trade negotiations/talks
  - Public support for the current trade policy is declining.
  - a bilateral trade deal

### [trade](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/trade_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/treɪd/` · **US:** `/treɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tra/trade/trade__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tra/trade/trade__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - trade in something The firm openly traded in arms.
  - trade with somebody Early explorers traded directly with the Indians.
  - trade in something with somebody Her family traded in textiles with the Far East.
  - trading partners (= countries that you trade with)
  - trade something Our products are now traded worldwide.
  - In 1900, more than 60% of cotton goods traded internationally were made in Lancashire.
  - trade something with somebody The turnover of agricultural goods traded with the US amounted to $45 million last year.

### [translate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/translate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/trænzˈleɪt/` · **US:** `/trænzˈleɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tra/trans/translate__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tra/trans/translate__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - translate something into something He translated the letter into English.
  - Her books have been translated into 24 languages.
  - Can you help me translate this legal jargon into plain English?
  - translate something from something This chapter was translated from the French by Oliver Breen.
  - translate something from something into something His works have been translated from French into countless languages.
  - translate something as something ‘Suisse’ had been wrongly translated as ‘Sweden’.
  - translate (something) The novel has been widely translated.
  - I don't speak Greek so Dina offered to translate for me.
  - translate from something My work involves translating from German.
  - translate into something My father dictated in Polish while I translated into English.
  - translate from something into something I spoke to him through my friend who translated from Arabic into English.

### [translation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/translation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/trænzˈleɪʃn/` · **US:** `/trænzˈleɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tra/trans/translation__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tra/trans/translation__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an error in translation
  - translation from something into something He specializes in translation from Danish into English.
  - translation from something The request must have lost something in the translation from the French.
  - translation into something Several books are already in translation into other languages.
  - translation into something The poems do not survive the translation into English.
  - translation of something (into something) The systems are used for the online translation of text.
  - in translation The book loses something in translation.
  - The irony is lost in translation.
  - There will be simultaneous translation in English and Chinese.

### [transport](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/transport_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/trænˈspɔːt/` · **US:** `/trænˈspɔːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tra/trans/transport__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tra/trans/transport__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to transport goods/passengers
  - transport something/somebody + adv./prep. He collapsed and was immediately transported to hospital.
  - Most of our luggage was transported by sea.
  - Cattle could be transported by rail to any part of the country.

### [treat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/treat_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/triːt/` · **US:** `/triːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tre/treat/treat__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tre/treat/treat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - treat somebody/something with something to treat people with respect
  - to treat people with caution/suspicion/dignity
  - Treat your keyboard with care and it should last for years.
  - treat somebody/something + adv./prep. All candidates will be treated equally, regardless of age.
  - They treat their animals quite badly.
  - She felt unfairly treated.
  - treat somebody/something like something My parents still treat me like a child.
  - treat somebody/something as something He was treated as a hero on his release from prison.
  - treat somebody/something as if… They treat him as if he weren't even there.

### [treatment](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/treatment) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtriːtmənt/` · **US:** `/ˈtriːtmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tre/treat/treatment__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tre/treat/treatment__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The drug is used in the treatment of depression.
  - treatment for something He is receiving treatment for shock.
  - She has been undergoing cancer treatment.
  - He may have to seek treatment abroad.
  - These bites rarely require treatment.
  - Two motorists needed hospital treatment following the accident.
  - The centre provides treatment to young drug addicts.
  - She is responding well to treatment.
  - to require hospital/medical treatment
  - No effective treatment exists for this highly infectious disease.
  - There are various treatments available for this condition.
  - Guests at the health spa receive a range of beauty treatments.

### [trend](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/trend_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/trend/` · **US:** `/trend/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tre/trend/trend__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tre/trend/trend__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - social/economic/political/demographic trends
  - fashion/market trends
  - trend towards something There is a growing trend towards later retirement.
  - trend in something The article discusses current trends in language teaching
  - This downward trend in sales is likely to continue.
  - There has been an upward trend in the number of accidents reported.
  - Predicting long-term trends in consumer behaviour is more difficult.
  - trend for something A trend for romance and nostalgia has emerged.
  - trend for somebody/something to do something There is an increasing trend for students to live at home.
  - You seem to have set (= started) a new trend.
  - This trend is being reversed (= is going in the opposite direction).
  - One region is attempting to buck (= oppose or resist) the trend of economic decline.
  - Inflation continued its downward trend last month.
  - Regional growth figures are following the national trend.
  - The number of visitors to the city is showing an upward trend.
  - The underlying trend of inflation is still upwards.
  - If this trend continues, the species could soon become extinct.
  - on trend Linen is on trend (= fashionable) again this summer.

### [trick](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/trick_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/trɪk/` · **US:** `/trɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tri/trick/trick__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tri/trick/trick__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They had to think of a trick to get past the guards.
  - The kids are always playing tricks on their teacher.

### [trick](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/trick_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/trɪk/` · **US:** `/trɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tri/trick/trick__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tri/trick/trick__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - trick somebody I'd been tricked and I felt stupid.
  - trick your way + adv./prep. He managed to trick his way past the security guards.

### [truth](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/truth) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/truːθ/` · **US:** `/truːθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tru/truth/truth__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tru/truth/truth__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Do you think she's telling the truth?
  - We are determined to get at (= discover) the truth.
  - She had only spoken the truth.
  - So now you know the truth.
  - I knew the truth would come out in the end.
  - the truth is (that)… The truth is that there are no easy answers.
  - The truth of the matter is we can’t afford to keep all the staff on.
  - The sad truth is that, at 72, he is past his prime.
  - The simple truth is that new roads just encourage more traffic.
  - the truth about something I don't think you are telling me the whole truth about what happened.
  - These documents reveal the truth about his past.
  - The awful truth about his disappearance finally dawned on her.
  - the truth behind something Only recently has the truth behind the killings emerged.
  - It’s the gospel truth! (= completely true)
  - I honestly don't know, and that's the truth.

### [tube](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tube) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tjuːb/` · **US:** `/tuːb/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tub/tube_/tube__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tub/tube_/tube__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He had to be fed through a feeding tube for several months.
  - She poured the liquid down the tube.

### [type](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/type_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/taɪp/` · **US:** `/taɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/typ/type_/type__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/typ/type_/type__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - How fast can you type?
  - typing errors
  - type something I typed my name and password.
  - type something in Type (in) the filename, then press ‘Return’.
  - type something out This letter will need to be typed (out) again.
  - type something up Have you typed up that report yet?

### [typically](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/typically) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtɪpɪkli/` · **US:** `/ˈtɪpɪkli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/typ/typic/typically__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/typ/typic/typically__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The standard chips are typically used for databases and other business software.
  - Generating solar power has typically involved higher capital costs.
  - The Bengal tiger is typically found in the forests of Central and Southern India.
  - The factory typically produces 500 chairs a week.
  - A typically priced meal will be around $10.
  - Typically, the contracts were for five years.
  - Pears are available year-round, but they are typically associated with autumn.
  - It typically takes several years of losses before you see any profits.

### [tyre](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tyre) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtaɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈtaɪər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tyr/tyre_/tyre__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tyr/tyre_/tyre__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a front/rear tyre
  - to pump up a tyre
  - a flat tyre
  - Someone had slashed the tyres on her car.
  - Remember to check your tyre pressure regularly.
  - a burst/punctured tyre
  - bald/worn tyres
  - car/bicycle tyres
  - He drove off with a screech of tyres.
  - I need him to show me how to change a tyre.
  - Keeping your tyres inflated to the proper pressure can improve mileage.


## U

### [ugly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ugly) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈʌɡli/` · **US:** `/ˈʌɡli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/ugl/ugly_/ugly__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/ugl/ugly_/ugly__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an ugly face
  - an ugly building
  - an ugly scar
  - The witch was hideously ugly.
  - Why do some of our government buildings look ugly?

### [unable](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/unable) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ʌnˈeɪbl/` · **US:** `/ʌnˈeɪbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/una/unabl/unable__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/una/unabl/unable__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a former soldier who has been unable to find work since the war ended
  - They have been unable or unwilling to resolve the conflict.
  - I was unable to attend the meeting, but sent my apologies.
  - Unable to get a taxi, she heads for the bus stop.
  - His family were unable to afford the cost of the school uniform.
  - The company is losing money and is unable to pay its bills.
  - Ageing power stations may be unable to cope with ever-increasing demand for electricity.
  - He lay there, unable to move.
  - I tried to contact him but was unable to.
  - I am afraid I am unable to provide the information you have requested.
  - She was unable to hide her excitement.
  - Let us know if you’re unable to come.
  - The Minister seems unable to grasp this simple point.
  - Christine feels unable to confide in her fiancé.
  - The accident left him unable to walk.

### [uncomfortable](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/uncomfortable) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ʌnˈkʌmfətəbl/` · **US:** `/ʌnˈkʌmfərtəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/unc/uncom/uncomfortable__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/unc/uncom/uncomfortable__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - uncomfortable shoes
  - uncomfortable to do something The headphones can be uncomfortable to wear.
  - I couldn't sleep because the bed was so uncomfortable.
  - I find these chairs incredibly uncomfortable.

### [underwear](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/underwear) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈʌndəweə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈʌndərwer/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/und/under/underwear__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/und/under/underwear__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I never wear underwear.
  - clean/thermal underwear
  - She packed one change of underwear.
  - Some mothers can't even afford to buy underwear for their kids.
  - in your underwear He was getting cold standing around in his underwear.

### [unemployed](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/unemployed) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌʌnɪmˈplɔɪd/` · **US:** `/ˌʌnɪmˈplɔɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/une/unemp/unemployed__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/une/unemp/unemployed__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - How long have you been unemployed?
  - unemployed workers/people
  - I've been unemployed for 5 months.
  - training for unemployed youth
  - an unemployed miner/labourer/builder
  - an increase in the number of people registered as unemployed
  - Thousands of labourers became unemployed with no alternative employment.

### [unemployment](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/unemployment) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌʌnɪmˈplɔɪmənt/` · **US:** `/ˌʌnɪmˈplɔɪmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/une/unemp/unemployment__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/une/unemp/unemployment__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an area of high/low unemployment
  - rising unemployment
  - It was a time of mass unemployment.
  - Government schemes to combat youth unemployment
  - the level/rate of unemployment
  - The unemployment rate rose to 4.7 per cent.
  - measures to help reduce/tackle unemployment
  - The point is that this type of economics causes unemployment.
  - unemployment figures/statistics
  - a rise in both inflation and unemployment
  - He applied for unemployment insurance and was approved.

### [unfair](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/unfair) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌʌnˈfeə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˌʌnˈfer/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/unf/unfai/unfair__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/unf/unfai/unfair__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They had been given an unfair advantage.
  - We hope the authorities will move to end such unfair practices.
  - unfair dismissal (= a situation in which somebody is illegally dismissed from their job)
  - It's so unfair!
  - unfair on somebody I was working really long hours, which was unfair on my wife and my kids.
  - it is unfair to do something It would be unfair not to let you have a choice.
  - it is unfair to/on somebody to do something It seems unfair on him to make him pay for everything.
  - unfair to somebody I don't want to be unfair to anyone, so you'll all get a chance.
  - it is unfair for somebody to do something Most Americans think it is unfair for the government to take more than 25% of anyone's income in taxes.
  - it is unfair that… They say it's grossly unfair that consumers are having to pay more now.
  - unfair criticism
  - Life seems so unfair sometimes.

### [union](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/union) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈjuːniən/` · **US:** `/ˈjuːniən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/uni/union/union__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/uni/union/union__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I've joined the union.
  - Teachers' unions in England are demanding the same improvements as in Scotland.
  - a union leader/official/representative/activist
  - the right to form a union
  - Do you belong to a union?
  - The union represents five million workers.
  - public sector unions
  - Greece's main workers' union
  - a union member
  - a member of a union
  - a fall in union membership

### [unless](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/unless) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/ənˈles/` · **US:** `/ənˈles/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/unl/unles/unless__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/unl/unles/unless__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You won't get paid for time off unless you have a doctor's note.
  - I won't tell them—not unless you say I can.
  - Unless I'm mistaken, she was back at work yesterday.
  - He hasn't got any hobbies—unless you call watching TV a hobby.

### [unlike](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/unlike_1) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/ˌʌnˈlaɪk/` · **US:** `/ˌʌnˈlaɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/unl/unlik/unlike__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/unl/unlik/unlike__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Music is quite unlike any other art form.
  - The sound was not unlike that of birds singing.

### [unlikely](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/unlikely) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ʌnˈlaɪkli/` · **US:** `/ʌnˈlaɪkli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/unl/unlik/unlikely__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/unl/unlik/unlikely__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - unlikely to do something The project seemed unlikely to succeed.
  - Of course, such an event is very unlikely to happen.
  - it is unlikely (that…) It was highly unlikely that the gunshot wound was self-inflicted.
  - It is extremely unlikely that the new owner would want to make people redundant.
  - It looks unlikely that prices will fall.
  - It's most (= very) unlikely that she'll arrive before seven.
  - Problems with the evidence made it unlikely that he would ever be prosecuted.
  - In the unlikely event of a problem arising, please contact the hotel manager.

### [unnecessary](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/unnecessary) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ʌnˈnesəsəri/` · **US:** `/ʌnˈnesəseri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/unn/unnec/unnecessary__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/unn/unnec/unnecessary__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They were found guilty of causing unnecessary suffering to animals.
  - All this fuss is totally unnecessary.
  - it is unnecessary to do something It was unnecessary to carry out more stringent safety testing.
  - it is unnecessary for somebody/something to do something They argued that it was unnecessary for government to regulate the industry.
  - Don't take any unnecessary risks.
  - Cigarette smoking causes 450,000 unnecessary deaths every year.
  - unnecessary expense
  - Antibiotics should be unnecessary for most patients with a sore throat.

### [unpleasant](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/unpleasant) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ʌnˈpleznt/` · **US:** `/ʌnˈpleznt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/unp/unple/unpleasant__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/unp/unple/unpleasant__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an unpleasant experience/surprise/task
  - unpleasant to do something The minerals in the water made it unpleasant to drink.
  - It can be unpleasant to sit next to a group of people arguing.
  - unpleasant for somebody to do something It must have been unpleasant for her neighbours to be exposed to the constant loud music.
  - Torrential made conditions unpleasant for both players and spectators.
  - What then took place was a deeply unpleasant attack.
  - an unpleasant truth/fact/reality/consequence
  - an unpleasant feeling/sensation/memory
  - an unpleasant smell/odour/taste

### [update](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/update_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈʌpdeɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈʌpdeɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/upd/updat/update__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/upd/updat/update__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They will send you regular updates by email.
  - a news/weather update
  - update on something Now let's get an update on our other big story of the day.
  - The police will give an update on the progress of the investigation tomorrow.
  - The committee agreed to provide an update on the situation at the school
  - Stand by for an update from our White House correspondent.
  - We are expecting an update from the hospital this morning.
  - We will bring you more updates as soon as possible

### [update](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/update_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌʌpˈdeɪt/` · **US:** `/ˌʌpˈdeɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/upd/updat/update__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/upd/updat/update__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an updated version of the app
  - It's about time we updated our software.

### [upon](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/upon) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/əˈpɒn/` · **US:** `/əˈpɑːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/upo/upon_/upon__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/upo/upon_/upon__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The decision was based upon two considerations.

### [upset](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/upset_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌʌpˈset/` · **US:** `/ˌʌpˈset/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/ups/upset/upset__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/ups/upset/upset__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I understand how upset you must be feeling.
  - upset about/at/over something There's no point getting upset about it.
  - upset about/at/over doing something He was really upset over not getting the present he wanted from Father Christmas.
  - I was upset at missing all the excitement.
  - upset (that)… She was upset that he had left without saying goodbye.
  - upset with somebody I think she may be a bit upset with you.

### [upset](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/upset_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ʌpˈset/` · **US:** `/ʌpˈset/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/ups/upset/upset__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/ups/upset/upset__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - upset somebody/yourself This decision is likely to upset a lot of people.
  - Try not to let him upset you.
  - Don't upset yourself about it—let's just forget it ever happened.
  - He had clearly been upset by the incident.
  - it upsets somebody that… It upset him that nobody had bothered to tell him about it.
  - it upsets somebody to do something It upsets me to think of her all alone in that big house.

### [used](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/used1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/juːst/` · **US:** `/juːst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/use/used_/used__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/use/used_/used__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - used to doing something I'm not used to eating so much at lunchtime.
  - used to somebody/something doing something He was used to people asking him for autographs.
  - I'm used to things going wrong in my life.
  - I'm so used to answering this question, sometimes it's automatic.
  - used to somebody/something I found the job tiring at first but I soon got used to it.
  - Maybe I just need some time to get used to the idea of being a mother
  - She had gradually grown used to him.


## V

### [valuable](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/valuable) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvæljuəbl/` · **US:** `/ˈvæljuəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/val/valua/valuable__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/val/valua/valuable__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - My home is my most valuable asset.
  - She claims hospitals waste valuable resources by admitting patients days before surgery.
  - valuable antiques
  - Luckily, nothing valuable was stolen.

### [value](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/value_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvæljuː/` · **US:** `/ˈvæljuː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/val/value/value__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/val/value/value__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to go up/rise/increase in value
  - to go down/fall/decline in value
  - rising property values
  - The value of the shares has continued to fall.
  - to the value of something The winner will receive a prize to the value of £1 000.
  - of value Don't leave anything of value in the car.
  - Sports cars tend to hold their value well.

### [various](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/various) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈveəriəs/` · **US:** `/ˈværiəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/var/vario/various__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/var/vario/various__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - various types/forms/kinds of somebody/something
  - We talked about jobs, family, football and various other things.
  - documentaries that explored various aspects of life in Germany
  - Tents come in various shapes and sizes.
  - She took the job for various reasons.
  - There are various ways of doing this.
  - He spent his childhood in various parts of Italy.
  - Various groups use the centre.
  - Data will be collected at various stages.
  - My films involve various different points of view.
  - Members of the various political parties were calling for something to be done.
  - (formal) His complaints were many and various.

### [version](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/version) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvɜːʃn/` · **US:** `/ˈvɜːrʒn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/ver/versi/version__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/ver/versi/version__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - version of something the latest version of the software package
  - an expanded version of the article
  - The original/final version is vastly superior.
  - a new/different/modified/revised version
  - an earlier/a previous version
  - an updated version of the app
  - a print/an online version
  - to create/produce/offer/release/use a version
  - There are two versions of the game, a long one and a short one.
  - the DVD version of the film
  - This amended version of the bill went to the Senate.
  - an edited version of her speech to the National Press Club in Canberra
  - the full version of the report
  - the de luxe/luxury version
  - I've only played the version for PC.
  - This is an edited version of the review.

### [victim](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/victim) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvɪktɪm/` · **US:** `/ˈvɪktɪm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vic/victi/victim__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vic/victi/victim__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - shooting/murder victims
  - flood/crash/accident victims
  - an innocent/unsuspecting victim
  - victims of crime/abuse/violence
  - the alleged victim of a serious assault
  - Several countries have pledged millions of dollars to help the victims of the tsunami.
  - The team will try to identify potential victims of domestic violence.
  - flood/disaster/earthquake/hurricane victims
  - cancer/stroke/heart attack victims
  - families/relatives of the victims
  - expressions of sympathy for the victims and their families
  - He made a victim impact statement to the court.

### [view](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/view_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/vjuː/` · **US:** `/vjuː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vie/view_/view__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vie/view_/view__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - view somebody/something + adv./prep. How do you view your position within the company?
  - The plan was not viewed favourably by the public.
  - view somebody/something as something to view something as a threat/an opportunity
  - He is widely viewed as a possible leader.
  - When the car was first built, the design was viewed as highly original.
  - view somebody/something with something She viewed him with suspicion.
  - You should view their offer with a great deal of caution.

### [viewer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/viewer) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvjuːə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈvjuːər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vie/viewe/viewer__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vie/viewe/viewer__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The programme attracted millions of viewers.
  - British television viewers saw him this week in a BBC documentary.
  - The average American TV viewer spends five hours a day watching the small screen.
  - an attempt to attract younger viewers
  - Her performance has grabbed the attention of 100 million YouTube viewers.
  - viewer of something a regular viewer of the show

### [violent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/violent) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvaɪələnt/` · **US:** `/ˈvaɪələnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vio/viole/violent__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vio/viole/violent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - violent crime/criminals
  - violent protests/attacks/incidents
  - Students were involved in violent clashes with the police.
  - The crowd suddenly turned violent.
  - violent towards/to somebody Was he ever violent towards you?
  - violent acts/behaviour
  - He met with a violent death (= he was murdered, killed in a fight, etc.).
  - They don't allow their kids to play violent video games (= that show a lot of violence).
  - Their father was a violent man.

### [volunteer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/volunteer_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌvɒlənˈtɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˌvɑːlənˈtɪr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vol/volun/volunteer__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vol/volun/volunteer__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Schools need volunteers to help children to read.
  - She does volunteer work at an orphanage.
  - volunteer for/with something She was a volunteer for the Red Cross before training as a nurse.
  - Volunteers from various organizations are working to save the lives of the earthquake victims.
  - a volunteer firefighter
  - volunteer helpers/carers
  - The Centre is run by a group of dedicated volunteers.

### [volunteer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/volunteer_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌvɒlənˈtɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˌvɑːlənˈtɪr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vol/volun/volunteer__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vol/volun/volunteer__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He has been volunteering for 11 years now.
  - volunteer to do something Jill volunteered to organize a petition.
  - I volunteered to help clean up the beach.
  - volunteer for something Several staff members volunteered for early retirement.
  - volunteer as something He volunteered as a translator for Spanish-speaking patients.
  - volunteer something Workers volunteer their time, as no funds are available to pay professional staff.
  - volunteer something as something He volunteered his services as a driver.
  - volunteer something for something She has been volunteering her computer skills for the foundation.

### [vote](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/vote_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/vəʊt/` · **US:** `/vəʊt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vot/vote_/vote__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vot/vote_/vote__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to win/lose votes
  - You can cast your vote at the local polling station.
  - He took 53% of the votes in the election.
  - They've started opening the ballot boxes and counting the votes.
  - No candidate gained a majority of the votes in the first round.
  - The Tory candidate received only 4203 votes.
  - vote for/against somebody/something There were 21 votes for and 17 against the motion, with 2 abstentions.
  - The motion was passed by 6 votes to 3.
  - The Green candidate won over 3 000 of the 14 000 votes cast.

### [vote](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/vote_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/vəʊt/` · **US:** `/vəʊt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vot/vote_/vote__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vot/vote_/vote__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - How did you vote at the last election?
  - Only about half of the electorate bothered to vote.
  - vote for/against somebody/something to vote for a candidate/party/bill
  - Did you vote for or against her?
  - vote in favour of something Over 60% of members voted in favour of (= for) the motion.
  - vote on something We'll listen to the arguments on both sides and then vote on it.
  - About 1.8 million people voted in the election.
  - vote something We voted Democrat in the last election.
  - to vote yes/no
  - vote to do something The board voted unanimously not to file for bankruptcy.
  - The Senate voted overwhelmingly to retain sanctions.
  - Parliament voted to set up an independent inquiry into the matter.
  - vote that… The meeting voted that the town council should reject the scheme.
  - Everyone over 18 has the right to vote.
  - to be registered/eligible to vote


## W

### [warm](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/warm_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/wɔːm/` · **US:** `/wɔːrm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/war/warm_/warm__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/war/warm_/warm__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - warm something/somebody/yourself Come in and warm yourself by the fire.
  - The alcohol warmed and relaxed him.
  - warm something/somebody/yourself up I'll warm up some milk.
  - There is little doubt that the planet is warming.
  - warm up As the climate warms up, the ice caps will melt.
  - We soon warmed up in front of the fire.
  - warm through Return the bowl to the heat to warm through.
  - warm something through Poach until the fish is warmed through.

### [warn](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/warn) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/wɔːn/` · **US:** `/wɔːrn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/war/warn_/warn__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/war/warn_/warn__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - warn somebody I tried to warn him, but he wouldn't listen.
  - If you're thinking of getting a dog, be warned—they take a lot of time and money.
  - warn about/against somebody/something Security experts warned about the problems months ago.
  - warn somebody about/against somebody/something He warned us against pickpockets.
  - warn somebody of something Officials warned the pilot of an anonymous threat.
  - He has been warned of the danger he is in.
  - warn of something Police have warned of possible delays.
  - warn somebody about something He is warning youngsters about the dangers of fireworks.
  - warn that… Aid agencies have repeatedly warned that a humanitarian catastrophe is imminent.
  - warn somebody that… She was warned that if she did it again she would lose her job.
  - warn somebody what, how, etc… I had been warned what to expect.
  - warn (somebody) + speech ‘Beware of pickpockets,’ she warned (him).

### [warning](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/warning_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwɔːnɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈwɔːrnɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/war/warni/warning__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/war/warni/warning__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I had absolutely no warning.
  - to get fair/advance/adequate warning
  - warning against doing something Doctors issued a warning against eating any fish caught in the river.
  - warning against somebody/something The World Health Organization repeated its warning against non-essential travel to the island
  - The bridge collapsed without (any) warning.
  - Let me give you a word of warning.
  - warning about somebody/something Many people continue to ignore warnings about the dangers of sunbathing.
  - warning about doing something The report contained dire warnings about eating too much fat and salt.
  - warning of something There was little warning of the coming disaster.
  - warning to somebody Their deaths would serve as a warning to others.
  - warning that… The union failed to heed warnings that strike action would lead to the closure of the factory.
  - Smoke detectors will sound an alarm to give you a warning that there is a fire in the house
  - warning to do something She went ahead with the interview, despite warnings not to speak to foreign journalists.
  - a flood/tornado warning

### [waste](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/waste_3) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/weɪst/` · **US:** `/weɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/was/waste/waste__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/was/waste/waste__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the disposal of waste material
  - Waste water is pumped from the factory into a nearby river.

### [waste](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/waste_1) *(noun)*
