- **UK:** `/weɪst/` · **US:** `/weɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/was/waste/waste__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/was/waste/waste__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I hate unnecessary waste.
  - It seems such a waste to throw good food away.
  - I hate to see good food go to waste (= be thrown away).
  - waste of something The report is critical of the department's waste of resources.
  - What a waste of paper!

### [waste](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/waste_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/weɪst/` · **US:** `/weɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/was/waste/waste__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/was/waste/waste__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - waste something Stop wasting time and just get on with it!
  - to waste food/energy/resources
  - waste something on something Why waste money on clothes you don't need?
  - We got straight down to business without wasting time on small talk.
  - waste something (in) doing something She wasted no time in rejecting the offer (= she rejected it immediately).
  - You're wasting your time trying to explain it to him (= because he will not understand).

### [water](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/water_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwɔːtə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈwɔːtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wat/water/water__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wat/water/water__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to water the plants/garden

### [wave](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wave_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/weɪv/` · **US:** `/weɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wav/wave_/wave__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wav/wave_/wave__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The people on the bus waved and we waved back.
  - wave at/to somebody Why did you wave at him?
  - He waved to the crowd as they greeted him.
  - wave something The driver leaped out, waving his fist and swearing.
  - wave something about/around A man in the water was shouting and waving his arms around frantically.
  - wave something at somebody She waved her hand dismissively at the housekeeper.
  - wave somebody something My mother was crying as I waved her goodbye.
  - wave something to somebody My mother was crying as I waved goodbye to her.

### [weapon](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/weapon) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwepən/` · **US:** `/ˈwepən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wea/weapo/weapon__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wea/weapo/weapon__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Modern nuclear weapons are much more destructive than either biological or chemical weapons.
  - a lethal/deadly weapon
  - The police still haven't found the murder weapon.
  - Police believed the men were carrying weapons.
  - She was convicted of possessing an offensive weapon.
  - Two officers fired their weapons.
  - with a weapon A lone gunman opened fire with an automatic weapon.

### [weigh](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/weigh) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/weɪ/` · **US:** `/weɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wei/weigh/weigh__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wei/weigh/weigh__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - How much do you weigh (= how heavy are you)?
  - She weighs 60 kilos.
  - A healthy baby usually weighs 6 lbs or more.
  - The average male tiger weighs around 200 kg.
  - The diamond weighs 4.50 carats.
  - (informal) These cases weigh a ton (= are very heavy).

### [western](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/western_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwestən/` · **US:** `/ˈwestərn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wes/weste/western__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wes/weste/western__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - western Spain
  - Western Europe
  - the western slopes of the mountain
  - all over the western hemisphere
  - Economic growth in China's western regions

### [whatever](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/whatever_1) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/wɒtˈevə(r)/` · **US:** `/wətˈevər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wha/whate/whatever__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wha/whate/whatever__us_3_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Take whatever action is needed.
  - Do whatever you like.

### [whatever](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/whatever_1) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/wɒtˈevə(r)/` · **US:** `/wətˈevər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wha/whate/whatever__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wha/whate/whatever__us_3_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Take whatever action is needed.
  - Do whatever you like.

### [whenever](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/whenever_1) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/wenˈevə(r)/` · **US:** `/wenˈevər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/whe/whene/whenever__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/whe/whene/whenever__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You can ask for help whenever you need it.

### [whether](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/whether) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/ˈweðə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈweðər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/whe/wheth/whether__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/whe/wheth/whether__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He seemed undecided whether to go or stay.
  - It remains to be seen whether or not this idea can be put into practice.
  - I asked him whether he had done it all himself or whether someone had helped him.
  - I'll see whether she's at home (= or not at home).
  - It's doubtful whether there'll be any seats left.

### [while](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/while_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/waɪl/` · **US:** `/waɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/whi/while/while__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/whi/while/while__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - for a while I only stayed for a short while.
  - They chatted for a while.
  - I haven't seen him for quite a while (= a fairly long time).
  - He is fitter now than he has been for a long while.
  - after a while After a while John started to relax a bit.
  - in a while I'll be back in a little while (= a short time).
  - It took me a while (= a fairly long time) to realize what he meant.
  - This could take a while (= a long time), so be patient.
  - They walked back together, talking all the while (= all the time).
  - for the while There's no need to do anything for the while (= for a short time).
  - He left a while ago.
  - The problems started a while back.

### [whole](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/whole_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/həʊl/` · **US:** `/həʊl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/who/whole/whole__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/who/whole/whole__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The scheme would cover the whole of the UK.
  - The effects will last for the whole of his life.

### [will](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/will_4) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wɪl/` · **US:** `/wɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wil/will_/will__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wil/will_/will__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to have a strong will
  - to have an iron will/a will of iron
  - Her decision to continue shows great strength of will.
  - The matter became a battle of wills between the king and the barons.
  - The meeting turned out to be a clash of wills.
  - will to do something The government lacked the political will to reform the tax system.
  - In spite of what happened, he never lost the will to live.

### [win](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/win_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wɪn/` · **US:** `/wɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/win/win__/win__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/win/win__/win__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - two wins and three defeats
  - a comfortable/an easy win
  - a home/an away win
  - The team are in hot form with nine straight wins (= nine wins, one after another, without any losses).
  - consecutive/successive wins
  - They have not had a win so far this season.
  - win over somebody France swept to a 6–2 win over Denmark.
  - win against somebody People still talk about the famous win against Brazil.
  - (figurative) The company is scoring big wins with new products.

### [wing](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wing_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wɪŋ/` · **US:** `/wɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/win/wing_/wing__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/win/wing_/wing__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The swan flapped its wings noisily.
  - wing feathers

### [within](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/within_1) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/wɪˈðɪn/` · **US:** `/wɪˈðɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wit/withi/within__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wit/withi/within__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You should receive a reply within seven days.
  - The ambulance arrived within minutes of the call being made.
  - Two elections were held within the space of a year.

### [wonder](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wonder_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwʌndə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈwʌndər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/won/wonde/wonder__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/won/wonde/wonder__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He retained a childlike sense of wonder.
  - in wonder She gazed down in wonder at the city spread below her.
  - His eyes were filled with wonder at the sights before him.

### [wonder](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wonder_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwʌndə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈwʌndər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/won/wonde/wonder__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/won/wonde/wonder__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘Why do you want to know?’ ‘No particular reason. I was just wondering.’
  - wonder who, where, etc… I wonder who she is.
  - I was just beginning to wonder where you were.
  - You have to wonder just what he sees in her.
  - I can't help wondering whether I'm missing something here.
  - Have you ever wondered what your cat really thinks of you?
  - I always wondered why you never got married.
  - I'm still wondering how that could have happened.
  - I sometimes wonder where all this is going to end.
  - I often wonder what became of him.
  - wonder about somebody/something We were wondering about next April for the wedding.
  - + speech ‘Where's Natasha?’ he wondered aloud.

### [wool](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wool) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wʊl/` · **US:** `/wʊl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/woo/wool_/wool__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/woo/wool_/wool__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Sheep were kept for their wool and meat.

### [worldwide](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/worldwide) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌwɜːldˈwaɪd/` · **US:** `/ˌwɜːrldˈwaɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/world/worldwide__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/world/worldwide__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an increase in worldwide sales
  - The show has a worldwide audience of 115 million viewers.
  - a worldwide terrorist network
  - The story has attracted worldwide attention.
  - a worldwide shortage of qualified engineers
  - The company employs 28 000 people worldwide.

### [worldwide](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/worldwide) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌwɜːldˈwaɪd/` · **US:** `/ˌwɜːrldˈwaɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/world/worldwide__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/world/worldwide__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an increase in worldwide sales
  - The show has a worldwide audience of 115 million viewers.
  - a worldwide terrorist network
  - The story has attracted worldwide attention.
  - a worldwide shortage of qualified engineers
  - The company employs 28 000 people worldwide.

### [worry](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/worry_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwʌri/` · **US:** `/ˈwɜːri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/worry/worry__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/worry/worry__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The threat of losing their jobs is a constant source of worry to them.
  - A heart attack can be brought on by stress and worry.
  - worry about something Levels of worry about crime had fallen by a third.
  - with worry to be frantic with worry
  - She couldn't sleep with worry.

### [worse](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/worse_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/wɜːs/` · **US:** `/wɜːrs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/worse/worse__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/worse/worse__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Working-class children fared rather worse.
  - worse than somebody/something They are performing much worse than their counterparts at competitor firms.
  - I didn't do it very well, but, if anything, he did it worse than I did.

### [worst](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/worst_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/wɜːst/` · **US:** `/wɜːrst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/worst/worst__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/worst/worst__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He was voted the worst dressed celebrity.
  - Manufacturing industry was worst affected by the fuel shortage.
  - Singapore has been among the countries worst hit by the virus.
  - The report depicts British teenagers as the worst behaved in Europe.
  - Worst of all, I lost the watch my father had given me.

### [worth](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/worth_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/wɜːθ/` · **US:** `/wɜːrθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/worth/worth__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/worth/worth__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Our house is worth about £100 000.
  - How much is this painting worth?
  - to be worth a bomb/packet/fortune (= a lot of money)
  - It isn't worth much.
  - If you answer this question correctly, it's worth five points.
  - This order is potentially worth millions of pounds to the company.

### [written](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/written_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈrɪtn/` · **US:** `/ˈrɪtn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wri/writt/written__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wri/writt/written__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Having a written record of what I've done is very valuable.
  - written instructions
  - The council is inviting written submissions from the public before June 19.

### [wrong](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wrong_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/rɒŋ/` · **US:** `/rɔːŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wro/wrong/wrong__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wro/wrong/wrong__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - My name is spelt wrong.
  - The program won't load. What am I doing wrong?
  - I was trying to apologize but it came out wrong (= what I said sounded wrong).
  - ‘I thought you were going out.’ ‘Well you must have thought wrong, then!’


## Y

### [yard](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/yard) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/jɑːd/` · **US:** `/jɑːrd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/yar/yard_/yard__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/yar/yard_/yard__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They have a gorgeous old oak tree in their front yard.
  - My next-door neighbor was watering her yard.

### [young](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/young_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/jʌŋ/` · **US:** `/jʌŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/you/young/young_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/you/young/young__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's a movie that will appeal to the young.
  - It's a book for young and old alike.

### [youth](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/youth) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/juːθ/` · **US:** `/juːθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/you/youth/youth__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/you/youth/youth__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in somebody's youth He had been a talented musician in his youth.
  - She regrets that she spent her youth travelling and not studying.
  - to relive/recapture your youth
  - He joked that being good at cards was the sign of a misspent youth (= having wasted his time when he was young).
